Its detail names the move: `HEAD moved from A to B` when HEAD moved, then `changed paths:` with the paths the commits since the start changed and the paths that differ from HEAD now, at most 20 and then a count; or `working tree differs from the state it started in` when HEAD did not move and no path can be named. A git error reading the checkout fails the check, with the error as its detail. The check is stored only when it fails. A failed one ends the step `checks_failed` at once, with the return stored, nothing sent back and no judge call. A read-only step with no recorded state, which a step started before this check has, is not compared, and a restart keeps the state a step recorded (`enginehost.stepRun.judge`, `enginehost.stepRun.checkoutMove`, `enginehost.stepRun.endCheckoutMoved`; `enginehost.stepGitCheckout`, `enginehost.stepGitCheckoutCheck`, `enginehost.stepGitCheckoutMove`; `enginehost.Steps.readoptRun`).
  3. For a writing step whose return passes `schema` and whose `status` equals `done_value`, four commit checks run, in this order: `head` (the return's commit is the top-level `commit` string when the return names one, and HEAD must be that commit; when the return names none (missing, null, empty, or non-string), the return's commit is the workspace's HEAD if HEAD has moved past the start commit, and a return naming none while HEAD is the start commit fails `head`), `ancestry` (the start commit is an ancestor of HEAD), `clean` (no tracked change, staged or unstaged, and no untracked file that is not ignored), and `owned` (every path changed from the start commit to the return's commit is owned: equal to an `owned_paths` entry, or below an entry that ends in `/`, which owns every path under that directory; both sides of a rename are checked). A writing step started unowned, which a release step is, runs the first three alone and no `owned` check, since it may change any path (`enginehost.commitChecksWithoutOwned`, `enginehost.stepRun.doneChecks`; `enginehost.StepStart.Unowned`).
  4. `gate`: runs only when all four commit checks pass and the step has a gate.
  5. `assertions`: runs only for an agent gate step, on every return that passes `schema`, whatever its `status`. The return's `results` must hold one entry per assertion of the gate, in the gate's order, each with the assertion's text as `assertion` and its kind as `kind`, and each `fails` result must have a finding whose `citation` is that text. The failure details are `results has N entries for M assertions`, `result I is for assertion "R", want "A"`, `result I of assertion "A" has kind "K", want "J"` and `result I fails assertion "A" and no finding cites it`. Here N is the number of results, M the number of assertions, I the result's place counted from 1, R the result's assertion text, A the assertion's text, K the result's kind and J the assertion's kind. A value that does not decode as a verdict fails with `read verdict: ` and the decoder's error (`enginehost.stepRun.assertionsCheck`, `enginehost.assertionsMismatch`, `enginehost.citesAssertion`, `enginehost.stepRun.valueChecks`).
  6. `return`: runs only for a step with a return check (`StepStart.ReturnCheck`, which only an in-process caller sets), on every return that passes `schema`, whatever its `status`. Its detail is the check's failure lines joined with `; `, and it passes when there are none (`enginehost.stepRun.valueChecks`, `enginehost.stepLoopReturnRecord`; `enginehost.StepStart`, `enginehost.StepStart.ReturnCheck`).

  A read-only step, a return that fails `schema`, or a return whose `status` is not `done_value`, has the `schema` check alone, except that an agent gate step's return that passes `schema` also has `assertions`, and a step with a return check adds `return` to each return that passes `schema`.
- **Gate.** A writing step's gate runs only on a done return whose commit checks all pass. It runs in the step's workspace, with its argv and no shell, and it holds the pool members its needs name. It queues through `orreryd`'s Pools service, runs once its grant holds those members, and keeps the grant until its process group has exited, or until its holder ends when a shutdown detaches the gate (see [Shutdown](#step-lifecycle)). Its limit counts from the grant, not from the queue. It runs with `orreryd`'s environment, minus `TMUX` and `TMUX_PANE`. Each attempt runs under its own holder: `orrery holder --gate-limit` with the session `<step id>-gate`, in the holder directory `holders/<session>` under the gate state directory, which `orreryd` sets to `gates` under the state directory (`cmd/orreryd.startSessions`; `enginehost.stepRun.runDoneGate`, `enginehost.planGate`, `enginehost.gateStateDir`). The holder's child is a gate (see [Holder](#holder)). The attempt's request names that directory, so the grant lasts with the holder as well as with `orreryd` (`enginehost.gateAttempt`; see [Pools](#pools)). Before the gate launches, the row stores the grant, the holder directory and the round in `gate_grant`, `gate_holder` and `gate_round`. The return that judges the gate clears them (`enginehost.stepRun.runDoneGate`; `internal/store/queries.sql: UpdateStepReturn`; see [Steps in the store](#steps-in-the-store)). A hold placed on one of its members (`orrery hold`) stops the gate. Its process group is killed, that run records nothing, and the grant is requeued. The gate runs again from its start once the broker grants it again. A requeued request waits like any other, and a held member is not granted until it is freed (`enginehost.runGrantedGate`, `enginehost.gateWatch`, `enginehost.gateLeftRunning`; `grants.Broker.hold`). The gate passes when it exits 0 without passing its limit. When the limit passes, its holder stops it as a stop does: `SIGTERM` to its process group, then `SIGKILL` once the grace has passed (see [Holder](#holder); `holder.Child.limitAfter`, `holder.Child.Stop`), and the check fails with `gate ran past its limit of <limit> and was killed`. The check's detail opens with the record `gate <name> from <source> ran <argv as a JSON array> holding <POOL:MEMBER,...>; `, then gives `gate exited <status>` or the limit text, followed by `; its scope's memory cap killed <n> of its processes` when the scope's memory cap killed any while the gate ran (see [Exit file](#exit-file); `enginehost.gateResult.MemoryKills`). The gate keeps the newest 1 MiB of each of its stdout and stderr (`enginehost.stepGateLogTail`), redacted (see **Redaction** below). The check keeps the newest 8 KiB of each as its `stdout` and `stderr` tails, from the start of the first whole line in those bytes, or of the first whole character when they hold no line break; the tails are not in the detail (`enginehost.stepGateRecord`, `enginehost.stepLoopGateCheck`, `enginehost.stepCheckTail`, `enginehost.stepGateCheckTail`). A failed gate check also keeps the whole of both kept streams in a log that outlives the gate's holder, whose directory is cleared once the gate's result is read: `logs/<step id>/round-<round>.log` under the gate state directory, which holds `== stdout ==` and the stdout, then `== stderr ==` and the stderr, each ending in a newline when it is not empty. The check's `log` names that file. A log that cannot be written leaves `log` empty and adds `; gate output not kept: <error>` to the detail. A passing gate check keeps no log, and the engine removes no log (`enginehost.stepRun.doneChecks`, `enginehost.stepRun.keepGateLog`, `enginehost.gateLogsName`). A gate that cannot start, or whose grant ends before or while it runs, is an error that ends the step `failed` (`enginehost.gateAttempt`, `enginehost.gateWatch`, `enginehost.gateGrantGone`).
  - **Re-adoption.** A restart re-adopts a gate that the row stores as running in its round (`enginehost.stepRun.runDoneGate`). If the gate's holder still serves, `orreryd` reclaims the grant by its id for a request that names that holder, and waits for the gate's end as an attempt does. Otherwise the holder's exit record is the outcome. An exit or a limit is the result, and nothing reruns. The broker may still keep the gate's grant for its ended holder until its next tick, so `orreryd` reclaims that grant by its id for a request naming the holder and ends it at once: the gate's members are free when the step's end is stored. A stop reruns the gate once on the grant the stop ended. A holder that cannot be attached but has written its record is read the same way. Any other outcome is lost: a reclaim that the broker refuses, a holder that ended with no record, or a record whose reason is neither an exit, a limit nor a stop. A lost gate ends its step `engine_restarted`, and its holder is ended and cleared (`enginehost.readoptGrantedGate`, `enginehost.readoptServed`, `enginehost.readoptEnded`, `enginehost.gateEndKept`, `enginehost.gateRerun`; `enginehost.stepRun.writingChecks`).
- **Judge.** A return that passes its checks and whose `status` equals `done_value` is judged by a Haiku call, except an agent gate step's return, which is never judged, and a return of a step with a return check, which ends the step `done` with no call (`enginehost.stepRun.judge`, `enginehost.stepRun.endDoneReturn`). Without a Haiku caller the step ends `done` with no call. The input is the step's first prompt and the return. A writing step adds the `git diff --stat` from the start commit to the return's commit, and a step with a gate adds the gate check's `stdout` and `stderr` tails, each under its own heading when it is not blank, without the check's record or exit status (`enginehost.stepJudgeInput`). The reply is `met` or `unmet` with a reason. `met` ends the step `done`. The first `unmet` sends the session back once with the reason, and the next return that passes its checks is judged again. A second `unmet` ends the step `goal_unmet`, and its return is kept. A writing step whose next return after the send-back is not `done` also ends `goal_unmet`, with the reason `returned <status> after the judge sent back commit <commit>, which passed every check; that commit is kept`, when HEAD is still the commit the judge saw and the return names no other commit; the commit is held in memory, so a step re-adopted after a restart ends `returned` as before (`enginehost.stepRun.keepJudgedCommit`). A failed diff stat, call or reply stores its error with no verdict and ends the step `done`. The judge's send-back is not one of the three send-backs.

  An agent gate step's return that passes its checks ends by its verdict, with no call. Its reasons are, in order: each result that is not `holds`, as `fails: A` or `undetermined: A` with A the assertion's text; then `status S` when the return's `status` S is not `done_value`; then `an earlier return held fails: A` when the return holds no `fails` result and an earlier return did. The reasons are joined with `; `. The step ends `done` with no reason, and `gate_failed` with them otherwise, and its return is kept. The first `fails` result of any schema-valid agent gate return is kept for the run, whether or not that return's check passed. A turn end an agent gate step takes as no round (held, owner nudge or watchdog nudge) also keeps the first `fails` result of its output when that output passes `schema`, though the output is not stored or checked, so no later return ends `done` (`enginehost.stepRun.assertionsCheck`, `enginehost.stepRun.latchFirstFails`, `enginehost.stepRun.latchNoRound`, `enginehost.stepRun.verdictReasons`, `enginehost.stepRun.endVerdict`, `enginehost.stepRun.turnEnded`, `enginehost.stepRun.judge`).
- **Watch.** An agent gate step also checks its gate's `limit` on each unheld tick, before the backstops and whether or not a turn runs. At the first tick at or past `limit` seconds of wall time from the run's start, or from its latest resume from a usage limit (see Usage limit below), the step ends `overtime` with the reason `agent gate ran past its limit of N seconds`, where N is the limit in seconds. No monitor call is made and no verdict is stored (`enginehost.stepRun.watchGateLimit`, `enginehost.newStepRun`, `enginehost.stepRun.tick`). A held tick is skipped before this check, as it is before the backstops. While a turn runs, the loop checks four backstops on each tick, the first one due in this order: `overtime` (model time reaches the step's model limit), `tool_stuck` (a main-conversation tool call open for 30 minutes of wall time, or 60 after its one extension), `silent` (10 minutes of model time with no event) and `periodic` (5 minutes of turn time since the turn began or the last verdict). Each trip makes one Haiku monitor call. Its verdict is `progressing`, `long_command`, `looping` or `wedged`, with a one-line `doing`, and a non-empty `doing` becomes the row's `doing`. The verdict is applied in this order: `overtime` ends the step `overtime`; `looping` or `wedged` nudges the step once, and then ends it with that verdict; a `long_command` on `tool_stuck` extends that call once, and any other verdict on `tool_stuck` ends the step `tool_stuck`; `silent` nudges once, and then ends the step `silent`; `periodic` does nothing. A nudge interrupts the running turn. The interrupted turn is no round: the step is continued with the nudge text instead of being checked, and an agent gate step keeps the first `fails` result of its output when that output passes `schema`. A nudge does not count toward the three send-backs. A failed monitor call stores its error with no verdict, and the trip acts without one: `overtime` and `tool_stuck` end the step, `silent` nudges on its first trip and ends on the next, and `periodic` does nothing. The monitor's argv, its cap and timeout, and the trip policy with its file and line citations are in [section 15 of the engine protocol](engine-protocol.md#15-the-step-watch-and-judge).
- **Send-back.** A return with a failed check is sent back by `Continue`, as a message that lists each failed check with its detail and, for a check that kept them, its `stdout` and `stderr` tails, each labelled (`enginehost.stepLoopFailures`). A failed `checkout` check is not sent back; it ends the step (see **Checks**). The step may be sent back three times. A fourth failing return ends the step `checks_failed`, with the same failure lines, tails included, as its reason (`enginehost.stepRun.retry`). Nudges and the judge's send-back are counted apart, and they are not among these three.
- **Redaction.** Gate and capture tails are redacted before anything stores, shows, judges or sends them. `internal/redact` replaces each credential of these shapes with `[redacted]` and leaves other bytes as they were: the value of an `Authorization`, `Proxy-Authorization` or `x-api-key` header written as a `Name: value` line, from the value to the end of the line, with the name kept; the value of a JSON-quoted `Authorization`, `Proxy-Authorization` or `x-api-key` member, its name quoted with the quote right after the name, where a quoted value is replaced up to its closing unescaped quote with both quotes kept, and any other value to the end of its line, with the name and its quotes kept; the value of an `aws_secret_access_key` assignment; an `sk-` key of 20 or more characters, `sk-ant-` keys included, unless a letter comes just before the `sk-`; a GitHub token with the prefix `ghp_`, `gho_`, `ghu_`, `ghs_` or `ghr_`, or the prefix `github_pat_`, followed by 20 or more letters, digits or underscores; an AWS access key id, `AKIA` or `ASIA` followed by 16 uppercase letters or digits and a word boundary; a Slack token, `xox`, a lowercase letter, `-`, and 10 or more letters, digits, underscores or hyphens; and a PEM or PGP private key block, replaced whole from its BEGIN line to its END line, or up to the cut at either end of a tail (`redact.Text`). A tail at least its capacity long is taken as cut at its start: a gate run's `Tail`, 8 KiB when it names none (`enginehost.gateTailDefault`), a step gate's 1 MiB (`enginehost.stepGateLogTail`), or a capture's 1 MiB (`enginehost.evidenceCaptureLogTail`). A step gate re-adopted after a restart is redacted as having the 8 KiB capacity, since a holder an older `orreryd` launched keeps that and its exit record does not say which it kept (`enginehost.stepRun.runDoneGate`). The text before its first newline is replaced by `[redacted]`, and a tail with no newline is `[redacted]` alone (`redact.CutTail`). The lost first line is the accepted cost: a credential cut in half at the start cannot be matched, so the whole line goes. The redaction runs where a gate's exit record becomes its result, on both tails (`enginehost.gateExitResult`, `enginehost.gateRedactTail`), and on every read of a capture's stdout or stderr log, which applies the cut form to a stored log of at least 1 MiB (`enginehost.Evidence.readLog`). Nothing else is redacted by the engine: not kept artifacts, transcripts, owner prompts or push text. `serve` redacts the epic text it reads, as [Epics and Steps](#epics-and-steps) gives it, and that redaction is on the read alone, so the stored text stays as the engine wrote it. A log stored before this release is redacted when it is read, but the judge reads the stored file, so that log reaches the judge as stored (`enginehost.judgePrompt`).
- **Ends.** Each step ends once, with one of these end kinds:

  | End | Set when | `status` |
  |---|---|---|
  | `done` | a return passes its checks, its `status` equals `done_value`, and the judge says `met`, fails to give a verdict, or is not run (always, for a step with a return check); or, for an agent gate step, a return passes its checks with no verdict reason (see Checks) | `done_value` |
  | `returned` | a return passes its checks and its `status` is another value, except an agent gate step's, which ends `gate_failed` | that value |
  | `checks_failed` | a fourth return fails a check, or a read-only step's return finds its checkout moved | empty |
  | `failed` | the process exits on its own (`the step's process exited` after a clean exit, otherwise the exit's reason), the first prompt cannot be sent, a return or verdict cannot be recorded, a gate cannot run or its grant ends before or while it runs, a `Continue` cannot be sent, a respawn whose agent no longer resolves, or a started step whose init agents list lacks its agent (see Launch and Restart) | empty |
  | `stopped` | `StopStep` ends the step | empty |
  | `engine_restarted` | `orreryd` starts with the step's row open and no loop for it, and the step is not re-adopted (see [Restart](#step-lifecycle)); or a re-adopted gate's outcome is lost (see [Gate](#step-lifecycle)); or the step runs an agent gate that a restart does not re-adopt (see [Restart](#step-lifecycle)) | empty |
  | `overtime` | the watch's `overtime` trip: model time reaches the step's model limit; or, for an agent gate step, wall time from the run's start, or from its latest resume from a usage limit, reaches its gate's `limit` (see Watch) | empty |
  | `tool_stuck` | the watch's `tool_stuck` trip, when its verdict is neither `long_command` nor `looping` or `wedged`, or its call has had its extension | empty |
  | `silent` | the watch's `silent` trip with a verdict other than `looping` or `wedged`, once the step has been nudged | empty |
  | `looping` | a `looping` verdict, once the step has been nudged | empty |
  | `wedged` | a `wedged` verdict, once the step has been nudged | empty |
  | `goal_unmet` | the judge's second `unmet` verdict, or a writing step's return that is not `done` after the judge sent back a commit that passed every check (see Judge); the return is kept | empty |
  | `gate_failed` | an agent gate step's return passes its checks and a verdict reason applies (see Judge): a result is `fails` or `undetermined`, the `status` is not `done_value`, or an earlier return held a `fails` result; the return is kept | the return's `status` |

  The end is stored with the reason, and the step's session is stopped and its worktree removed, whatever ended it.
- **Stage.** The loop is in one stage at a time: `turn`, `checks`, `gate`, `judge`, `held` or `limited`. The row stores the stage and the unix seconds it started, in `loop_stage` and `loop_stage_from` (see [Steps in the store](#steps-in-the-store)). A new step starts in `turn` at its start time (`store.StepAdd.applyEngine`). A stage the loop is already in keeps its start, so the start moves only on a change (`enginehost.stepRun.setStage`). The loop writes each stage at these points:
  - `turn` is stored with each prompt that continues the session, in the one write that stores the prompt: a send-back, a judge send-back, a nudge, or an owner's text to a held step. That write also holds the loop state the prompt changes, the cleared nudges and a held step's resume, so a restart before it finds the nudge still pending and the step still held, and the turn the prompt was to follow is not judged as a round (`enginehost.stepRun.continueStep`, `enginehost.StepPrompt`, `enginehost.Manager.Continue`, `enginehost.stepRun.turnEnded`). A usage-limit resume stores it in the write that clears the limit, after its `Continue` is sent (`enginehost.stepRun.resumeWith`).
  - `checks` is stored before a return's checks run (`enginehost.stepRun.enterChecks`). The return that judges a gate stores it too, since the gate's end sets it (`enginehost.stepRun.doneChecks`, `enginehost.stepRun.commitReturn`).
  - `gate` is stored in the write that records the gate's grant, so a gate that waits in the queue leaves the stage at `checks`, with its start unchanged (`enginehost.stepRun.runDoneGate`, `enginehost.gateAttempt`).
  - `judge` is stored before the judge is called (`enginehost.stepRun.enterJudge`).
  - `held` is queued when an owner hold lands. The queue is a `Put` that does not wait for the store, so a failed put is logged and the next change stores the state again (`enginehost.stepRun.hold`, `enginehost.stepRun.saveLoop`).
  - `limited` is stored in a limited turn's write, before the step's process is released (`enginehost.stepRun.limitTurn`).

  A restart rebuilds the run with both columns, so each stage and its start are kept (`enginehost.stepRun.resume`, `enginehost.Steps.readoptRun`). A row added before migration `00032` has the stage `""` and the start `0` until the loop next stores a stage.
- **Stop.** `StopStep` cancels the step's loop, which kills a running gate, waits for the loop to return, and then stores `stopped` through the same end path. A step that has already ended is left as it is, and `StopStep` returns success. A held step ends `stopped` the same way (`enginehost.Steps.Stop`). Under any holder scope the cancel kills the gate: its holder is stopped, its process group is killed and its directory is cleared (`enginehost.gateWatch`, `enginehost.gateLeftRunning`, `enginehost.gateEnd`).
- **Hold.** `HoldStep` interrupts the step's running turn and marks the step held (`enginehost.stepRun.hold`, `enginehost.Steps.Hold`). It is refused with `failed_precondition` when the step is already held, when no turn is running, and when the step is running its gate (`enginehost.stepRun.hold`, `enginehost.stepRun.do`). A failed interrupt is returned as `internal`, and the step stays unheld. A landed hold drops any nudge pending for the turn, the watchdog's and the owner's (`enginehost.stepRun.hold`). A held step's loop skips each tick, so the watch checks nothing (`enginehost.stepRun.run`). A held step's turn end is no round, and it continues nothing, though an agent gate step keeps the first `fails` result of its output when that output passes `schema` (`enginehost.stepRun.turnEnded`).
- **Owner nudge.** `NudgeStep` sends the owner's text to the step (`enginehost.Steps.Nudge`). Empty text is `invalid_argument`, and it is refused before the step is looked up (`enginehost.Steps.Nudge`). It is refused with `failed_precondition` when the step is running its gate, and when the step is not held and no turn is running (`enginehost.stepRun.nudgeOwner`, `enginehost.stepRun.do`). On a held step, the text resumes the step at once when the held turn has already ended. Otherwise it waits for that turn's end, which is no round, though an agent gate step keeps the first `fails` result of its output when that output passes `schema`, and is continued then (`enginehost.stepRun.nudgeOwner`, `enginehost.stepRun.turnEnded`). On a running turn, the turn is interrupted, and its end is continued with any pending watchdog text followed by the owner's text, in place of a round, though an agent gate step keeps the first `fails` result of its output when that output passes `schema` (`enginehost.stepRun.nudgeOwner`, `enginehost.stepRun.turnEnded`). Owner text sent while a nudge is pending is joined to it, one line per text (`enginehost.stepRun.nudgeOwner`, `enginehost.joinNudges`). A nudge neither spends nor replaces the watchdog's nudge, and an OK reply means the nudge landed (`enginehost.Steps.Nudge`).
- **Usage limit.** A turn that ends on a usage limit limits its step (`enginehost.stepRun.limitTurn`).
  - Cause. The turn is limited when the store holds a rejected rate limit for the step that was stored at or after the step's newest prompt (`store.Store.StepRateLimit`; `internal/store/queries.sql: GetStepRateLimit`), or when the session handed the run an assistant message whose error is `rate_limit` since the previous turn end (`enginehost.stepRun.Event`, `enginehost.stepRun.limitTurn`; `session.assistantEvents`). The stored rate limit is the session's latest `rejected` rate limit event (`enginehost.recorder.ops`; see [Planner turns](#planner-turns), The kept rate limit).
  - No round. A limited turn is no round. The step is not checked, judged or sent back for it, and it counts toward none of the send-back, nudge or judge counts. The run drops its pending nudges and its hold (`enginehost.stepRun.turnEnded`, `enginehost.stepRun.limitTurn`).
  - Release. The run stores its loop state with the limit (`enginehost.stepRun.limitTurn`, `enginehost.stepRun.loopState`), then releases the step's process (`enginehost.stepRun.limitTurn`; `enginehost.Manager.Release`). Release stops the process and records nothing, and the observer is told of no exit (`enginehost.Manager.Release`; `enginehost.Manager.recordLine`, `enginehost.Manager.noteEvent`). The step's slot is freed when the process's pump ends (`enginehost.Manager.pump`).
  - Spends nothing. While the step is limited, its loop skips each tick, so the watch makes no trip, monitor call or nudge (`enginehost.stepRun.tick`). Its exits are ignored (`enginehost.stepRun.exited`), and its session receives nothing until a resume (`enginehost.stepRun.resumeLimited`, `enginehost.stepRun.autoResumeLimited`, `enginehost.stepRun.resumeWith`).
  - Open. The step stays open and limited through exits and restarts, and nothing ends it but a resume or a stop. A restart rebuilds the step's run from its row, restores it as limited, and does not handle the row's stored turn end again (`enginehost.stepRun.resume`; `enginehost.Steps.readoptRun`). `Steps.Reconcile` starts the loop of a limited row with no live process (`enginehost.Steps.Reconcile`). The step's holder has ended, so the restart clears its holder directory (`enginehost.Manager.reconcileHolders`, `enginehost.Manager.readoptHolder`).
  - Reset. A limit with a known reset stores the step's due row in the same write that records the limit. The row's id is the lowercase hex SHA-256 of `orrery-due`, the kind `step`, the step id and the reset in unix seconds, joined with NUL bytes, so one reset has one row. A limit with no known reset stores none, so the step waits for a command (`store.StepLoop.applyEngine`; `store.dueNamespace`, `store.dueKindStep`, `store.insertDueResume`, `store.dueID`). `StepEnd` closes the step's rows, so a limit that arrives after the end stores none (`store.StepEnd.applyEngine`). The due pass runs at start and then every `limitTick`, 5 seconds (`cmd/orreryd.brokerTick`, `cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startLimits`, `cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startIdle`, `cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startDue`). A `due` row is resumed once the clock is at least 60 seconds past its reset, oldest reset first (`enginehost.dueGrace`, `enginehost.DuePass.Pass`). The row resumes its step through `Steps.AutoResume` with that reset (`enginehost.DuePass.resumeStep`; `enginehost.Steps.AutoResume`). A refusal by the step cap or the step's gate leaves the row `due` for the next pass. A step that is not limited at that reset, has ended, or has no row closes the row with no resume (`enginehost.DuePass.resumeStep`). A resume closes its step's rows in the write that clears the limit (see Resume below). The reset is reported on the step (`enginehost.stepMessage`), grouped and worded in the limit notice (`enginehost.LimitNotices.Run`, `enginehost.LimitNotices.update`), and worded in the planner's turn reason (`planner.Runner.settle`).
  - Resume. `Steps.Resume` runs in the step's loop (`enginehost.Steps.Resume`; `enginehost.stepRun.resumeLimited`). `resumeLimited` sends the owner's prompt "The usage limit has reset. Carry on with your task from where you stopped." with no PromptID (`enginehost.stepResumePrompt`, `enginehost.stepRun.resumeLimited`). The automatic resume sends the same text under the PromptID that `resumePromptID` derives from the step id and the reset (`enginehost.resumeIDNamespace`, `enginehost.resumePromptID`, `enginehost.stepRun.autoResumeLimited`). `Continue` returns nil without sending when an engine prompt of the step already carries that PromptID, so an automatic resume that was sent before a restart is not sent again (`enginehost.Manager.Continue`). Continue respawns the released process with `--resume` (`enginehost.Manager.Continue`, `enginehost.Manager.respawnStep`). Then the step's limit and its stored rate limit are cleared in one write, which also closes the step's due rows (`enginehost.stepRun.resumeWith`; `store.EngineRateLimitCleared.applyEngine`; `store.StepDueResumesClosed`, `store.StepDueResumesClosed.applyEngine`). An agent gate step's gate `limit` counts again from the resume, which that write stores in `loop_gate_from`, so the wait for the reset never ends the step `overtime`, and an evidence judge's resume spends no judge (`enginehost.stepRun.resumeWith`; `enginehost.stepRun.watchGateLimit`). A restart restores it from the row (`enginehost.stepRun.resume`).
  - Refusals. `ErrStepNotLimited` refuses a step that no limit holds (`enginehost.ErrStepNotLimited`, `enginehost.stepRun.resumeLimited`). `ErrStepNotRunning` refuses a step with no loop or one that has ended (`enginehost.Steps.runOf`). A Continue that the step cap refuses returns an error matching `ErrCapReached` and leaves the step limited (`enginehost.stepRun.resumeWith`). Any other Continue error ends the step `failed` and is returned (`enginehost.stepRun.resumeWith`). The Connect codes are those of [Steps](#steps) (`enginehost.stepCommandError`).
  - Stop. `StopStep` ends a limited step `stopped`, and finish stops no session for it, since its process was released (`enginehost.Steps.Stop`; `enginehost.stepRun.finish`).
  - Planner. A planner turn's limited step is stopped by its runner, as [Planner turns](#planner-turns) gives (`planner.Runner.await`).
  - Agent gate. An agent gate step is rebuilt at a restart only when the Steps' `ReadoptGate` rebuilds its gate, as Restart below gives. The evidence judge's `evidence_judge` gate is rebuilt from its run's stored item. A limited agent gate step that is rebuilt stays limited, and `Steps.Reconcile` starts its loop as it starts any limited row's. Any other agent gate step ends `engine_restarted` there (`enginehost.Steps.readoptRun`, `enginehost.Steps.readoptAgentGate`, `enginehost.Steps.Reconcile`; `enginehost.EvidenceReadoptGate`).
  - Limit notice. `LimitNotices` checks the limited step rows every `limitTick`, which is 5 seconds (`cmd/orreryd.limitTick`, `cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startLimits`). It groups them by key, `<kind>@<reset>`, where `<reset>` is the reset in unix seconds, or `0` when the reset is unknown (`enginehost.limitGroups`, `enginehost.limitKeyOf`). Each group names the epics its steps are held under: the contract title of each epic, sorted and without repeats, the label of an epic that no stored epic has, or `a step outside any epic` for a step with no epic. A group is told as one notice when its names differ from its told mark, and the mark is then stored with those names. The marks are read before the first check, so a limit told before a restart is not told again unless its names change. A limit whose reset changes is a new group, so it is told again (`enginehost.LimitNotices.check`, `enginehost.LimitNotices.readMarks`, `enginehost.LimitNotices.update`; `store.Store.TellLimit`). A limit with no mark, open at start, is told as any untold limit is (`enginehost.LimitNotices.update`). A marked limit with no open group is seen at the check's time, and its mark is forgotten, so a seen write clears its key (`enginehost.LimitNotices.update`; `route.Router.Changed`). The marks are described in [Told limits in the store](#told-limits-in-the-store).
  - The notice's desk body is `Held by the KIND limit, reset RESET: NAMES`, where `RESET` is RFC 3339 in UTC or `unknown`, and `NAMES` are the group's names joined by `, ` (`enginehost.limitBody`). It names no step. Its desk state is `limited` and its title is `usage limit`. Its push has project `usage limit`, state `limited` and the same body as its summary (`enginehost.limitNotice`).
  - The notice is never watched and never judged, so it is routed by rules 3 to 7 of [Notice routing](#notice-routing) (`route.Router.finish`, `route.Router.decide`, `route.Router`). It has a stored row with the `limit` source, so a restart restores it and a shutdown keeps it (`enginehost.RestoreLimitNotice`; `route.Router.rowLocked`; `route.Router.Wait`). Its phone push and its clear carry the `limit` key (`push.clearPayload`, `push.boundedPayload`; see [Push payload](#push-payload)). A kept push for its key is stored in `pushed_limit_notices` and deleted by its clear (`notice.Notices.putRow`, `notice.Notices.deleteRow`; see [Pushed notices in the store](#pushed-notices-in-the-store)).
- **Shutdown.** `Steps.Close` cancels each loop and waits until none runs. Without a gate scope, the cancel kills a running gate, as Stop does. With one, which `orreryd` sets from `--holder-scope` (`cmd/orreryd.startSessions`), each loop is cancelled with `errGateDetach`. Its gate keeps running under its holder, and the step's row stays open for the next start's Restart. The grant stays with the gate, and the broker keeps it across a restart while its holder lives (see [Pools](#pools)) (`enginehost.Steps.cleanRestartedStep`, `enginehost.Steps.Close`, `enginehost.errGateDetach`, `enginehost.gateWatch`).
- **Restart.** At start, `orreryd` calls `Steps.Readopt` before the manager's Reconcile, and `Steps.Reconcile` after it (`cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.reconcile`). `Readopt` reads each open step row, and the turn end its session last stored after its last prompt, before any holder is re-attached. It rebuilds each row's run as `Start` builds one, restored from the row: the round, the continue count, the pending nudges, whether the step is held, whether the judge has sent it back, and whether its one nudge is spent. It restores the gate that the row stores as running, with its grant, holder and round. Each run is registered with the manager as the step's observer, so the manager re-adopts the step's holder when that holder is live and tracked. A row whose run cannot be rebuilt is logged and left unregistered (`enginehost.earliestEnded`, `enginehost.Steps.Readopt`, `enginehost.Steps.readoptRun`). A step that runs an agent gate is re-adopted only when the Steps' `ReadoptGate` rebuilds its gate: the agent gate of the row's gate name, with an agent (`enginehost.StepsConfig.GateScope`; `enginehost.stepRowRunsAgentGate`, `enginehost.Steps.readoptAgentGate`). `orreryd` sets `ReadoptGate` to the evidence readopt, which rebuilds an evidence judge step's `evidence_judge` gate from its run's stored item (`cmd/orreryd.startSessions`; `enginehost.EvidenceReadoptGate`; see [Evidence](#evidence)). A row whose `ReadoptGate` is unset, returns no gate or returns a gate of another name is left unregistered and ends `engine_restarted`, as is a row whose stored turn end is a return, since resume would take that return up as an ordinary step's and its verdict would never be read (`enginehost.Steps.readoptRun`, `enginehost.stepRowRunsAgentGate`, `enginehost.Steps.readoptAgentGate`). The first `fails` result the gate keeps restarts empty (`enginehost.Steps.readoptRun`). The manager settles each row it does not re-adopt as it settles other engine rows, with no notice for a step (see [Reconcile at start](#lifecycle-rules)). Then `Steps.Reconcile` starts the loop of each registered step that the manager reports live, and of each registered step whose row is limited, an agent gate step among them (`enginehost.Steps.Reconcile`). Each other open row with no loop ends `engine_restarted`. A step that a restart ends this way is told nothing, with or without an epic, as Kind and silence above gives (`enginehost.Steps.endRestarted`). The ends are queued together and stored in one store transaction. A dropped end, whose step row is gone, does not stop the rest: they are stored, and each stored row has its worktree removed and its gate holder ended and cleared. The dropped row is not cleaned up, and the first dropped end is returned (`enginehost.Steps.readoptAgentGate`, `enginehost.Steps.Reconcile`, `enginehost.Steps.endRestarted`; `enginehost.Writer.flush`). A re-adopted loop resumes from what it stored. A stored turn end that the latest return covers resumes that return: failed checks are sent back again, a return with another status ends `returned`, and a done return is judged again. A stored turn end that the latest return does not cover, stored while the session's background flag is set, ended while a background task was listed, so it is taken up only when its return's `status` equals `done_value`, as Returns above gives. Any other such turn end is left unread, and the step waits for the turn claude starts once the task ends, with no turn running meanwhile, as a live step does (`store.Store.StepTurn`, `store.StepTurnEnd`). Any other stored turn end is handled as one the session has just given. With no stored turn end, the watch counts the turn as running (`enginehost.stepRun.tick`, `enginehost.stepRun.resume`, `enginehost.stepRun.resumeReturn`, `enginehost.stepWatch.restore`). `Readopt` also reads the newest 40 finished blocks the step's session stored, and the rebuilt watch keeps them as the recent lines the monitor is shown, each aged from when it was stored, so a monitor call after a restart is never made with an empty window. Notices are not stored, so none is kept (`store.Store.StepBlocks`, `enginehost.stepWatch.seed`, `enginehost.Steps.readoptRun`). The loop stores its round, continue count, pending nudges, held flag, judge flag and nudged flag as each changes, and a change that continues the session in the write of the prompt that continues it (`enginehost.stepRun.continueStep`). `Continue`, which the loop sends, respawns a step that is not live from its rows: it first waits for a drained holder of the step to end, with its directory removed (see [Reconcile at start](#lifecycle-rules)), then takes a step slot, the slot a drained step kept for its id with no cap check or else one under the step cap, and only then resumes the row's claude session with the same agent and schema flags. A respawn runs the `agent` rule of `StartStep` before claude starts. An agent that no longer resolves ends the step `failed` before claude starts, with a reason that names the agent and each place checked, and no claude process starts (`enginehost.Manager.Continue`, `enginehost.Manager.respawnStep`).


#### Epics

`orreryd` serves the `orrery.engine.v1.Epics` service (`proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto`) on the session socket beside `Steps`, when it runs with `--sessions` (`enginehost.Serve`; `cmd/orreryd.serve`). Its epics are stored in orreryd's store (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). With no store open, the service has no store behind it, and every call answers `unavailable` (`cmd/orreryd.startSessions`; `enginehost.epicService.ready`). `serve` calls its `ListEpics` and `GetEpic` to merge the epics with the records, and `Epics.History` calls `GetEpic` with `history` set (see [Epics and Steps](#epics-and-steps); `server.EpicStoreClient`). `serve`'s `PauseEpic`, `ResumeEpic`, `StopEpic`, `RetryEpic` and `AnswerEpic` calls go to the client that `server.Config.EpicCommands` names, and it calls no other epic method (`server.epicsServer.forwardCommand`; `cmd/orrery.runServe`).

| Call | Request | Response | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CreateEpic` | `contract`, `decisions`, `workflow` | `epic` | checks the contract, pins the workflow it names at the base (see [Workflows](#workflows)), stores it accepted with its seed decisions and its `created` trigger, and returns the stored epic |
| `GetEpic` | `id`, `history` | `epic` | returns the stored epic with that id, with its summary, its planner state, its pinned workflow and its workflow node runs; with `history`, also its triggers and tells (see **GetEpic and ListEpics** below) |
| `ListEpics` | none | `epics` | returns a summary of each stored epic, ordered by `id` |
| `AnswerEpic` | `epic_id`, `id`, `text`, `question`, `choice` | `decision`, `warning` | records the answer as a decision on the epic: tied to the question `question` names, with the choice `choice` gives it, or a note when `question` is empty (see **AnswerEpic** below) |
| `PauseEpic` | `id` | `epic` | pauses the epic, so the planner starts no turn for it (see **Controls** below) |
| `ResumeEpic` | `id` | `epic`, `resumed`, `still_limited` | clears the pause of the epic, resumes the epic's limited task steps, and lists the steps resumed and the steps a full step cap left limited; a park stays |
| `StopEpic` | `id` | `epic` | pauses the epic, flags its running turn to stop, and stops that turn's step; with no running turn, an epic that holds its repository's release slot frees the slot and stops its open release step instead (see **Controls** below and [Release](#release)) |
| `RetryEpic` | `id`, `node` | `epic` | ends the park of the epic, answers its open park question `retry`, and grants each parked loop of the epic one more round; on a release its integrator stopped, also sends the release back to planning with the integrator's report (see **Replan.** under [Release](#release)); with `node`, reruns that workflow node under its next attempt, its parked runs or its latest integrate run when that run ended `failed`, which also ends the park and answers the open park question `retry` (see **Controls**) |
| `KickEpic` | `id`, `text` | `epic` | records `text` as the owner's decision on the epic and adds the pending trigger `owner-kick:<seq>` that carries it, so the epic's next plan run claims it; refused for a paused, parked or ended epic (see **KickEpic** below) |
| `FollowEpics` | none | `follows` | returns, for each question label across all epics, how many answered questions had a recommendation and how many were answered with it (see **FollowEpics** below) |
| `ReleaseEpic` | `id`, `undo_none` | `epic` | releases the epic: resumes its stopped release at its first unfinished step, running again each undo-none step that `undo_none` names; an epic that ended with gaps is released on the owner's word, with no undo-none step (see [Release](#release)) |
| `CloseEpic` | `id`, `kind`, `by` | `epic` | closes the epic as `superseded`, `shipped` or `abandoned`, which ends it: its running turn and node runs end, its open questions are answered, and its release slot is freed; a planner close also adds the tell that routes its ending (see **CloseEpic** below) |
| `AmendEvidence` | `id`, `requirement`, `item`, `reason` | `amendment`, `epic` | stores the owner's amendment of the evidence item of the Requirement `requirement` names, checking: the item replaces the Requirement's own only once a red run of it at the epic's base is judged not to hold there, and a red that holds refuses it (see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments)) |
| `SeeEpic` | `id` | none | stores the time of the call as the epic's seen mark, replacing the earlier mark (see **SeeEpic** below) |
| `ArchiveRow` | `kind`, `id` | none | stores the time of the call as the archive mark of the list row of `kind` and `id`, replacing the mark an earlier call stored (see **ArchiveRow** below) |
| `UnarchiveRow` | `kind`, `id` | none | stores the time of the call as the unarchive time of that row, which clears its archive mark (see **ArchiveRow** below) |

The calls are declared in `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: Epics`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: Evidence`, and implemented in `enginehost.epicService`.

**Callers.** `orrery mcp` and `orrery epic` send each Epics call with the session they run in, as request headers (`stepmcp.CallerInterceptor`, `cmd/orrery.runMCP`, `cmd/orrery.runEpic`; see [Step and epic tools](engine-protocol.md#14-step-and-epic-tools)). A session that orrery started, which carries `ORRERY_SESSION` in its environment, sends its value as `protocol.HeaderSession`. Otherwise a session in tmux sends its `TMUX` and `TMUX_PANE` as `protocol.HeaderTmux` and `protocol.HeaderTmuxPane`, when both are set. Any other call carries no caller header (`stepmcp.stampCaller`; `protocol.EnvSession`).

`orreryd` records the session of an Epics call on the session socket. `cmd/orreryd.serveOpts` gives the handlers of that socket `enginehost.EpicCallInterceptor` when a store is open. The interceptor records a unary call only when its request names an epic, the call returns no error, and its request carries a caller header that resolves to a sessions row. `CreateEpic` names the id of its contract, `AnswerEpic` names its `epic_id`, and `GetEpic`, `PauseEpic`, `ResumeEpic`, `StopEpic`, `RetryEpic`, `ReleaseEpic`, `CloseEpic` and `AmendEvidence` name their `id` (`enginehost.epicNamedBy`). `ListEpics` and `FollowEpics` name no epic, so they record nothing. The call's name is `epic_` and the lowercased verb of its method, the method less its `Epic` or `Evidence` noun, so `CreateEpic` records `epic_create`, `CloseEpic` records `epic_close`, and `AmendEvidence` records `epic_amend` (`enginehost.epicCallName`).

An `Orrery-Session` header resolves to the sessions row with that id, of any kind. A pane pair resolves to the pane row whose `server` is `-S` followed by the socket, which is the part of `TMUX` before its first comma, and whose `pane` is `TMUX_PANE` (`enginehost.epicCallerOf`, `store.resolveEpicCaller`; `internal/store/queries.sql: GetPaneSession`). A session header that names no row records nothing, and the pane headers are not tried in its place. The write itself is `store.Store.RecordEpicCall`, which stores the call in the `epic_sessions` table (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). A record the store refuses as busy is tried again 1 second later, until it lands or the call's context ends, before the call answers, since an `epic_create` record is the only mark of the epic's creator (`enginehost.recordEpicCall`). Any other record error is logged under the epic and the call's name, and the call's answer is returned unchanged (`enginehost.EpicCallInterceptor`).

**Contract.** `CreateEpic` checks the contract before it stores anything. The checks run in the order below, and the first one that fails is the refusal (`enginehost.checkEpic`; `enginehost.epicService.CreateEpic`). A refusal is `invalid_argument` with the message `<field>: <reason>`. The field is named by its proto name, and a row of a section by its zero-based index, as in `release_steps[0].undo: empty` (`enginehost.epicFieldError`, `enginehost.epicFieldError.Error`, `enginehost.epicRowField`). The checks, in order:

1. `contract: missing`, when the request has no contract (`enginehost.checkEpic`).
2. `id: bad form, want <repo-id>-<7 hex>/<slug>`, when the id does not match `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*-[0-9a-f]{7}/[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`. `id: over 128 bytes`, when it matches and is longer than 128 bytes (`enginehost.epicIDMaxBytes`, `enginehost.epicIDPattern`, `enginehost.checkEpicHead`).
3. `repository: not absolute`, when the path is not absolute. `repository: not inside a git work tree`, when `git rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree`, run in that directory, fails or does not print `true`. Any failure of the git call is this refusal (`enginehost.checkEpicRepository`; `enginehost.stepGit`).
4. `intent: empty`, when the intent is empty after trimming white space (`enginehost.checkEpicHead`).
5. `care: not limited, serious or severe`, unless the care is `limited`, `serious` or `severe` (`enginehost.checkEpicHead`).
6. `base: empty`, when the base is blank after trimming white space. `base: not a commit in the repository`, when `git rev-parse --verify --quiet --end-of-options <base>^{commit}`, run in the repository, fails. `CreateEpic` then stores the full commit that name resolves to (`enginehost.checkEpicBase`; `enginehost.epicService.CreateEpic`).
7. For each gate in order: `gates[i]: <reason>`, when `gates.Define` refuses the gate, with the reason it gives, under the same rules as a gates file's table (see [Gate files](#gate-files)). `gates[i].name: duplicate`, when an earlier gate has the name (`enginehost.checkEpicGates`; `gates.Define`).
8. For each done gate in order: `done_gates[i]: <reason>`, when the name resolves neither from the contract's gates nor from the repository's project file, or `done_gates[i]: not a command gate` when it resolves to an agent gate. A done gate's name resolves as [Gate files](#gate-files) gives. Then `review_gates: refused, the reviewers fill the review list`, when the contract names any review gate. The review list is filled from the reviewers, as [Reviewers](#reviewers) gives (`enginehost.checkEpicGateRefs`; `gates.ResolveDefined`).
9. For each failure in order: `failures[i].id: unknown id`, unless the id is `F` followed by a number with no leading zero, and `failures[i].id: duplicate` when an earlier failure has the id. Then `failures[i].rating: not limited, serious or severe`, unless the rating is one of the three levels (`enginehost.epicFailureIDPattern`, `enginehost.checkEpicFailures`, `enginehost.checkEpicRowID`).
10. `requirements: none`, when the contract has no requirements. For each requirement in order: `requirements[i].id` is refused as `failures[i].id` is, with `R` in place of `F` (`enginehost.epicRequirementIDPattern`, `enginehost.checkEpicRequirements`, `enginehost.checkEpicRowID`). Then `requirements[i].item: missing`, when the requirement names no evidence item. Otherwise the first line that `evidence.Check` gives for the item, named as `requirements[i].item.` and that line, such as `requirements[i].item.judge: empty or blank`. The kind and tests lines are `kind: none, name test, capture or supplied`, `kind: "<kind>" is not test, capture or supplied`, `kind: test without a capture`, `kind: capture without a capture`, `kind: supplied without supplied files`, `tests: none, a test item lists its test files`, `tests: set on a capture item` and `tests: set on a supplied item`, each named under the item as the other lines are (`evidence.kindLines`, `evidence.testsLines`). The form of an item is in [Evidence](#evidence) (`enginehost.checkEpicRequirementItem`; `evidence.Check`). A new epic's requirement that also names the legacy `evidence` or `kind` is refused with `requirements[i].evidence: set beside an item` or `requirements[i].kind: set beside an item` (`enginehost.checkEpicRequirementItem`).
11. For each premise in order: `premises[i].id` is refused as a failure's id is, with `P` in place of `F` (`enginehost.epicPremiseIDPattern`, `enginehost.checkEpicPremises`).
12. For each addition in order: `additions[i].location: unknown id`, when the location is an `F`, `R` or `P` id (`^[FPR][1-9][0-9]*$`) that names no failure, requirement or premise of the contract (`enginehost.epicRowIDPattern`, `enginehost.checkEpicAdditions`).
13. For each release step in order: `release_steps[i].undo: empty`, when it is empty after trimming (`enginehost.checkEpicReleaseSteps`).
14. For each seed decision in order: `decisions[i].id: empty`, or `decisions[i].id: duplicate` when an earlier decision has the id. Then `decisions[i].text: empty`, when the text is the empty string. The text is not trimmed (`enginehost.checkEpicDecisions`).
15. For each gate's argv in order, then each requirement's capture in order, each element `gates[i].argv[j]` or `requirements[i].item.capture[j]`: `<field>: holds white space: split the element into argv elements, or pass the line to a shell after -c`, when the element holds white space and the element before it is not exactly `-c`. A clustered flag before it, such as `-lc`, does not exempt it, and the reason is `<field>: holds white space after -lc, and only -c exempts it: write the flags apart, as -l -c`, with the flags of that cluster. Then `<field>: holds a quote the shell cannot close: split the element into argv elements, or pass the line to a shell after -c`, when the element's single quotes, double quotes or backslashes leave a quote unclosed, whether or not it holds white space. The splitter reads single quotes, double quotes with backslash escapes, and a backslash outside quotes, and expands nothing (`enginehost.checkContractLint`; `enginehost.checkArgvElements`; `enginehost.argvSpaceReason`; `enginehost.isClusteredFlag`; `enginehost.apartFlags`; `enginehost.splitShellWords`).
16. For each requirement in order, once every capture of entry 15 has passed: `requirements[i].item.capture: -run pattern "<pattern>" matches <Test> in <dir> at the base: name a new top-level test`, when a `go test` the capture runs has a `-run` pattern whose top-level element compiles and matches a top-level `Test` function present at the base, in the packages the `go test` names. A `go test` is a word whose base name is `go` followed by the word `test`, in the capture's argv, or in a line after `-c`, where the words `&&`, `||`, `;` and `|` end that line's invocation. The pattern is the value of its `-run X`, `-run=X`, `--run X` or `--run=X`, and the last one given counts. The packages are the words that are not flags, read after the `go test` and skipping each `go test` or `go build` flag that takes a separate value together with its value (`-count`, `-timeout`, `-tags`, `-p`, `-parallel`, `-cpu`, `-bench`, `-benchtime`, `-skip`, `-shuffle`, `-coverprofile`, `-covermode`, `-coverpkg`, `-o`, `-exec`, `-fuzz`, `-fuzztime`, `-list`, `-C`, `-ldflags`, `-gcflags`, `-mod`, `-modfile`, `-outputdir`, `-cpuprofile`, `-memprofile`, `-blockprofile`, `-mutexprofile` and `-trace`). Reading stops at `-args`. `./dir` and `./dir/` name that directory only, `./dir/...` that directory and every directory below it, `./...` the whole repository, and with no package word, the repository root only. The top-level element is the first part of the pattern, split on `/` outside brackets and parentheses with `\/` kept, and it matches unanchored as `regexp.MatchString` does. An empty element matches every test. A test is a `func TestName(` line in a `*_test.go` file of those packages at the base, where `TestName` is `Test` followed by nothing or by a character that is not a lowercase letter, and `TestMain` is not a test. `<dir>` is the package directory of the file that declares the test, as `./pkg`, or `./` for the repository root. Accepted, with no refusal here: a pattern or a package word that holds a backtick or a `$` followed by a letter, `_`, `{` or `(`, where a trailing `$` stays literal; a package word that neither starts with `./` nor is `.`; a pattern whose top-level element does not compile; and a capture with no `-run`. A `git grep` at the base that fails for any reason other than finding no line is not accepted: its error is returned, and `CreateEpic` refuses with `invalid_argument` and that error's text (`enginehost.checkContractLint`; `enginehost.goTestArgs`; `enginehost.goTestArgsIn`; `enginehost.isShellOperator`; `enginehost.parseGoTestArgs`; `enginehost.takesSeparateValue`; `enginehost.topLevelElement`; `enginehost.topLevelMatcher`; `enginehost.goTestScopes`; `enginehost.goTestScopeOf`; `enginehost.goTestScope.pathspec`; `enginehost.globEscape`; `enginehost.goTestFuncsAtBase`; `enginehost.checkGoTestArgs`; `enginehost.displayDir`; `enginehost.checkArgvElements`).
17. For each release step in order, once every check of entries 15 and 16 has passed: `release_steps[i].step: <reason>`, for the first step whose text the rules below refuse. Within a step the rules run in order, and the first one that matches gives the reason. (a) `merge`: the text holds the whole word `merge` in any case, `(?i)\bmerge\b`. `merged`, `merges` and `merging` pass, and `do not merge` is refused. The reason is `says merge: a release step fast-forwards main to the release candidate; write fast-forward`. (b) `fixed version`: the text holds `\bv[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+`, with a lowercase `v`, so `v0.86.3` and `rel/v0.86.2` are both refused. The reason is `names the fixed version <v>: a release step's tag is chosen when the step runs; name the next version above the newest tag instead`, where `<v>` is the first version the text names. (c) `CI`: every occurrence of `do not wait for CI` is removed in any case, and the rest of the text then holds the whole word `CI` in capitals, `\bCI\b`. So `so CI runs` is refused, and `circle` or a lowercase `ci` in a path is not. The reason is `a release does not wait for CI; write "do not wait for CI"` (`enginehost.checkContractLint`; `enginehost.checkReleaseSteps`; `enginehost.releaseStepReason`; `enginehost.releaseMergePattern`; `enginehost.releaseVersionPattern`; `enginehost.releaseWaitPattern`; `enginehost.releaseCIPattern`).

**Reviewers.** After the checks, `CreateEpic` merges the reviewers of three layers, orrery's defaults, the repository's project file and the contract's `reviewers`, and generates the conformance assertions from the contract's `exclusions` (see [Reviewers](#reviewers); `enginehost.epicReviewers`). A reviewer that no layer can hold is refused as `invalid_argument`, and so is an enabled reviewer the layers leave incomplete. The message is `read the reviewers of <repo>: <file>: reviewers[<index>] <name>: <key>: <reason>` when a project file's entry or syntax is refused as it is read, and `merge the reviewers of <repo>: <source>: reviewers[<index>] <name>: <key>: <reason>` otherwise. `<source>` is the project file, or `epic_create` for the contract's own entries, and `<index>` is the entry's position in its layer. The reasons are those of [Reviewers](#reviewers) (`enginehost.epicService.CreateEpic`; `reviewers.Error`). Nothing is stored then.

**Workflow.** After the checks, the reviewers and the base, `CreateEpic` pins the workflow that `workflow` names, or `epic` when it names none (`enginehost.epicService.CreateEpic`). The workflow is loaded as the base commit holds it. One that does not load there is refused as `invalid_argument` with the message `workflow: at base <commit>: <refusal>`, where `<commit>` is the full commit the base names and `<refusal>` is the refusal of [Workflows](#workflows). Nothing is stored then (`enginehost.pinWorkflow`).

A contract that passes is stored in one write, with its sections, its seed decisions, its pinned workflow and its `created` trigger (`store.Store.CreateEpic`). The trigger has the id `created` and the cause `epic created`, and it is `pending` (`store.Store.CreateEpic`, `store.createdTriggerID`, `store.createdTriggerCause`). Its `phase` is `accepted`, and its `created` and `changed` are both the time of the call. The response is the stored epic read back (`enginehost.epicService.CreateEpic`). An `id` that is already stored answers `already_exists`, and nothing is stored (`enginehost.epicService.CreateEpic`; `store.Store.CreateEpic`).

**Summary.** `CreateEpic` stores the epic's `summary` empty, and orreryd's summary loop writes it once. The loop runs when `orreryd` starts with its store open, and then at each `limitTick`, which is 5 seconds (`cmd/orreryd.startSessions`, `cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startSummaries`, `cmd/orreryd.limitTick`, `enginehost.EpicSummaries.Run`). Each pass reads the epics whose summary is empty, in id order, and calls Haiku for each one that this start has not tried. A start tries each epic once, and the calls run one at a time (`enginehost.EpicSummaries.pass`, `enginehost.EpicSummaries.summarize`). A call runs the model `protocol.DefaultSummaryModel` as one structured `claude -p` turn, with `--json-schema` set to `enginehost.epicSummarySchema`, which requires a `summary` string. Its input is the instruction and the contract's text, as `enginehost.epicContractText` renders it (`enginehost.haikuArgv`, `enginehost.Haiku.call`, `enginehost.epicSummaryOf`). The reply's summary is trimmed. A call that fails, a reply that does not decode and a blank summary are each logged, and they leave the summary empty until a later start tries the epic again (`enginehost.EpicSummaries.summarize`, `enginehost.epicSummaryOf`). The write runs under a context that ignores the loop's cancellation, so a reply the call returned is stored as the loop stops (`enginehost.EpicSummaries.summarize`). A write the store refuses, a busy store among the causes, is logged and its summary kept, and each later pass writes the kept summaries again before it reads, until they land, with no second call (`enginehost.unstoredWrites`). The session title and epic report loops keep a refused write the same way. The store keeps the first summary only, as [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store) gives (`store.Store.SetEpicSummary`).

**Report.** Each ending of an epic that ends `released`, `done` or `ended_with_gaps` gets one report, once a call for it returns a why. `orreryd`'s report loop runs when it starts with its store open, and then at each `limitTick`, 5 seconds, as the summary loop does (`cmd/orreryd.startSessions`, `cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startReports`, `cmd/orreryd.limitTick`, `enginehost.EpicReports.Run`). Each pass reads the epics whose phase is one of those and whose stored report is for another phase, in id order (`store.Store.UnreportedEpics`, `internal/store/queries.sql: ListUnreportedEpics`). It calls Haiku for each such ending that this start has not tried, one call at a time, so a start tries each ending once (`enginehost.EpicReports.pass`, `enginehost.EpicReports.report`). The call's input is an instruction, then the epic's outcome, its Requirements shipped and not shipped with their texts, and the gaps `kinds.Gaps` gives (`enginehost.epicReportText`, `enginehost.epicReportInstruction`). A Requirement is shipped when `enginehost.requirementState` reads it `green` from the epic's current evidence runs, which is a passed green run of its item, and it is not shipped otherwise. Both lists are in contract order (`enginehost.requirementSplit`, `store.EpicFlow`). The why comes from `enginehost.Haiku.Call` under `enginehost.epicReportSchema`, which requires a `why` string, and it runs the model `protocol.DefaultSummaryModel`. The why is trimmed, and a blank one is an error (`enginehost.epicWhyOf`). A failed read, call or reply is logged and leaves the epic with no report until a later start tries it again (`enginehost.EpicReports.report`).

`store.Store.SetEpicReport` stores the outcome, the two id lists and the why. It changes nothing unless the epic's phase is still the phase the report was made for and its stored report is for another phase, and it does not move `changed` (`internal/store/queries.sql: SetEpicReport`). It runs under a context that ignores cancellation, so a reply the call returned is stored as the loop stops (`enginehost.EpicReports.report`).

**Epics stored before O3d2.** An epic stored before O3d2 stays readable. Its requirements keep their `evidence` and `kind` and carry no `item`, so `GetEpic` answers them with no item, and an evidence run of one of them is refused (`enginehost.requirementItemMessage`; `enginehost.evidenceRunRequirementItem`). Its tasks have no gate, and the migration gives each one the evidence `{"conclusion": <its old check>, "judge": ""}` (`internal/store/migrations/00020_evidence.sql: +goose Up`). Such a task has no judge, so no judge step starts for it. Nothing in O3d2 builds such an epic, and the build of O3d3 does not either, since it lists only epics with a base (`internal/store/queries.sql: ListBuildEpics`).

**Phase and changed.** The create sets `phase` to `accepted`. Later writes set it, and no other write does: `reviewing` when the end-of-epic review freezes (`store.Store.FreezeReview`), `correcting` when the review ends (`store.Store.EndReview`), `built` when a plan that holds an `integrate` node ends built while the phase is `accepted` (`store.Store.EndPlanBuilt`, `store.endAndBuild`), `clean` or `ended_with_gaps` when the conclude node ends the epic (`store.Store.EndEpic`, through `interpret.Ending`), `clean` when `ReleaseEpic` releases an epic that ended with gaps (`store.releaseEndedWithGaps`), `releasing` when the epic takes the release slot from `clean` and when `ReleaseEpic` resumes it (`store.Store.TakeReleaseSlot`, `store.Store.ReleaseEpic`), `stopped` when a release stop ends it (`store.Store.StopRelease`), `correcting` when a replan sends the release back to planning and `clean` when the release resumes after it (`store.Store.ReplanRelease`, `store.Store.ResumeReplannedRelease`; see **Replan.** under [Release](#release)), `released` or `done` when the release node ends the epic (`store.Store.EndEpic`, through `interpret.Ending`; see [Release](#release)), and `met_at_base` when the owner answers `end` to a **Met at base** park question (see **Met at base** under [Workflows](#workflows); `store.answerMetAtBase`). `CloseEpic` sets the closed phase its kind names (see **CloseEpic** above; `store.closeEpicRows`). Every one of them but the five release writes named below uses `store.setEpicPhase` (`internal/store/queries.sql: SetEpicPhase`). Each of these writes also sets `phase_at`, the unix seconds the phase was set. `store.setEpicPhase` keeps the time the epic already has when the write sets the phase it has (`internal/store/queries.sql: SetEpicPhase`). `CreateEpic` sets `phase_at` to `created` (`store.Store.CreateEpic`, `internal/store/queries.sql: InsertEpic`). The release writes that set the phase in their own query set it to the time they are given: the take of the slot, the resume, the stop, the replan and the resume after a replan (`store.Store.TakeReleaseSlot`, `store.Store.ReleaseEpic`, `store.Store.StopRelease`, `store.Store.ReplanRelease`, `store.Store.ResumeReplannedRelease`; `internal/store/queries.sql: StartEpicRelease`, `internal/store/queries.sql: ResumeEpicRelease`, `internal/store/queries.sql: StopEpicRelease`, `internal/store/queries.sql: ReplanEpicRelease`, `internal/store/queries.sql: CleanReplannedEpic`). An epic stored before `00042_endings.sql` reads `phase_at` as `0` until a write changes its phase (`internal/store/migrations/00042_endings.sql: ALTER TABLE epics ADD COLUMN phase_at`). The ended phases are `met_at_base`, `clean`, `ended_with_gaps`, `released`, `done` and `stopped`, and the closed phases `closed_superseded`, `closed_shipped` and `closed_abandoned`, and every other phase is not ended (`kinds.Ended`, `store.IsClosedPhase`). A task's build runs while its epic has a base and its phase is not ended (`kinds.Build.Decide`). `changed` is the time of the epic's last write: its creation, or its latest answer or planner action (`internal/store/queries.sql: UpdateEpicChanged`; `store.appendDecision`). The planner's writes and the controls do not move `changed`, except that a planner action carried out or refused appends a decision, which moves it as an answer does (`store.takeAction`, `store.refuseAction`, `store.appendDecision`). They change the planner tables and the epic's `paused`, `parked` and `park_class` columns (`store.Store.FreeTurn`, `store.setParked`; `store.Store.PauseEpic`, `store.Store.ResumeEpic`, `store.Store.ResumeDueEpic`, `store.Store.CloseDueResume`, `store.Store.StopEpic`, `store.Store.RetryEpic`, `store.Store.RetryNode`). The interpreter's writes change the node runs, their checks and their triggers, the integrate writes also change the epic's bases and each task's batch, the review writes change the review's rows and the phase, and the release writes change the release runs, the slot and the phase (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). A park of any class sets `parked` and `park_class`, as **Park classes** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns) gives. None of them moves `changed` either, and no release write does, except a `ReleaseEpic` that appends an owner decision, which names undo-none steps or releases an epic that ended with gaps: each owner decision it appends moves `changed`, as any answer does (`store.recordNamedReleases`, `store.releaseEndedWithGaps`, `store.appendDecision`) (see [Node runs in the store](#node-runs-in-the-store) and [Node checks in the store](#node-checks-in-the-store)).

**GetEpic and ListEpics.** `GetEpic` answers an unknown id with `not_found` (`enginehost.epicService.GetEpic`). The stored epic it returns also carries its planner state: `paused`, `parked` and `park_class`, then `turns`, `tasks` and `questions`, each in order, where `park_class` is the class of the park, empty when the epic is not parked (`proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.park_class`), and its `branch`, the state of the epic's branch: `''`, `creating` or `created` (`enginehost.storedEpicMessage`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic`). It carries `amendments`, the evidence amendments of its Requirements in seq order, each with its amender, the item it found and the item it asks for, its reason, its state, its runs and its refusal (see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments); `enginehost.amendmentMessage`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicAmendment`). It also carries its pinned workflow, as `workflow`: the workflow's `name`, its canonical `text` and the `sha256` of that text. The pinned workflow's node rows are not carried (`enginehost.storedEpicMessage`, `enginehost.workflowMessage`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicWorkflow`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.workflow`). It also carries `reviewers`, the epic's merged reviewers in the order their names first appear, as [Reviewers](#reviewers) describes them. Its `contract` has no `reviewers` and no `review_gates`, since `enginehost.contractMessage` leaves both empty (`proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.reviewers`; `enginehost.storedEpicMessage`, `enginehost.epicReviewerMessage`). A turn has `seq`, `step`, `state`, `reason`, `outline`, `outcome`, `decisions`, `started` and `ended`. A task has the id `T<seq>` and has `turn`, `title`, `deliverable`, `files`, `requirements`, `contributes`, `test_changes`, `gate`, `evidence` and `status`. Its `test_changes` are the test files the planner named for it, each also one of its `files`, as the plan check requires (`enginehost.taskMessage`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicTask.test_changes`). Its `check` is empty since O3d2, and the evidence conclusion carries the check. Its build is carried as well: `stage`, `attempt`, `step`, `base`, `revision`, `failure`, `guard`, `review`, `held`, the `red` and `green` evidence runs its build names (`null` until it names one), and `reviews`. Each review has `attempt`, `gate`, `step`, `verdict` and `findings`, and each finding has `id`, `claim`, `location`, `step`, `result`, `reason` and `unverified`, which is true when the finding was settled as confirmed without a verifier; a finding's citation, evidence and verify-by are not carried. `guard` is the build's last guard refusal, the text that **The guard** under [Task builds](#task-builds) gives, and it is empty while the guard has refused nothing for the task (`enginehost.taskMessage`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicTask.guard`). `attempt` and `turn` are 64-bit integers, which protojson writes as strings. The fields are described in [Task builds](#task-builds) (`enginehost.taskMessage`, `enginehost.taskReviewMessage`, `enginehost.taskFindingMessage`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicTask`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicTaskReview`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicTaskFinding`; `enginehost.evidenceServiceRunMessage`). A requirement carries its `item`, or none for a requirement stored before O3d2, and its `owner`, `contributors`, `state` and `disagreement`. `owner` is the `T<seq>` of the lowest-seq task not failed that claims it, empty when none does. `contributors` are the `T<seq>` of each task not failed that contributes to it, in seq order. `state` is `green` when a green run of its current item passed, `red` when a red run of it passed and no green one did, and `pending` otherwise, where a run passes as `evidence.Passed` gives (`enginehost.requirementClaims`, `enginehost.requirementOwnership`, `enginehost.requirementState`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicRequirement`). `disagreement` is the line `kinds.Disagreement` gives, the judge and exit disagreement at the epic's base. It is set for a test item when at least one of its reproductions in the integrate and sync runs that ended passed at the stored base ran, and none of those that ran timed out or exited non-zero. It is empty otherwise (`kinds.Disagreement`, `enginehost.requirementClaims`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicRequirement.disagreement`). It also carries `nodes`, the epic's workflow node runs, in the order of the pinned workflow's nodes, then by path, with a run of a node the workflow does not hold after the others. Each run has its `path`, `node`, `kind`, `state`, `outcome`, `round`, `loop_round`, `attempt`, `output` and `revision`, and the rows its kind holds: a plan's `turns` and `tasks` above its mark, a build's `tasks`, an evidence node's `runs` for its attempt, each the run that decides its Requirement: the first run, or the rerun an environment exit started (`kinds.EvidenceRunID`), a gate's or command's `check` and an agent's `step` id. A check holds its `state`, `exit`, `timed_out`, `not_run`, `stdout` and `stderr`, and is unset until it is stored. The stored epic that `CreateEpic`, `GetEpic`, `PauseEpic`, `ResumeEpic`, `StopEpic` and `RetryEpic` return carries the same nodes, since each reads them in one read with the epic (`enginehost.epicService.storedEpic`, `enginehost.nodeMessages`, `enginehost.epicNodeMessage`, `enginehost.storedCheckOf`, `enginehost.nodeCheckMessage`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.nodes`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicNode`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicNodeCheck`). It also carries the end-of-epic review: `frozen`, the revision the review froze, `clean`, the revision the epic ended clean at, and `ledger`, an `EpicLedger` with the reviewers' `verdicts`, the ledger's `findings` and the correction `rounds`. Each verdict has `position`, `reviewer`, `step`, `verdict` (`holds`, `fails` or `gap`) and `reason`. Each finding has `id`, `position`, `reviewer`, `finding`, `claim`, `citation`, `location`, `evidence`, `verify_by`, `step`, `result`, `unverified` and `reason`, and its `closures`, each with `round`, `step`, `result` and `reason`. Each round has `round`, `revision`, `tasks` and `outcome`. A ledger finding carries its citation, evidence and verify-by, which a task's finding does not. The three fields are empty before the review starts, and `position`, `round` and each task seq are 64-bit integers, which protojson writes as strings (`enginehost.storedEpicMessage`, `enginehost.reviewLedgerMessage`, `enginehost.reviewVerdictMessage`, `enginehost.reviewFindingMessage`, `enginehost.reviewClosureMessage`, `enginehost.reviewRoundMessage`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.ledger`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.frozen`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.clean`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicLedger`). It also carries `releases`, the epic's release runs in the order the store reads them, by attempt, then part, then position, and `release_slot`, the holder of its repository's release slot, empty when the slot is free (see [Release](#release); `enginehost.epicService.storedEpic`, `enginehost.releaseRunMessage`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.releases`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.release_slot`). A question has the id `Q<seq>` and has `turn`, `text` and `state`, which is `open`, `answered` or `replaced`. It has its `label`, its `choices`, each with `id` and `does`, and its `recommendation`, with `choice`, `why` and `confidence`, which is unset when the question recommends none. Its `replaces` and `replaced_by` are the `Q<n>` of the question it replaces and of the question that replaced it, empty when there is none. Its `choice` is the choice that answered it, `other` for words, and is empty while the question is open or replaced (`enginehost.questionMessage`, `enginehost.choiceMessage`, `enginehost.recommendationMessage`, `enginehost.questionRef`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicQuestion`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicQuestionChoice`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicRecommendation`; `store.loadPlanSections`). With `history` set, `GetEpic` also carries `triggers`, oldest first by creation and then id, and `tells`, in seq order. Without it, both are empty (`enginehost.epicService.GetEpic`, `enginehost.epicService.fillHistory`, `store.Store.EpicHistory`, `enginehost.triggerMessage`, `enginehost.tellMessage`; `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: GetEpicRequest.history`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.triggers`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.tells`). It carries the epic's `summary`, which is empty until the summary loop writes it (see **Summary** above; `enginehost.storedEpicMessage`). It carries `phase_at`, the unix seconds its phase was last set (see **Phase and changed** above), its `release_proof`, each release step with its state (see **Release proof** under [Release](#release)), and its `report`, which is unset until a report is stored (see **Report** above). The stored epic carries `phase_at` and `report` through `enginehost.storedEpicMessage`, and `release_proof` through `enginehost.epicService.storedEpic` (`proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.phase_at`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.release_proof`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic.report`). `ListEpics` answers each stored epic as an `EpicSummary`: `id`, `repository`, `title`, `phase`, `phase_at`, `created`, `changed` and `seen`, with no sections and no workflow. The list is ordered by `id` (`enginehost.epicService.ListEpics`, `enginehost.summaryMessage`; `internal/store/queries.sql: ListEpics`).

**AnswerEpic.** Before the store is asked, `AnswerEpic` refuses an empty `epic_id` or `id` with `invalid_argument`, naming the first one that is empty (`enginehost.checkEpicAnswer`, `enginehost.epicService.AnswerEpic`). Then it checks the shape of the answer, in the order below. Each refusal is `invalid_argument`, except the last:

- A note, which is an answer with no `question`, with a `choice`: `choice: a note answers no question`. A note whose text is empty after trimming white space: `text: empty`.
- An answer to a question with no `choice`: `choice: empty`.
