- A `question` that is not `Q` followed by a positive decimal number: `not_found` with `question: no question <question> on epic <epic_id>`, before the store is asked (`enginehost.epicService.AnswerEpic`, `enginehost.questionNotFound`, `enginehost.questionSeq`).

`text` is required for a note and for the choice `other`, and optional for a declared choice (`enginehost.checkEpicAnswer`).

The store then records the answer in one write. A note is `AnswerEpic` (`store.Store.AnswerEpic`). An answer that names a question is `AnswerQuestion` (`store.Store.AnswerQuestion`):

- An `epic_id` that names no epic answers `not_found` (`enginehost.answerError`; `store.requireEpic`).
- An `id` already recorded on the epic with other text, or with equal text and another choice, answers `already_exists`. Text is compared exactly, and a note's choice is empty (`enginehost.answerError`; `store.replayAnswer`).
- An `id` already recorded with equal text and the same choice returns the stored decision, and writes nothing. This check runs first, so a repeat that names a question its first submission closed still returns the stored decision (`store.Store.AnswerQuestion`, `store.replayAnswer`).
- A question the epic does not hold answers `not_found`. A question that a later question replaced answers `failed_precondition` with `question: Qn was replaced by Qm`, and an answered question answers `failed_precondition` with `question: <question> is closed`. Each writes nothing (`enginehost.answerError`, `enginehost.questionNotFound`; `store.requireAnswerable`).
- A `choice` the question does not offer answers `invalid_argument` with `choice: <question> offers no choice <choice>`. A question offers each of its declared choices and `other`, which every question offers. `other` with text that is blank after trimming white space answers `invalid_argument` with `text: empty`. Both write nothing (`enginehost.answerError`; `store.requireAnswerable`, `store.offers`).
- Otherwise an answer that names a question is written as one unit: the question becomes `answered` with the `choice`, a pending trigger with the id `answer:<id>` and the cause `answer <id> to Q<n>` is inserted, and the decision is appended with its `question` set to that question's seq. A declared choice keeps its text as given, which may be empty. An answer to a park question that is not final ends the park whatever its choice, and an answer to a final park leaves the park as it is (see **Park questions**). An answer to a **Met at base** question also carries out its choice in the same write, as **Met at base** under [Workflows](#workflows) gives (`store.recordQuestionAnswer`, `store.endParkByAnswer`, `store.answerMetAtBase`).
- Otherwise a note is appended with `question` `0`. A note closes nothing and adds no trigger.
- Either way the decision is appended with the next `seq`, counted from 1, dated at the call, and the epic's `changed` moves to that time (`store.appendDecision`).

The response carries the decision, whose `question` is `Q<n>` or empty for a note. While questions are open, a note also gets a `warning` that names them in seq order: `recorded as a note: it answers no question, and Q1, Q2 are open`. A repeat of a note gets the warning too, when questions are open at the repeat (`enginehost.epicService.answerNote`, `enginehost.noteWarning`).

No call updates or deletes a decision. The store has no query that does, and the table's triggers abort any update or delete (`internal/store/queries.sql: InsertEpicDecision`, `internal/store/queries.sql: GetEpicDecisionByClientID`, `internal/store/queries.sql: MaxEpicDecisionSeq`, `internal/store/queries.sql: ListEpicDecisions`; `internal/store/migrations/00014_epics.sql: CREATE TRIGGER epic_decisions_no_update`, `internal/store/migrations/00014_epics.sql: CREATE TRIGGER epic_decisions_no_delete`).

**FollowEpics.** `FollowEpics` takes no fields and answers one `EpicFollow` for each question label, in label order, across all epics: its `label`, its `answered` count and its `followed` count. A label appears when one of its answered questions had a recommendation. `answered` counts those questions, and `followed` counts the ones whose `choice` is the recommended choice. Notes, open and replaced questions, and questions with no label or no recommendation are never counted (`enginehost.epicService.FollowEpics`, `enginehost.followMessage`, `store.Store.Follows`; `internal/store/queries.sql: ListEpicQuestionFollows`). Park questions count under the label `park`. A non-final park answered with `retry`, which `RetryEpic` and `RetryNode` answer, is followed, and one answered with other is answered and not followed. A final park recommends nothing, so it is never counted (see **Park questions**). The planner's prompt shows these counts as Advice followed (see [Planner turns](#planner-turns)).

**KickEpic.** `KickEpic` takes `id` and `text`, and refuses an empty `id` and a blank `text` before the store is asked (`enginehost.epicService.KickEpic`). It makes one store write (`store.Store.KickEpic`): the owner's text is appended as the next decision, with the id `owner-kick:<seq>`, dated at the call, and the call moves the epic's `changed`; then a pending trigger with the same id and the cause `owner kick: <text>` is added. An unknown epic is `not_found`. An epic in an ended phase (`met_at_base`, `clean`, `ended_with_gaps`, `released`, `done` or `stopped`), a paused epic and a parked epic are each `failed_precondition` and write nothing, with the texts `epic <id> is <phase>, an ended phase, and takes no kick`, `epic <id> is paused; resume it before kicking it`, and `epic <id> is parked; orrery epic retry <id> ends the park before a kick`. The call starts no turn. The planner claims the trigger at the epic's next pass during a plan run, and its turn prompt shows the trigger as `- owner-kick:<seq>: owner kick: <text>` (`planner.triggerLines`). A kick while no plan run is planning waits for the epic's next plan run (`interpret.Runner.Pass`, `planner.Runner.PassOnly`). The call returns the stored epic read back.

**SeeEpic.** `SeeEpic` takes `id` and stores the time of the call as the epic's seen mark, replacing the mark an earlier call stored. It writes that mark alone, so it moves neither `changed` nor `phase_at` (`enginehost.epicService.SeeEpic`, `store.Store.SeeEpic`; `internal/store/queries.sql: UpsertEpicSeen`). An empty `id` is `invalid_argument` with `id: empty`, and an unknown epic is `not_found` with `id: no epic <id>` (`enginehost.epicService.SeeEpic`, `enginehost.epicControlError`). No `orrery mcp` tool or `orrery epic` form calls `SeeEpic`. The web page and the phone reach it through `serve`'s `Epics.Seen` (see [Epics and Steps](#epics-and-steps); `server.epicsServer.Seen`).

**ArchiveRow.** `ArchiveRow` takes `kind` and `id` and stores the time of the call as the archive mark of the list row that `kind` and `id` name, replacing the mark an earlier call stored. `kind` is `epic`, `session` or `headless`. `UnarchiveRow` takes the same two fields and stores the time of the call as the row's unarchive time, which clears its archive mark. Archiving only hides a row from the lists, and it touches no epic, record, session or pane, with one exception: an `ArchiveRow` of kind `headless` also deletes that session's cron jobs, in the same write, so an archived headless session keeps none (see [Cron jobs](#cron-jobs); `store.Store.ArchiveRow`). `UnarchiveRow` deletes nothing. Neither call checks that the row exists in any table, so an epic that has only a record is archived by its `id` (`enginehost.epicService.ArchiveRow`, `enginehost.epicService.UnarchiveRow`, `store.Store.ArchiveRow`, `store.Store.UnarchiveRow`; see **Epics in the store** for `archived_rows`). A `kind` other than `epic`, `session` or `headless` is `invalid_argument` with `kind: "<kind>" is not epic, session or headless`, and an empty `id` is `invalid_argument` with `id: empty`, for both calls (`enginehost.rowError`, `store.checkRowKey`). No `orrery mcp` tool or `orrery epic` form calls `ArchiveRow` or `UnarchiveRow`. `serve`'s `Epics.Archive` and `Epics.Unarchive` reach them (see [Epics and Steps](#epics-and-steps); `server.epicsServer.Archive`, `server.epicsServer.Unarchive`).

**Controls.** `PauseEpic`, `StopEpic` and `RetryEpic` each take `id`, return the stored epic read back, and make one store write. `ResumeEpic` takes `id` and returns the stored epic read back. It makes one store write, except for an epic that is not paused and holds a limited task step, which it resumes with no store write (see **ResumeEpic** below). An empty `id` is `invalid_argument` with `id: empty`. An unknown epic is `not_found` with `id: no epic <id>`. A store refusal is `failed_precondition` with its text, and nothing is written (`enginehost.epicService.PauseEpic`, `enginehost.epicControlError`; `store.Store.PauseEpic`, `store.Store.ResumeEpic`, `store.Store.ResumeDueEpic`, `store.Store.CloseDueResume`, `store.Store.StopEpic`). The refusals are these:

- `PauseEpic` of an epic the owner paused, or paused with no recorded source: `epic <id> is already paused`. An epic a usage limit paused is accepted (see the effects below).
- `ResumeEpic` of an epic that is not paused and holds no limited task step: `epic <id> is parked, not paused; retry ends a park` when it is parked, or `epic <id> is not paused` otherwise.
- `StopEpic` of an epic with no running turn that holds no release slot: `epic <id> has no running turn`.
- `RetryEpic` of an epic in phase `stopped`, which a release stop sets, unless the release integrator stopped it, as the effects below give: `epic <id> is stopped; retry ends a stopped release only when its release integrator stopped it`. Only `ReleaseEpic`, or a planner's `resume_release` action, resumes such a stopped release (see **Release** under [Release](#release) and **Actions** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns); `store.retryEpicIn`, `store.retryStoppedRelease`).
- `RetryEpic` of an epic that is not parked: `epic <id> is not parked`.
- `RetryEpic` of an epic in phase `met_at_base`, which the owner's `end` answer to a **Met at base** question under [Workflows](#workflows) sets, and `RetryNode` of such an epic naming its parked node: `epic <id> is parked for good: <its park reason>`. `RetryNode` naming a node with nothing to rerun is refused as the next refusal says first. An epic parked by a **Met at base** question that is not ended is not refused: `RetryEpic` and `RetryNode` rerun its parked evidence run, as `retry` does (see **Met at base**).
- `RetryEpic` with a `node` that has nothing to rerun, a loop id included: `node <node> of epic <id> is not parked`. A node has nothing to rerun when it has no parked run and its latest integrate run did not end `failed`. The latest integrate run is the one in the newest settle walk of the plan, except that a run which ended `passed` with no revision moved nothing, an empty batch, so it does not count. A running or passed latest run, and an earlier failed run that a later run of the node superseded, are refused the same way (`store.retryFailedIntegrate`, `store.latestIntegrateRun`).
- `RetryNode` naming the release node: `node <node> of epic <id> is the release; resume it with orrery epic release <id>`. Only `ReleaseEpic` resumes a stopped release, since it moves the phase back to `releasing`; a plain retry would rerun the node while the phase stays `stopped` (`store.Store.RetryNode`; see [Release](#release)).

The effects are these (`store.Store.PauseEpic`, `store.Store.ResumeEpic`, `store.Store.ResumeDueEpic`, `store.Store.CloseDueResume`, `store.Store.StopEpic`, `store.pauseAsOwner`, `store.resumeEpic`, `store.closeEpicDueResumes`; `internal/store/queries.sql: PauseEpic`, `internal/store/queries.sql: SetEpicPauseSource`, `internal/store/queries.sql: ResumeEpic`):

- `PauseEpic` sets `paused` and records the owner as the pause source. It closes the epic's due rows in the same write, so no reset that is still due resumes the epic (`store.Store.PauseEpic`, `store.pauseAsOwner`; `internal/store/queries.sql: PauseEpic`, `internal/store/queries.sql: SetEpicPauseSource`). On an epic a usage limit paused, it takes over the pause as the owner's, so the epic stays paused past the reset (`store.Store.PauseEpic`). A running turn finishes, and the planner starts no turn for the epic.
- `ResumeEpic` clears `paused` and the pause source when the epic is paused, closes the epic's due rows, and resumes its limited task steps. It does not clear a park. The steps are described below (`store.Store.ResumeEpic`, `store.resumeEpic`; `internal/store/queries.sql: ResumeEpic`).
- `StopEpic` sets `paused` as the owner's pause, closes the epic's due rows, and flags the running turn's `stop`, in one write, and returns the turn's step, which is empty while the turn has none. The service then stops that step when it has one (`enginehost.epicService.StopEpic`). A step that has already ended is left as it is, so a result it stored stays. A failure of that stop is `internal` after the pause and the flag are stored. The turn ends as [Planner turns](#planner-turns) gives. An epic with no running turn that holds its repository's release slot is not refused in that case: one write frees the slot and returns the id of its open release step, if one runs, and the service stops that step as above. The epic keeps its phase and is not paused (`store.Store.StopEpic`, `store.stopReleaseSlot`; see [Release](#release)).
- `RetryEpic` clears `parked` and stores the epic's highest turn seq in `retried`, so the failed turns up to that seq stop counting toward a park. Its triggers stay pending. Each open park question of the epic is answered `retry` (`store.closeParkQuestions`). Each parked loop of the epic also turns running with one more granted round, so its next round runs, in the same write (`store.Store.RetryEpic`; `store.grantParkedLoops`). A loop has its `max` rounds plus the rounds granted to it (see **Running**). A parked node run is not rerun by this, so it stays parked until `RetryNode` reruns it, except the parked evidence run that a **Met at base** question names: `RetryEpic` reruns that run at its next attempt, as `retry` does, before it answers the question (`store.rerunMetAtBaseRuns`).
- `RetryEpic` of an epic in phase `stopped` whose release integrator stopped it, whose parked release node run's attempt holds a round of the release integrator that ended `returned`, its blocked return, sends the release back to planning on the owner's word, whatever the side of the round's decisions (see **Replan.** under [Release](#release)). In one write it stores a replan release run of that round, with the round's start and report, as the integrator's own replan stores one; turns the release node run running at the same attempt, its outcome and output cleared; answers each open park question `retry`; and ends the park as above. The epic stays `stopped` and keeps whatever release slot it holds until the release's next decision sends it back to planning (`store.retryStoppedRelease`, `store.recordReplan`; `internal/store/queries.sql: UnparkEpicNodeRun`). A planner's `retry_epic` action does the same, since it runs `RetryEpic`. This is how a release stopped by its integrator, such as one stopped before the integrator named a side, is moved on: `orrery epic retry ID`.
- `RetryEpic` with `node` reruns each parked run of that workflow node id under its next attempt, through `RetryNode`, and ends the park in the same write. The run's outcome, output, revision, refs, refs-checked flag, result and end are cleared, so its work is prepared and started again. A node with no parked run whose latest integrate run ended `failed` reruns that run instead, with its outcome, output, refs-checked flag and end cleared and its revision, refs and result kept, as the integrate node's **Retry** under [Workflows](#workflows) gives. The run is the latest failed integrate run whose candidate never reached the branch, so a later integrate run that ended `passed` with no revision does not hide it (`store.retryFailedIntegrate`, `store.latestIntegrateRun`). The epic's `retried` is set as above, but no loop is granted a round, and a loop is not a node a retry names. Each open park question of the epic is answered `retry` in the same write, as it is without `node` (`store.closeParkQuestions`). Its attempt counts up by one from `0`, where a run starts (`store.Store.RetryNode`, `store.Store.RetryEpic`, `enginehost.epicService.retryPark`; `internal/store/queries.sql: RetryEpicNode`).
- `ResumeDueEpic`, which the due pass calls for an epic's row, resumes the epic only when the row is `due` and the pause is a usage limit's, as `ResumeEpic` does. It closes the row in every case (`store.Store.ResumeDueEpic`).
- `StopTask`, which a build's stopped step calls, stores the task's build and pauses the epic as the owner's in one write, closing the epic's due rows as `StopEpic` does. A task that is not open, or whose stored build is not from, pauses nothing (`store.Store.StopTask`; `store.pauseAsOwner`). No reset resumes an epic that a build stop paused.

**ResumeEpic.** `ResumeEpic` reads the epic, then its limited task steps: the open steps that a usage limit holds and that carry a task label, oldest first. A planner turn's step carries no label, so it is not one of them. A build step's label is `T<seq>`, an evidence judge step's is `evidence-<run id>`, and a review or closure step's is the path of its node run (`enginehost.epicService.limitedTaskSteps`; `interpret.Runner.startReviewUnits`; `build.Runner.implementerStart`, `build.Runner.reviewStart`, `build.Runner.verifierStart`; `enginehost.judgeStart`). The store's `ResumeEpic` runs when the epic is paused or holds no such step. When the epic is paused, it clears `paused` and its pause source, and closes its due rows. When the epic is not paused and holds no such step, the store refuses the call with the message above. An epic that is not paused and holds such a step is not refused, and the store is not called (`enginehost.epicService.ResumeEpic`). Each limited step is then resumed with `Steps.Resume`, which sends the owner's resume prompt in the step's loop (`enginehost.epicService.ResumeEpic`; `enginehost.Steps.Resume`). A resume that the step cap refuses leaves the step limited, and the step is listed in `stillLimited`. A step that is no longer limited, or has ended, is in neither list (`enginehost.epicService.ResumeEpic`). Any other error from a step's resume is `internal` with its text. The call stops there, the steps resumed before it stay resumed, and a pause the store cleared stays cleared (`enginehost.epicService.ResumeEpic`). The response is the stored epic read back, with `resumed` and `stillLimited` (`enginehost.epicService.ResumeEpic`). Resuming a step continues a step that already exists, so it runs whether or not the epic is parked. A park stays until `RetryEpic` ends it.

**CloseEpic.** `CloseEpic` takes `id`, `kind` and `by`, and returns the stored epic read back. `kind` is `superseded`, `shipped` or `abandoned`, and the close ends the epic in the phase `closed_superseded`, `closed_shipped` or `closed_abandoned` that the kind names. `by` is the epic that replaces it. A `superseded` close requires `by`, and an `abandoned` or `shipped` close stores whatever `by` it is given (`store.Store.CloseEpic`, `store.closeEndOf`). An empty `id` is `invalid_argument` with `id: empty`, and an unknown epic is `not_found` with `id: no epic <id>`. A close the store refuses writes nothing and is `failed_precondition` with its text: a `kind` that is none of the three, which reads `close epic <id>: kind "<kind>" is not superseded, shipped or abandoned`, and a `superseded` close with no `by`, which reads `epic <id> is superseded and names no epic that replaces it` (`enginehost.epicService.CloseEpic`, `enginehost.epicControlError`, `store.closeEndOf`). A close checks no other phase, so a close of an epic that is already closed writes its close again (`store.closeEpicIn`).

The closer is `planner` when the call's session is the step of the epic's running turn, and `owner` otherwise. A planner close routes the ending, and an owner close does not (`enginehost.epicService.closerOf`, `store.CloserPlanner`, `store.CloserOwner`). The close is one write (`store.closeEpicIn`, `store.closeEpicRows`):

- The running turn ends `free`, with the reason `epic closed as <kind>`, and its claimed triggers return to pending. Every pending trigger of the epic is then consumed, so the planner has nothing to claim (`store.endClosedTurn`, `store.closeEpicRows`; `internal/store/queries.sql: ConsumePendingEpicTriggers`).
- Each running node run of the epic ends with no outcome (`internal/store/queries.sql: EndRunningEpicNodeRuns`).
- Each open question of the epic is answered with the closed phase as its choice. No decision and no trigger is written for it (`store.answerOpenQuestions`, `internal/store/queries.sql: SetEpicQuestionAnswered`).
- Each evidence amendment of the epic that is checking is refused with the refusal `epic <id> closed as <kind>`, and the planner action that asked for it is refused with the same text; one that is capturing, whose item is applied already, is applied. Neither writes a decision, a tell or a trigger (`store.settleClosedAmendments`; see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments)).
- `parked` and `park_class` are cleared, `closed_by` is set to the closer and `superseded_by` to `by`, and the phase is set to the closed phase with its time as `phase_at` (`internal/store/queries.sql: SetEpicClosed`, `store.setEpicPhase`). The pause is cleared and the epic's due resumes close, as `ResumeEpic` does, and the epic's release slot is freed (`store.resumeEpic`, `internal/store/queries.sql: FreeEpicReleaseSlot`).
- A planner close adds one tell, `Closed as <kind>.`, or `Closed as <kind> by <by>.` when `by` is given. Attention routes it as the epic's notice, which opens on the epic's Report, so the owner is told of a planner close. An owner close adds no tell and no decision, so `changed` does not move (`store.closeTell`, `planner.tellBlockOf`, `planner.reportedPhase`).

The steps stop after the write lands. The service stops the step of the turn the close ended and each other running step of the epic (`enginehost.epicService.stopClosedSteps`). A planner close returns the same steps through its action, and the runner stops them (see **Actions** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns); `store.closePlannedEpic`). A closed epic is not live, which [Epics and Steps](#epics-and-steps) gives: the planner claims no turn for it (`store.checkClaimable`), the interpreter passes over it (`interpret.Runner.Pass`), a pass under way when the close lands carries out nothing more for it (`interpret.Runner.passFlow`), and no build or node starts on it (`kinds.Ended`). No sync of origin's main starts on it either, and one that ran ends with the close (see **Sync main** under [Workflows](#workflows)).

**Refusals.** The Connect codes and messages:

| Connect code | When | Message |
|---|---|---|
| `unavailable` | any call with no store open | `epic store unavailable` |
| `invalid_argument` | `CreateEpic`, for each failed check above | `<field>: <reason>`, as the check gives it |
| `invalid_argument` | `CreateEpic` with a reviewer no layer can hold, or an enabled reviewer the layers leave incomplete | `read the reviewers of <repo>: ...` or `merge the reviewers of <repo>: ...`, as the **Reviewers** paragraph above gives it |
| `already_exists` | `CreateEpic` with an `id` already stored | `id: <id> is already in the database` |
| `invalid_argument` | `CreateEpic` with a workflow that does not load at the base | `workflow: at base <commit>: <refusal>`, where the refusal is one of [Workflows](#workflows) |
| `invalid_argument` | `AnswerEpic` with an empty `epic_id` or `id`, a note with a `choice` or with blank text, or a question with no `choice` | `epic_id: empty`, `id: empty`, `choice: a note answers no question`, `text: empty` or `choice: empty` |
| `invalid_argument` | `AnswerEpic` with a `choice` the question does not offer, or `other` with blank text | `choice: <question> offers no choice <choice>`, or `text: empty` |
| `not_found` | `GetEpic` with an unknown `id` | `id: no epic <id>` |
| `not_found` | `AnswerEpic` with an unknown `epic_id` | `epic_id: no epic <id>` |
| `not_found` | `AnswerEpic` with a `question` that is not `Q<n>`, or that the epic does not hold | `question: no question <question> on epic <epic_id>` |
| `failed_precondition` | `AnswerEpic` with a `question` that a later question replaced, or that is answered | `question: Qn was replaced by Qm`, or `question: <question> is closed` |
| `already_exists` | `AnswerEpic` with an `id` recorded with other text or another choice | `id: <id> was recorded with other text` |
| `invalid_argument` | `PauseEpic`, `ResumeEpic`, `StopEpic`, `RetryEpic`, `KickEpic`, `ReleaseEpic`, `CloseEpic` or `SeeEpic` with an empty `id` | `id: empty` |
| `invalid_argument` | `KickEpic` with a `text` that is blank | `text: empty` |
| `invalid_argument` | `ArchiveRow` or `UnarchiveRow` with a `kind` other than `epic`, `session` or `headless` | `kind: "<kind>" is not epic, session or headless` |
| `invalid_argument` | `ArchiveRow` or `UnarchiveRow` with an empty `id` | `id: empty` |
| `invalid_argument` | `ReleaseEpic` with a position in `undo_none` below 1 | `undo_none: position <n> is below 1` |
| `not_found` | `PauseEpic`, `ResumeEpic`, `StopEpic`, `RetryEpic`, `KickEpic`, `ReleaseEpic`, `CloseEpic` or `SeeEpic` with an unknown `id` | `id: no epic <id>` |
| `failed_precondition` | a control refused by the state of its epic | the refusal text (see **Controls**, and [Release](#release) for `ReleaseEpic`) |
| `failed_precondition` | `CloseEpic` with a `kind` that is none of the three, or `superseded` with no `by` | the refusal text (see **CloseEpic**) |
| `invalid_argument` | `AmendEvidence` with an empty `id` or `requirement`, no `item`, or an `item` that `evidence.Check` refuses | `id: empty`, `requirement: empty`, `item: missing`, or one `item.<field>: <reason>` line per refusal of the item, joined with `; ` (`enginehost.checkAmendEvidence`) |
| `not_found` | `AmendEvidence` with an unknown `id` | `id: no epic <id>` |
| `failed_precondition` | `AmendEvidence` from a step of the epic, the step of its running turn included | `step <session> of epic <id> may not amend its evidence; the planner amends through its amend_evidence action, and the owner through this call` (`enginehost.epicService.ownerCaller`) |
| `failed_precondition` | `AmendEvidence` that the store refuses | the refusal text (see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments)) |
| `internal` | a store failure, or a failed `Steps.Stop` in `StopEpic` | the error text |

(`enginehost.epicUnavailableMessage`, `enginehost.epicService.ready`, `enginehost.epicService.CreateEpic`, `enginehost.epicService.GetEpic`, `enginehost.epicService.AnswerEpic`, `enginehost.answerError`, `enginehost.questionNotFound`, `enginehost.checkEpicAnswer`, `enginehost.epicControlError`, `enginehost.rowError`, `enginehost.refuseEpic`, `enginehost.epicStoreError`)

#### Release

A clean epic is released by the `release` node of its workflow (see [Workflows](#workflows)). An epic that ended with gaps reaches that node only once `ReleaseEpic` makes it clean (see **ReleaseEpic (R6).** below). The node's run takes the repository's release slot, prepares a candidate, has the release integrator finish a merge of origin's main that conflicts, sends the release back to planning when the integrator finds the candidate itself needs a change (see **Replan.** below), runs the epic's release steps in order, judges the result from the stored evidence, and then ends the epic `released`, or `done` when the epic has no release steps. `kinds.Release.Decide` decides each step from one read of the epic's rows (`store.Store.EpicFlow`), and `interpret.Runner.release` carries out the action it returns. The run ends `passed`. A stop parks the run and the epic, with its reason, and `orrery epic release` resumes the stopped release (see **ReleaseEpic (R6).** below).

**Slot and phases (R1).** A release phase is `clean`, `releasing`, `stopped`, `released` or `done` (`store.PhaseClean`, `store.PhaseReleasing`, `store.PhaseStopped`). The conclude node sets `clean` when the review leaves no gap (see **Conclude.** under [Workflows](#workflows)), and `ReleaseEpic` sets it for an epic that ended with gaps (see **ReleaseEpic (R6).** below). A clean epic with release steps takes its repository's release slot and moves to `releasing` in one write (`kinds.releaseClean`, `interpret.Runner.takeSlot`, `store.Store.TakeReleaseSlot`). The slot is the one row of `epic_release_slots` for the repository, so one epic holds it at a time (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). A clean epic while another epic holds the slot takes no action and waits, and its `release_slot` names the holder (`kinds.releaseClean`, `enginehost.epicService.storedEpic`). A clean epic with no release steps takes no slot and reaches its ending at once (`kinds.releaseClean`). A `releasing` epic keeps the slot through a stop. A stopped epic keeps it until `StopEpic` frees it, and `ReleaseEpic` takes it back when it is free (`store.resumeRelease`; see **Controls** under [Epics](#epics)). A replan frees it, and the release takes it again once the replan's plan has ended (see **Replan.** below). A released or done epic frees the slot it holds, and then its run ends `passed` (`kinds.releaseEnded`, `interpret.Runner.freeSlot`, `store.Store.FreeReleaseSlot`).

**Candidate (R2).** `interpret.Runner.prepareRelease` makes the candidate without a checkout. It reads origin's default branch, the one `git ls-remote --symref origin HEAD` names, and fetches its commit into `refs/orrery/<slug>/release/origin` (`interpret.fetchOrigin`, `interpret.defaultBranch`, `build.Slug`). Both git commands run with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, so an origin that asks for a username or password fails the read rather than waiting on a prompt, and each runs in a process group of its own (`interpret.remoteOutput`). A release's read has no time limit of its own. When that commit is an ancestor of the epic's branch tip, the candidate is the tip, and no Done gate runs (`interpret.decideRelease`, `interpret.isAncestor`). A sync of origin's main that passed while the epic ran merged origin's commit into the tip, so the candidate is the tip unless origin's main has moved since (see **Sync main** under [Workflows](#workflows)). Otherwise origin's commit is merged into the tip with `git merge-tree --write-tree`, without a checkout (`interpret.mergeTree`, `interpret.runMergeTree`, `interpret.mergedTree`). A merge that does not conflict is committed with `git commit-tree`, which honors `commit.gpgSign`, and pinned at `refs/orrery/<slug>/release/<run id>-<attempt>` (`interpret.mergeOrigin`, `interpret.runPin`, `build.Pin`). A merge that conflicts records no candidate and prepares no revision. Its result names origin's commit, the branch of origin it was fetched from and each conflicted path once, and the release integrator finishes the merge (see **Release integrator.** below; `interpret.decideRelease`, `interpret.Runner.prepareRelease`). A merge that conflicts in no path git names is the error `merge of origin's commit <origin> into <tip> conflicts` (`interpret.conflictMessage`). A merged candidate runs the epic's Done gates in order at the candidate, walked as an integrate run walks them (see **Batch integration** under [Workflows](#workflows); `kinds.doneGates`, `kinds.gatesAction`). A gate that does not pass stops the release with `done gate <gate> did not pass at <candidate>`. A gate lost at a restart twice stops it with the park reason of that walk. When every gate passes, the candidate is recorded with the merge as its one commit (`interpret.Runner.recordCandidate`). Any error of the prepare stops the release with its text, and the prepare waits while the epic is paused or parked (`kinds.prepareRelease`, `kinds.prepare`). The prepare writes its result, `{error, behind, candidate, tip}`, with `origin`, `branch` and `conflicts` added for a merge that conflicts, before it sets the run's revision, so a restart between the two writes keeps whether the candidate was behind (`interpret.Runner.prepareRelease`, `store.Store.RecordNodeResult`, `store.Store.PrepareNode`, `kinds.ReleaseResult`).

**Release integrator.** A releasing epic whose prepare recorded a conflicting merge hands that merge to the release integrator, a writing step that finishes and commits it, in at most three rounds (`kinds.candidateAction`, `kinds.integratorAction`). A merge that does not conflict starts no integrator. Each round runs as one step (`kinds.integratorStart`, `interpret.Runner.startIntegrator`):

- Its role is `integrator` in the machine's role file. A role file with no `integrator` key runs it under the agent `release` names, so a role file written before the integrator existed keeps working; an `integrator` key that names no agent is an error, as it is for any role (`roles.AgentOr`).
- Round `n` is `store.NodeWorkID` of the run's id at the generation of its candidate (`kinds.gatedRun`), its attempt and `integrator <n>`, so a restart finds the round under that id (`kinds.integratorWork`). The step carries the epic and the run's path as its labels, and `EpicFlow` reads it back by them (`store.readLabeledSteps`).
- It runs in `wt`, inside a directory named by `store.NodeWorkID` of the run's id, its attempt and `integrator`, under `release-worktrees` in the state directory, so a runner with no state directory cannot start a round (`interpret.Runner.integratorDir`). The first round removes any worktree an earlier start left there, adds the worktree detached at the tip, and runs `git -c merge.conflictStyle=zdiff3 merge --no-ff --no-commit <origin>` in it, so HEAD is the tip, MERGE_HEAD is origin's commit and each conflict is in place with its merge base. git's exit for a merge that conflicts is no error, and a merge that is not in progress afterwards is one (`interpret.startMerge`). A later round runs in the same worktree, whose HEAD must be the merge the round before it made (`interpret.keptMerge`).
- It starts as a `StepStart` at the tip with `Unowned` set, its done value `done`, the schema `interpret.IntegratorSchema` and the integrator's return check (see **Integrator checks.** below). It starts only while the epic holds the release slot and is neither paused nor parked. Without the slot the release stops with `the release integrator did not start: this epic does not hold the release slot`. A role, worktree, merge, prompt or return check that fails, or a start refused for good, records the round failed with `<conflict>; the release integrator did not start: <cause>` (`interpret.Runner.failIntegrator`). `<conflict>` is `merge of origin's commit <origin> into <tip> conflicts in <paths>` throughout (`interpret.conflictMessage`).
- Its prompt is the role text (`internal/interpret/integrator.md`), then these sections, each under a `## ` heading in this order, with `None.` for a section with no lines (`interpret.Runner.integratorPrompt`, `planner.WriteSection`):
  - `Contract`: the Intent, each Requirement with its evidence item as the planner gets it, and each Must Not Ship entry, which the store holds as the epic's exclusions (`interpret.integratorContract`, `planner.RequirementItems`, `planner.ListLines`).
  - `Decisions`: every epic decision, then every turn's (`planner.DecisionLines`).
  - `Merge`: the worktree; the tip as the candidate's side, HEAD when the merge began, ours and stage 2; origin's commit with its branch as origin's side, MERGE_HEAD, theirs and stage 3; the merge base; and the commit message `Merge origin's commit <origin> into release tip <tip>`. A later round adds the merge it amends, which is HEAD (`interpret.mergeLines`, `interpret.mergeMessage`).
  - `Conflicted paths`: each conflicted path as `- <code> <path>`, where the code is the one git status gives the stages `git merge-tree --write-tree -z` names for it, such as `UU`, `AA`, `UD` or `DU`. Under it come the commits of each side since the merge base that touched it, newest first, as `  candidate: <commit> <subject>` and `  origin: <commit> <subject>`, at most 20 a side (`interpret.conflictedLines`, `interpret.conflictStatus`, `interpret.pathCommits`).
  - `Tasks`: each accepted task as `- T<n> <title>`, then its deliverable, its files and the commit the integrate run that passed composed for it, or `None.` (`interpret.integratorTaskLines`, `interpret.composedCommits`).
  - `Candidate commits` and `Origin commits`: `git log --oneline` of each side from the merge base, the newest 200 and then how many there are (`interpret.logLines`).
  - `Same change`: each pair of a candidate commit and an origin commit whose `git patch-id --stable` is equal, as `- candidate <commit> and origin <commit>, same: <subject>`, and each pair with equal subject and author date whose patches differ, with `differs` in place of `same` (`interpret.sameChangeLines`, `interpret.sideCommits`, `interpret.patchIDs`).
  - `Checks after you`: each Done gate with its argv, or why it does not resolve, and each capture with its Requirement and argv (`interpret.Runner.checkLines`).
  - `Previous attempt`: the failure the round before's merge met, as **Integrator rounds.** below gives it (`interpret.previousLines`).
- Its return is `status`, which is `done` or `blocked`, `commit`, `resolutions` (each a `path`, a `took` of `both`, `origin`, `candidate` or `regenerated`, and `kept`), `other_changes` (each a `path` and a `change`), `checks` (each a `command` and a `result`), `unresolved` (each a `path`, `candidate`, `origin`, `decision` and `side`) and `evidence` (`interpret.IntegratorSchema`). `side` is `candidate` when the decision is a change to the epic's own candidate that the merge cannot make, such as a candidate test that fails for a cause the candidate's side alone holds, and `merge` when it is how to resolve the merge itself; the role text says which is which (`internal/interpret/integrator.md`).

**Integrator checks.** The integrator's return check runs on every return that passes `schema`, as `return` under **Checks** in [Step lifecycle](#step-lifecycle) gives, and each failure is one line the step loop sends back. A round whose returns fail four times ends `checks_failed` (`interpret.integratorCheck`). A done return names a commit, has exactly one resolution for each conflicted path and none for another path, leaves `unresolved` empty and gives `evidence`. A blocked return names no commit and gives `unresolved` or `evidence` (`interpret.integratorShape`). The commit of a done return must also pass these checks, besides the step's own head, ancestry and clean checks (`interpret.integratorFailures`):

- `head`: HEAD is the commit.
- `parents`: its parents are exactly the tip and then origin's commit, so it is one commit on the tip and no other commit follows it (`interpret.parentsFailure`).
- `unmerged`: `git ls-files -u` lists no path (`interpret.unmergedFailure`).
- `markers`: no path the commit changes from either parent holds a line that matches `^<<<<<<< `, `^=======$` or `^>>>>>>> ` (`interpret.markerFailure`).
- `other_changes`: each path where the commit differs from the tree `git merge-tree --write-tree` writes for the merge is a conflicted path or is listed in `other_changes` (`interpret.unlistedFailure`, `interpret.mergeTreeOf`).

A re-adopted integrator step gets its check back: `orreryd`'s `StepsConfig.ReadoptCheck` asks the planner's, then `interpret.ReadoptCheck`, then the build's. `interpret.ReadoptCheck` rebuilds the check for a row with the integrator's schema from the running release run its path label names. A run that is not running, or whose result names no conflicting merge from the row's revision, is an error, so the row is not re-adopted and ends `engine_restarted` (`cmd/orreryd.readoptCheck`, `interpret.ReadoptCheck`).

**Integrator rounds.** Once a round's end is stored, the release stores a `ReleaseRun` of part `integrator` for it, at the position `3 × generation + n`, before anything else runs (`kinds.integratorPosition`, `interpret.Runner.recordIntegrator`, `store.Store.RecordRelease`):

- A `done` end whose commit passes the commit checks again passes. Its head is the commit, which is pinned at `refs/orrery/<slug>/release/<run id>-<attempt>`; nothing is pinned before (`interpret.integratorCommit`, `interpret.runPin`). A commit that fails them fails the round with `<conflict>; the release integrator's commit was refused: <checks>`.
- A `blocked` return fails the round with its report: `the release integrator stopped the merge of origin's commit <origin> into release tip <tip>:`, then `- <path>: side: <side>; decision: <decision>; candidate: <candidate>; origin: <origin>` for each path it stopped on, or `: <evidence>` when it named none. The report fits `TurnReasonMax`, the most of it the epic's park keeps: each path's line gets an equal share of what is left, at least 160 bytes, and is cut at its end, and the paths no room is left for are counted in a last line, `paths not shown: <n>`, which room is always kept for (`interpret.blockedReason`, `interpret.cutText`). A blocked return that names at least one path, every one with side `candidate`, also asks for a replan: the write that stores the round also stores a release run of part `replan` at the round's attempt, position `0`, with step `replan`, the round's tip as its start and the report as its reason, unless a replan run of the epic already names that tip, since a replan that left the tip where it was would only send the same merge back to the integrator (`interpret.candidateSide`, `store.Store.RecordIntegratorRound`). Any other blocked return asks for none.
- Any other end fails the round with `<conflict>; ` and the end as `interpret.releaseEndReason` names it for `release integrator`, or with the decode error of a `done` return that does not decode.

A failed round stops the release with its reason, unless a replan run of its attempt was stored: then the release goes back to planning (see **Replan.** below; `kinds.integratorAction`, `kinds.replanAction`). A passed round's commit runs the epic's Done gates in order and then the capture of each Requirement that names an evidence item, in the green phase, at the commit, walked under the id `<run id>-integrator-<n>` as an integrate run walks them (`kinds.mergedChecks`, `kinds.doneGates`, `kinds.captures`). The action that runs them names the commit as its revision, and the work runs there (`kinds.Action`, `interpret.pass.work`). A capture fails as an integrate run's does (`kinds.captureCauses`). When every gate and capture passes, the commit is recorded as the candidate, with itself as its one commit, and the first release step starts from it (`interpret.Runner.recordCandidate`). When one fails, the next round starts in the same worktree, with the failure under `Previous attempt`: the gate's name, its exit, its time out or why it did not run, and its stdout and stderr tails; or each failing capture with why it did not run, or its exit, verdict and judge's reason (`kinds.gateFailure`, `kinds.captureFailures`). So a failure is sent back twice at most. A failure in the third round stops the release with `done gate <gate> did not pass at <commit>`, or with the capture causes and `at <commit>`. A gate or capture lost at a restart twice, or whose last run exits `store.EnvironmentExit`, stops it with the park reason of that walk. The integrator's worktree is removed before the candidate is recorded and before the run's stop (`interpret.Runner.removeIntegrator`).

**Replan.** A release run whose attempt holds a replan run is sent back to planning, so the planner plans the change to the candidate that the release integrator named, and the release then merges origin's main again from the new candidate. The kind decides `replan`, with the replan run's reason, for a releasing epic whose failed integrator round has a replan run of its attempt, and for a stopped epic whose release node run a retry turned running with one (`kinds.Release.Decide`, `kinds.replanAction`; see **Controls** under [Epics](#epics)). The plan the replan runs is the workflow's last plan node, in the order the walk meets them, loop bodies included, whose body holds an `integrate` node, so the change reaches the epic's branch; in the built-in `epic` that is the correct loop's `fix` (`interpret.replanPlan`). A workflow with no such plan stops the release instead, with the report and `; the workflow has no plan whose body holds an integrate node, so the release cannot plan the change to the candidate` (`interpret.pass.replan`). Otherwise the interpreter removes the integrator's worktree, then one write (`store.Store.ReplanRelease`), which changes nothing unless the release node run is running at that attempt and the epic is `releasing` or `stopped`:

- moves the epic to `correcting` and frees the release slot it holds, so another epic may release while this one plans (`internal/store/queries.sql: ReplanEpicRelease`, `internal/store/queries.sql: FreeEpicReleaseSlot`);
- starts that plan's run at `<release path>/A<attempt>/<plan id>` (`kinds.ReplanPrefix`), with `store.NodeRunID` of the epic, that path, the release run's round and `start` as its id, and the epic's highest turn seq as its mark, as a later plan starts;
- adds the trigger `release:` and `store.NodeRunID` of the epic, the plan's path, the round and `replan`, with the cause `the release goes back to planning for a change to the candidate: <report>`, which wakes the planner with the integrator's report (`interpret.replanTriggerPrefix`, `interpret.replanCause`);
- adds the same text as a tell, cut to `TurnReasonMax` bytes, routed as the epic's own notice.

While the plan runs, the release decides it as any plan is decided: the planner's turns, the build of each task of a turn above its mark under `<plan path>/<build id>/T<seq>`, and its settle walks, whose integrate runs move the epic's branch (`interpret.pass.replanning`, `interpret.pass.plan`). The plan ends as **Built or park** under [Workflows](#workflows) gives. A correcting epic whose replan plan has ended resumes its release: one write (`store.Store.ResumeReplannedRelease`), which changes nothing unless the release node run is running at that attempt, moves the run to its next attempt, clearing its outcome, output, revision, refs, refs-checked flag, result and end, and moves the epic from `correcting` to `clean` (`kinds.resumeAction`; `internal/store/queries.sql: NextEpicNodeRunAttempt`, `internal/store/queries.sql: CleanReplannedEpic`). The release then takes the slot as a clean epic does, and prepares its candidate from the epic's branch tip. A candidate that passed at or before a replan's attempt is never reused, since the replan changed the branch (`kinds.currentCandidate`, `kinds.lastReplan`). The planner's role text names a trigger whose id starts with `release:` and says to plan the change each listed decision names, and nothing else (`internal/planner/role.md`). A blocked return with any decision on the merge's side, or with no path named, stops the release as before, and so does a second candidate-side block from a tip a replan already named; `orrery epic retry ID` on such a stopped release sends it back to planning on the owner's word (see **Controls** under [Epics](#epics)).

**Release steps (R3).** After the candidate passes, the release steps run in order. A step that has a passed run, at any attempt, is passed over. Each other step runs as one writing step (`kinds.walkSteps`, `kinds.stepAction`, `interpret.Runner.startReleaseStep`):

- Its role is `release` in the machine's role file (`roles.Agent`). It runs in a worktree detached at its start, which is the candidate for the first step and the head the last passed step left for each later one. The worktree is `wt`, inside a directory named by the step's id, under `release-worktrees` in the state directory. The id is `store.NodeWorkID` of the run, its attempt and `release <n>` (`interpret.Runner.releaseStepDir`, `build.AddWorktree`). A runner with no state directory cannot start a step.
- It starts as a `StepStart` with `Unowned` set, its done value `done` and the epic as its label. Its done return runs the head, ancestry and clean checks and no owned check, and it has no gate (`enginehost.commitChecksWithoutOwned`, `enginehost.StepStart.Unowned`; see **Checks** under [Step lifecycle](#step-lifecycle)).
- Its prompt names only that step, its target and its undo, the release's postconditions (`release_then`), its start commit, and the one rerun of CI on the same commit that the step may make. It says that a judge decides whether the release met its postconditions from the evidence its steps return and nothing else, running no command, so the step's evidence must show each postcondition it affects with the output that proves it. Its return is `status`, which is `done` or `blocked`, `commit` (HEAD), `commits` (each commit it added on top of its start) and `evidence` (`interpret.releaseStepPrompt`, `interpret.releaseStepSchema`).
- Once the step's end is stored, the release stores a `ReleaseRun` from it, before any later step starts. The run passes only when the end is `done`, HEAD descends from the step's start, no commit between the start and HEAD is a merge, and the commits the step listed are exactly the commits between them (`interpret.Runner.recordReleaseStep`, `interpret.releaseStepReason`, `store.Store.RecordRelease`). The step's worktree is removed once its run is stored (`build.RemoveWorktrees`). A passing step's HEAD is the start of the next step.

**Release proof.** `store.ReleaseProofs` gives each contract release step, in contract order, a state and the run that decides it. The first of these rules that holds gives the state (`store.ReleaseProofs`, `store.releaseProofOf`):

- `passed` when a release run of the step's position and part `release` passed. The passed run of the highest attempt decides it.
- `running` when the release node run is running and has a work step for the position with no end. The work step is the one `store.NodeWorkID` names for the run's id, its attempt and `release <n>` (`store.runningStep`).
- `failed` when a release run of the position did not pass. The failed run of the highest attempt decides it (`store.highestAttempt`).
- `waiting` otherwise.

For `passed` and `failed`, the deciding run's `evidence`, `reason`, `attempt` and time are the proof's. For `running` and `waiting` they are empty and `0` (`store.decidedProof`, `store.releaseStepRuns`). `GetEpic` carries the proofs as `release_proof` (`enginehost.epicService.storedEpic`, `enginehost.releaseProofMessage`). `Epics.Get` carries them as `release_steps`, with each `evidence` and `reason` redacted (`server.epicReleaseProofMessages`; see [Epics](#epics)).

**Stops (R4).** Every failure the release finds stops it. `interpret.Runner.stopRelease` makes one write (`store.Store.StopRelease`): the release node run parks with outcome `failed` and the reason as its output, the epic parks with the reason as the class `store.releaseStopClass` gives it, and the epic moves from `releasing` to `stopped`. The write leaves the release slot as it is. An owner class, `release_undo_none` or `release_owner_stop`, opens the park question and adds the reason as a tell, which is routed as the epic's own notice (see **Tells** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)), so each such stop makes a notice of its own. A `release_step` stop adds no tell and wakes the planner instead (see **Park classes** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)). The run's output is cut to `NodeOutputMax` bytes (`store.capNodeOutput`), and the epic's `parked` text and the tell to `TurnReasonMax` bytes (`store.capTurnText`). The stop reasons are these (`kinds.Release.Decide`):

- the error of a candidate prepare, or `release result is not a JSON object: <error>` when the run's stored result does not decode;
- a Done gate that did not pass, or a Done gate lost at a restart twice, as above;
- a round of the release integrator that failed with no replan run of its attempt, a Done gate or capture that failed on the merge of its third round, and `the release integrator did not start: this epic does not hold the release slot`, as **Integrator rounds.** above gives;
- `release step <n> (<step>) failed: <reason>`, where the reason is the step's end, such as `step ended stopped`, with its status, its stored reason and its first failed check (`interpret.releaseEndReason`), or the reason its run recorded, such as `HEAD <head> does not descend from the step's start <start>`, `the step added a merge commit: <commits>`, or `the step did not start: <cause>` (`interpret.Runner.failReleaseStep`);
- `release step <n> (<step>) did not start: this epic does not hold the release slot`, and `the judge did not start: this epic does not hold the release slot` for the judge;
- `release step <n> (<step>) failed at an earlier attempt with undo none, so it runs again only when named`;
- the judge's failure, as below.

A step that a restart ends `engine_restarted` has a failed end, and the release stops at it like any other. The step's prompt lets it rerun CI once on the same commit. The release never reruns a failed step by itself, and a further attempt is the owner's call through `ReleaseEpic`, or the planner's `resume_release` action, which resumes a stopped release of an epic that holds its repository's release slot (see **Actions** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)). A release its integrator stopped is also moved on by `RetryEpic`, the owner's `orrery epic retry ID` or the planner's `retry_epic`, which sends it back to planning (see **Replan.** above).

**Judge (R5).** After the last release step passes, a judge runs (`kinds.judgeAction`, `interpret.Runner.startJudge`). It is a read-only step with role `judge`, run at the candidate in the repository and not at the head the steps left. Its prompt holds `release_then`, and for each passed release step the step's name, its commits and its evidence, all taken from the stored release runs. The prompt tells it to run no command. Its return is `status` (`done`), `released` and `uncovered`, the postconditions the evidence does not cover (`interpret.releaseJudgePrompt`, `interpret.releaseJudgeSchema`). The judge passes when its end is `done`, `released` is true and `uncovered` is empty. Otherwise the release stops with the reason: its end, its decode error, `uncovered postconditions: <list>`, or `the judge did not release the epic` (`interpret.releaseJudgeReason`, `interpret.Runner.recordJudge`).

**Ending.** A passed judge ends the epic. `interpret.Runner.endEpic` calls the `Ending` of `interpret.Config` with the phase `released` (or `done` for an epic with no release steps) and the body `kinds.EndingBody` gives for that phase: `Released. No gaps.` or `Done. No gaps.` when the review held none, and otherwise `Released with <n> gap(s):` or `Done with <n> gap(s):` and one `- <gap>` line per gap, as **Conclude.** gives them (`interpret.Ending`, `interpret.Runner.endEpic`, `kinds.endingAction`, `kinds.Gaps`). The next pass frees the slot and ends the run `passed` (`kinds.releaseEnded`). orreryd sets the store as its `Ending`: `store.Store.EndEpic` sets the phase and adds the body as the epic's next tell in one write, and an epic already in that phase changes nothing, so a restart ends it once (`cmd/orreryd`; `store.Store.EndEpic`). A `Runner` with no `Ending` fails a release that reaches its ending with `no Ending configured`; the epic then keeps its phase and whatever slot it holds, and that error is returned again on each pass.

**StopEpic.** `StopEpic` on an epic with no running turn that holds the release slot, whether it is releasing or stopped, frees the slot and stops its open release step, its judge or its open round of the release integrator, as **Controls** under [Epics](#epics) gives. The stopped step's end is recorded as a failed run, and a releasing epic stops on that run. A releasing epic with no open step stops at its next step or judge decision, since that decision finds the slot not held (`store.stopReleaseSlot`, `store.openReleaseStep`, `store.readLabeledSteps`).

**ReleaseEpic (R6).** `orrery epic release ID [--undo-none N]...` and the `epic_release` tool make the call `ReleaseEpic` of the `Epics` service on the session socket (`enginehost.epicService.ReleaseEpic`). `serve` forwards no `ReleaseEpic` call (`server.epicsServer`). The call releases an epic in one write (`store.Store.ReleaseEpic`), in one of two ways. An epic that ended with gaps is released on the owner's word, as the paragraph after the list below gives. Any other epic must be stopped, and its call resumes the stopped release (`store.resumeRelease`). It takes the slot when the slot is free, and it:

- for each distinct position that `undo_none` names, in the order given, stores a `named` release run for the parked run's next attempt, and appends an owner decision with the id `release-undo-none-<position>-<attempt>` (`store.recordNamedReleases`);
- moves the parked release node run to its next attempt, clearing its outcome, output, revision, refs, result and end (`store.retryNode`), unparks the epic, moves it to `releasing`, and adds the tell `release resumed at step <n>, <step>`, or `release resumed at the judge` when every step has passed (`store.resumedReleaseText`).

The release resumes at the first step with no passed run (`store.unfinishedReleaseStep`). A step that failed at an earlier attempt runs again at the next one, except an undo-none step, which runs again only when the call names its position (`kinds.undoNoneStopped`). A named position whose step has passed is recorded as the owner's decision, and nothing runs for it, since the walk starts only steps with no passed run. A call on an epic that is not ended with gaps is refused, and writes nothing, when (`store.checkReleaseResume`, `store.namedUndoNone`):

- the epic is not stopped: `epic <id> is not stopped`;
- it has no parked release run: `epic <id> has no parked release run`;
- another epic holds the release slot of its repository: `epic <holder> holds the release slot of <repository>`;
- a position is below 1, above the number of steps, or of a step that is not undo-none: `position <n> of epic <id> is not an undo-none release step`;
- the first unfinished step is undo-none, has a failed run and is not named: `release step <n> of epic <id> is undo-none and failed; name it to run it again`.

An epic that ended with gaps is released on the owner's word, with no undo-none step (`store.releaseEndedWithGaps`). The call appends the owner decision `release-ended-with-gaps`, with the text `owner released epic <id>, which ended with gaps`, and moves the epic to `clean` in one write, adding no tell. Its release node then takes the release slot of the repository as a clean epic's does (see **Slot and phases (R1).** above). The call is refused, and writes nothing, when `undoNone` names any position: `epic <id> has not started a release; name no undo-none step`. Every other epic that is not stopped is refused with `epic <id> is not stopped`.

The service refuses an empty `id` and a position below 1 before the store is asked, and an unknown epic is `not_found`. The refusals are those of [Epics](#epics) (`enginehost.epicService.ReleaseEpic`).

#### Workflows

A workflow is a YAML file that names the nodes an epic's run is made of, in order. This subsection gives the file, its format, the load check that refuses a file, the built-in `epic`, what an epic keeps, and how `orreryd` runs a pinned workflow (see **Running**).

**The file.** A workflow is `.orrery/workflows/<name>.yaml` in the repository, or the built-in of that name. A workflow name, a node id and an include each match `^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*$` (`workflow.namePattern`). The file is one YAML document. Its top level is a mapping of exactly two keys: `name`, required and equal to the file's name without `.yaml`, and `nodes`, required, a non-empty sequence of nodes. Any other key is refused (`workflow.Parse`, `workflow.Check`, `workflow.document`, `workflow.readWorkflow`, `workflow.readTop`).

**Nodes.** A node is a mapping with exactly one of `kind`, `loop` or `include`. A node with none of them, or with two, is refused (`workflow.formOf`). The forms are these:

- A kind node has `id`, `kind` and the kind's settings, which sit beside `id` and `kind`. The registered kinds are these (`workflow.kinds`):
  - `plan` sits at the top level and in a loop body. Its setting is `body`, a non-empty sequence of `build`, `integrate` and the check kinds below. The load check requires neither a `build` nor an `integrate` in it. It reports the outcome `complete`.
  - `build` sits only in a plan body. It takes no settings and reports no outcome.
  - `integrate` sits only in a plan body. It takes no settings and no body, and reports the outcomes `passed` and `failed`. Its run is decided as **Batch integration** below gives (`workflow.kinds`, `kinds.Integrate`).
  - The check kinds `gate`, `evidence`, `owned`, `command` and `agent` sit at the top level, in a loop body and in a plan body. Each takes no body and reports the outcomes `passed` and `failed`. Their settings are these (`workflow.readGateSettings`, `workflow.readEvidenceSettings`, `workflow.readOwnedSettings`, `workflow.readCommandSettings`, `workflow.readAgentSettings`):
    - `gate` takes `gate`, a gate's name matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`, and `at`.
    - `evidence` takes `at`.
    - `owned` takes `paths`, a non-empty sequence of repository-relative paths. No element is empty, `.` or `..`, and none starts with `/`. A path that ends in `/` names a directory and everything under it.
    - `command` takes `argv`, a non-empty sequence of non-empty strings whose first item contains a `/`, `limit`, an integer of 1 or more that counts seconds, `needs`, an optional mapping from a pool name matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` to an integer of 1 or more, and `at`.
    - `agent` takes `agent` and `prompt`, each required and not blank, `schema`, a required mapping, and `at`.
    - `at` is `base` or `head`, and is required by `gate`, `evidence`, `command` and `agent`. `base` is the epic's base, and `head` is the head of the epic's branch `epic/<slug>` (`workflow.readAt`, `interpret.revisionOf`).
  - The epic kinds `review`, `conclude` and `release` sit only at the top level, and `closure` sits only in a loop body. Each takes no settings and no body. `review` and `conclude` report the outcome `passed`, and `closure` and `release` report `passed` and `failed`. The format registers them so a workflow can name them. The interpreter always registers `release`, whose run is [Release](#release), and registers `review`, `closure` and `conclude` only when it has both `Reviewers` and `Ending` (see **End-of-epic review**); without them a node of one of those starts and then waits (see **Running** and **Reserved names**; `interpret.New`).
- A loop node has `id`, `loop` and `body`. `loop` is a mapping of `max`, `until` and `spent`, and no other key (`workflow.readLoop`, `workflow.readLoopSettings`, `workflow.readLoopFields`, `workflow.readMax`, `workflow.readUntil`, `workflow.readSpent`):
  - `max` is required: an integer of 1 or more.
  - `until` is required: `<node id>.<outcome>`, naming a direct child of the loop's body whose kind reports that outcome.
  - `spent` is `park` or `continue`, and `park` when it is absent.

  A loop sits at the top level and in a loop body. Its `body` is a non-empty sequence that holds what the top level holds, `plan`, `loop`, `include` and the check kinds, and `closure`, but not `review`, `conclude` or `release`.
- An include node has the single key `include`, the name of another workflow. It sits at the top level and in a loop body, and expansion replaces it with that workflow's nodes.

The load check validates `max`, `until` and `spent`, and the interpreter enforces them when it runs a loop (see **Running**).

**Check kinds.** Each check kind's node run is decided on each pass by its kind, which asks the interpreter for work: `prepare` records the revision the work runs at, `start` starts the work, and `refs` compares the refs (see **Running**). Every check kind but `owned` is prepared first, and `prepare` waits while the epic is paused or parked, so no work starts then. Once the revision is recorded, the work runs to its end whatever the pause (`kinds.prepare`, `interpret.Runner.prepare`). The rules by kind are these (`kinds.Gate.Decide`, `kinds.Command.Decide`, `kinds.Evidence.Decide`, `kinds.Owned.Decide`, `kinds.Agent.Decide`):

- `gate` and `command` run one check. It ends `passed` when it exits 0 without timing out, and `failed` otherwise, with its stdout and then its stderr as output (`kinds.checkEnd`, `kinds.streams`). A check that did not run ends `failed` with the reason it did not run. `gate` runs the gate of its name, resolved from the epic's gates and then the repository's project file as the plan check resolves them. A gate that does not resolve, and an agent gate, do not run (`interpret.Runner.withGate`, `build.ResolveGate`). `command` runs its `argv` with no shell, and `argv[0]` is a path that is never looked up on `PATH` (`enginehost.gateProgram`). A command's `limit` and `needs` apply as a gate's do, so a command with no `needs` draws one member of the `gate` pool (`interpret.Runner.startCheck`, `enginehost.NodeChecks.request`, `gates.DefaultPool`). The check runs in a detached worktree at the run's revision, under the grant of its needs (`enginehost.NodeChecks.run`).
- `evidence` runs each Requirement that names an evidence item, in contract order, as `Evidence.RunItem` runs an item, under an id derived from the node run and its attempt. Its phase is `red` at `base` and `green` at `head` (`interpret.Runner.startEvidence`, `enginehost.Evidence.RunItem`). It ends `passed` when every such run passed, with one output line per Requirement. An epic whose Requirements name no evidence item ends `passed` with a note, and runs nothing (`kinds.Evidence.Decide`, `kinds.evidenceEnd`).
- `owned` records, as a JSON list, the paths the epic's branch changes over its change base, which is its base until a sync of origin's main passes (see **Sync main**; `interpret.changeBase`). An owned node records `[]` when nothing changed (`interpret.Runner.recordOwned`). It ends `passed` when every listed path is owned by `paths`, and `failed` with the paths that are not as output. A result that is not a JSON list of paths ends `failed` (`kinds.Owned.Decide`, `kinds.owns`). It records no revision.
- `agent` runs one read-only step of its `agent` with its `prompt` and `schema`, at the run's revision, labelled with the epic and no task (`interpret.Runner.startAgent`). It ends `passed` when the step ends `done`, and `failed` otherwise. Its output is the step's end with its result when it ends `done`, and with its reason otherwise (`kinds.Agent.Decide`).

**Failed checks.** A check node run that ends `failed` is decided by where it sits (`interpret.pass.failed`):

- At the top level it parks with the reason `workflow node <path> failed: <output>`. The park is a planner park of class `node`: it parks the epic and writes the trigger `park:<n>`, and adds no tell (`store.Store.ParkNode`; see **Park classes** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)). The walk stops at the parked run, so nothing after it runs until `epic retry ID NODE` reruns it (see [Epics](#epics)). An evidence node at base that found a Requirement met parks with a **Met at base** question instead (see **Met at base**).
- In a loop body it ends `failed` with its output. The walk takes that end as the end of the round, so the loop decides the round as it decides any other: it ends when its `until` names this outcome, and otherwise it moves on or spends its rounds (`interpret.pass.walk`, `interpret.pass.loop`).
- In a plan body it ends `failed` with its output, and it writes the trigger `workflow:` with a derived id and the cause `workflow node <path> failed: <output>`, so the planner plans a turn. A passing check in a plan body wakes nobody (`store.Store.EndNodesWaking`).

An integrate run is not decided by these rules. Its end is its batch, and a failed batch writes its trigger (see **Batch integration**).

**Ref compare.** A `gate`, `command`, `evidence` or `integrate` run snapshots the refs when its work is prepared, and compares a new snapshot with that one once its work has ended, before its outcome counts (`interpret.Runner.prepare`, `interpret.Runner.prepareBatch`, `interpret.Runner.refSnapshot`, `interpret.Runner.checkRefs`, `build.Snapshot`, `build.Compare`). An integrate run's snapshot is taken when its batch is claimed, and its compare comes after its Done gates and captures (see **Batch integration**). A snapshot holds the primary checkout's HEAD and every ref of the repository, except `refs/orrery/` and each epic's branch in that repository. A move parks when it changes `refs/heads/main`, the branch the primary checkout had checked out at the snapshot, or `HEAD`. The first parking move parks the run and the epic, at any position, with the reason `workflow node <path> moved <ref> (<from> to <to>)`. Every other move is a tell with that text, which routes to no surface (see **Tells** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)). The compare is recorded once, so a repeat adds no tell (`store.Store.CheckNodeRefs`, `store.parkCheckedRun`). `owned` and `agent` take no compare.

**Loading.** `Load` takes a workflow name and a reader of repository paths (`workflow.Reader`, `workflow.Load`). It reads `.orrery/workflows/<name>.yaml` first, and a repository file replaces the built-in of the same name. When that path is absent, `Load` uses the built-in of that name, which is `epic` alone. A name that is neither is refused, naming both (`workflow.Load`, `workflow.builtinText`, `workflow.resolve`). Each include is expanded in place by the same rule, and an include cycle is refused, naming the chain (`workflow.expand`, `workflow.expandBody`, `workflow.expandInclude`). A load includes each workflow name at most once: a second include of a name is refused wherever it sits. The expanded workflow is then checked (`workflow.Load`).

**The check.** `Check` refuses an expanded workflow that has an include left, an id held by more than one node anywhere in the workflow, or a loop whose `until` does not name a direct child of its body whose kind reports that outcome. It returns the first refusal (`workflow.Check`, `workflow.checkNodes`, `workflow.checkUntil`).

**The built-in `epic`.** The built-in is a `red` node, an `evidence` node at `base`; then a `plan` holding a `build` and an `integrate`; then a `review`; then a loop `correct` (`max` 2, `until` `closure.passed`, `spent` `continue`) whose body is a `plan` `fix` holding a `rebuild` and a `reintegrate`, then a `closure`; then a `conclude` and a `release` (`workflow.epicText`). Its canonical text is:

```yaml
name: epic
nodes:
  - id: red
    kind: evidence
    at: base
  - id: plan
    kind: plan
    body:
      - id: build
        kind: build
      - id: integrate
        kind: integrate
  - id: review
    kind: review
  - id: correct
    loop:
      max: 2
      until: closure.passed
      spent: continue
    body:
      - id: fix
        kind: plan
        body:
          - id: rebuild
            kind: build
          - id: reintegrate
            kind: integrate
      - id: closure
        kind: closure
  - id: conclude
    kind: conclude
  - id: release
    kind: release
```

Its sha256 is `635c8150d032f47b09ad509c244e39db5d3c098f1dd18b379cc1eb3cb4c6b7ba`. An epic created before this text keeps the text it pinned, a `plan` holding a `build` alone with sha256 `8e56cd7c52ea852c4deb42ce575ba11e4e6c0e0ca4f201ff03cf24ba0afd8e1e`, as **Storage** gives.

**Canonical text.** `Load` returns the expanded workflow in canonical form, and the lowercase hex sha256 of that text (`workflow.Load`). The text is indented two spaces. Its keys come in a fixed order: `name`, then `nodes`. A node's keys come in the order `id`, then `kind`, or `loop` with `max`, `until` and `spent` (which is always written), then `body`. A check kind's settings come after `kind`, in the order `at`, `gate`, `argv`, `limit`, `needs`, `paths`, `agent`, `prompt` and `schema`, and only the settings the kind takes are written (`workflow.canonDoc`, `workflow.canonNode`, `workflow.canonSettingsOf`, `workflow.canonLoop`, `workflow.canonical`, `workflow.canonNodes`).

**Refusals.** A refusal names the file, the node and the key: `<file>:<line>: node <id>: key <key>: <reason>` (`workflow.site.refusef`). A node with no `id` is named `#<n>`, its 1-based position in its list. A refusal about the workflow as a whole has no node part. A YAML syntax error is `<file>: <error>` (`workflow.document`). The file is `.orrery/workflows/<name>.yaml` for a repository file, `built-in epic` for the built-in, and the included file for a node an include brought in (`workflow.resolve`). The refusals are these:

| Refusal | When | Code |
|---|---|---|
| `key kind: unknown kind "<kind>"` | a `kind` names no registered kind | `workflow.readKind` |
| `key kind: <kind> cannot sit <where>` | a kind sits where it may not: `build` or `integrate` outside a plan body, `plan` in a plan body, `review`, `conclude` or `release` anywhere but the top level, or `closure` anywhere but a loop body. `<where>` is `at the top level`, `in a loop body` or `in a plan body` | `workflow.readKind`, `workflow.kinds` |
| `key loop: cannot sit <where>`, `key include: cannot sit <where>` | a loop or an include in a plan body | `workflow.readLoop`, `workflow.readInclude` |
| `key kind: one of kind, loop or include is required` | a node with none of the three | `workflow.formOf` |
| `key <form>: cannot sit beside <form>` | a node with two of the three; the second is named | `workflow.formOf` |
| `key <key>: unknown key` | a key the node or its kind does not take, or a top-level key other than `name` and `nodes`. A loop setting other than `max`, `until` and `spent` is named `loop.<key>` | `workflow.checkKeys`, `workflow.readLoopFields` |
| `key <key>: required` | `name`, `nodes`, `id`, a `body`, a setting its check kind requires (`at`, `gate`, `paths`, `argv`, `limit`, `agent`, `prompt` or `schema`, as the kind needs it), `loop.max` or `loop.until` is absent | `workflow.readTop`, `workflow.idOf`, `workflow.readKindBody`, `workflow.readLoop`, `workflow.readMax`, `workflow.readUntil`, `workflow.requireKey` |
| `key at: "<value>" must be base or head` | `at` is neither `base` nor `head` | `workflow.readAt` |
| `key gate: "<value>" must match ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` | a `gate` name does not match the pattern | `workflow.snakeOf`, `workflow.readGateSettings` |
| `key agent: must not be blank`, or `key prompt: must not be blank` | `agent` or `prompt` is blank | `workflow.readText` |
| `key argv: must be a sequence of strings`, `must hold at least one string`, `item <n> must be a non-empty string`, or `item 1 "<value>" must contain a /` | `argv` is not a non-empty sequence of non-empty strings, or its first item has no `/` | `workflow.readArgv` |
| `key limit: must be an integer of 1 or more`, or `key needs.<pool>: must be an integer of 1 or more` | a `limit` or a pool count is not an integer of 1 or more | `workflow.countOf`, `workflow.readLimit`, `workflow.readNeeds` |
| `key needs: must be a mapping`, or `key needs.<pool>: "<pool>" must match ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` | `needs` is not a mapping, or a pool name does not match the pattern | `workflow.readNeeds` |
| `key paths: must be a sequence of paths`, `must hold at least one path`, `item <n> must be a single value`, or `item <n> "<value>" must be a repository-relative path` | `paths` is not a non-empty sequence of paths, or a path is empty, absolute, or has a `.` or `..` element | `workflow.readPaths`, `workflow.isRepoPath` |
| `key schema: must be a mapping` | `schema` is not a mapping | `workflow.readSchema` |
| `key loop.max: must be an integer of 1 or more` | `max` is not an integer, or is below 1 | `workflow.readMax` |
| `key loop.until: must be <node id>.<outcome>` | `until` is not a node id and an outcome joined by a dot | `workflow.readUntil` |
| `key loop.spent: must be park or continue` | `spent` is another value | `workflow.readSpent` |
| `key loop: must be a mapping` | `loop` is not a mapping | `workflow.readLoopSettings` |
| `key nodes: must be a sequence of nodes`, `must hold at least one node`, or `item <n> must be a mapping` | a list of nodes that is not a non-empty sequence of mappings; `body` is named the same way | `workflow.readList` |
| `key <key>: must be a single value`, or `key <key>: appears twice` | a value that is a sequence or a mapping, or a key given twice | `workflow.pairsOf`, `workflow.text` |
| `key <key>: "<value>" must match ^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*$` | a `name`, `id` or `include` that does not match the pattern; `<key>` is the key that holds it | `workflow.nameOf` |
| `key name: "<value>" differs from the file's name "<name>"` | `name` is not the file's name | `workflow.workflowName` |
| `key id: already used at <file>:<line>` | a node id that a node earlier in the workflow holds | `workflow.checkNodes` |
| `key loop.until: <node> has no outcome "<outcome>"` | the named child's kind does not report the outcome | `workflow.checkUntil` |
| `key loop.until: "<node>" is not a node in the loop body` | no direct child of the body has that id | `workflow.checkUntil` |
| `key include: include cycle <a> -> <b> -> <a>` | an include names a workflow the chain already holds | `workflow.expandInclude` |
| `key include: "<name>" is included more than once` | an include of a name the load has already included | `workflow.expandInclude` |
| `key include: no file .orrery/workflows/<name>.yaml and no built-in "<name>"` | an include names neither a file nor a built-in | `workflow.expandInclude` |
| `workflow "<name>": no file .orrery/workflows/<name>.yaml and no built-in "<name>"` | the workflow `CreateEpic` names is neither a file nor a built-in | `workflow.Load` |
| `workflow name "<name>" must match ^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*$` | the name `CreateEpic` names is malformed | `workflow.Load` |
| `.orrery/workflows/<name>.yaml: not a regular file (mode <mode>)` | the base holds the path as anything but a regular file, such as a symlink | `enginehost.baseReader`; `workflow.resolve` |
| `<file>: empty file: a workflow is one YAML document` | the file has no document | `workflow.document` |
| `<file>: <error>` | a YAML syntax error | `workflow.document` |
| `<file>:<line>: a workflow is one YAML document` | a second document | `workflow.document` |
| `<file>:<line>: a workflow is a mapping of name and nodes` | the document is not a mapping | `workflow.readWorkflow` |

**Pinning.** `CreateEpic` loads the workflow at the base commit it resolves (`enginehost.pinWorkflow`). Each file is read through git: `ls-tree` finds the path at that commit, and `cat-file` returns its exact bytes (`enginehost.baseReader`). The working tree is never read. A path the commit does not hold falls through to the built-in.

**Storage.** The pinned name, canonical text, sha256 and node rows are written by the create (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). No query updates them, so an edit to a workflow file, committed or not, changes neither. An epic stored before migration `00022_workflow.sql` pins `epic`.
