**Running.** `orreryd` runs every epic through the interpreter, with `--sessions` and an open store (see [orreryd](#orreryd)). Each pass reads every epic, drives the open evidence amendments of the epics it read (see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments); `interpret.Runner.passAmendments`), and loads each epic's flow. An epic with no pinned text runs the built-in `epic`. Otherwise the pinned text must hash to the pinned sha256, and it is parsed as `pinned/<name>.yaml` and checked (`interpret.loadFlow`, `interpret.parsePinned`). A flow that does not load parks its epic as an `orchestrator` park with the reason `workflow <name>: <error>`, unless the epic is parked already (`interpret.Runner.parkUnloaded`). Each decision on an epic is made from one read of its rows: `EpicFlow` reads the epic, its node runs, its pending trigger count, its pending planner action count, its node checks, its evidence runs, its agent steps, its review and the steps labeled with its review, closure and release runs in one read-only transaction, so they describe one state (`store.Store.EpicFlow`, `store.readLabeledSteps`; `interpret.Runner.decide`). The pass carries out each structural action the decision asks for through the store, then decides again from a new read, at most 16 times (`interpret.Runner.passFlow`). A read that finds the epic closed ends the pass for it with nothing carried out and no planner pass, since a close that lands while the pass carries out an action, such as a sync's prepare, has ended the plan run, and the walk would read that run as ended and start the node after it (`interpret.Runner.passFlow`, `store.IsClosedPhase`).

A node run is one instance of a workflow node on an epic, stored as described in [Node runs in the store](#node-runs-in-the-store). A top-level node's path is its id. A node in a loop body in round `r` has the path `<loop path>@<r>/<id>`, a build child of a plan run has the path `<plan path>/<build id>/T<seq>`, and a sync run of a plan run has the path `<plan path>/M<turn>` (`interpret.pass.walk`, `interpret.pass.loop`, `interpret.pass.fanOut`, `interpret.pass.syncMain`; see **Sync main**). Its id is `NodeRunID` of the epic, the path, the round and a cause: the first 32 hex digits of the lowercase SHA-256 of `orrery-workflow`, the epic, the path, the round in decimal and the cause, joined with NUL bytes. The cause is `start` for a node or loop that starts, `turn <turn> task <position>` for a build child, with the position the task's 1-based place among its turn's tasks in seq order, and `sync` for a sync run. A later plan's trigger id is `workflow:` and the id for the cause `trigger` (`store.NodeRunID`; `interpret.pass.start`, `interpret.pass.causeOf`). The same inputs give the same id, and a start whose path or trigger id is stored already changes nothing, so a restart that decides the same rows again starts nothing twice (see [Node runs in the store](#node-runs-in-the-store)). The work of a check node run has its own ids, as `NodeWorkID` gives them: the first 32 hex digits of the lowercase SHA-256 of `orrery-workflow-work`, the node run's id, its attempt and a part, joined with NUL bytes. A gate's or command's check is named `check`, an agent's step `step`, and an evidence item by its Requirement's id. An integrate or sync run's Done gate checks are named `done 1`, `done 2` and so on, with `done <i> rerun` for a rerun, and its captures `capture <R>`, with `capture <R> rerun` for a rerun. A node run starts at attempt `0`, and `epic retry ID NODE` reruns a parked node at its next attempt (`store.NodeWorkID`, `kinds.checkRun`, `kinds.Agent.Decide`, `kinds.Evidence.Decide`).

The walk takes the top-level nodes in order. A node with no run starts, and the walk stops there, so a node starts when the one before it has ended. A parked run stops the walk. Once every top-level node has ended, the pass returns no action, so the epic keeps its phase (`interpret.Decide`, `interpret.pass.walk`). A running node is decided by its kind, and a node whose kind the interpreter does not register, such as `release`, or a `review` when the interpreter has no `Reviewers` and `Ending`, gets no action: its run stays running, parks nothing, and the walk waits there (`interpret.pass.running`). A registered kind decides: an end ends its run and every running run under it, and a run hands the node on to the planner or the build (see [Planner turns](#planner-turns) and [Task builds](#task-builds); `interpret.pass.running`). A check node's kind asks for its `prepare`, `start` or `refs` work instead, which the interpreter carries out (see **Check kinds**; `interpret.Runner.work`).

A loop starts at round 1. Each round walks the loop's body, and once every node of the round has ended, the loop decides from that round. It ends when its `until` node ended with the until outcome in that round. Otherwise it moves to the next round while its rounds, `max` plus the rounds granted to it, are not spent. When they are spent, it ends if `spent` is `continue` and parks if `spent` is `park` (`interpret.pass.loop`). A park parks the loop at its round, and parks the epic with the reason `loop <id> spent its <n> rounds without <node>.<outcome>`. That is a planner park of class `loop`: it writes the trigger `park:<n>` and no tell (`store.Store.ParkLoop`).

**Plan bodies.** A plan run's body holds its `build` nodes, its `integrate` nodes and its check nodes. Its tasks start under its first `build` node, as build child runs (see [Task builds](#task-builds)). Its other body nodes, the check nodes and the `integrate` nodes, walk as a settle walk under `<plan path>/S<seq>/`, where `<seq>` is the seq of a task, so an integrate run's path is `<plan path>/S<seq>/integrate`. A settle point is the seq of a task of a turn above the plan's mark. The highest such seq starts its walk only when no task of the epic is building, open and not held `waiting` on other tasks, whose build moves only once a batch integrates them, and no late walk runs (`interpret.pass.anyTaskBuilding`, `build.Waiting`; see **Requests** under [Task builds](#task-builds)). Each walk already started, settle or late, is decided on each pass, so an older walk finishes while a newer one waits (`interpret.pass.settle`, `interpret.pass.settlePoint`, `interpret.pass.lateWalks`). A task that waited is accepted after that walk's integrate run claimed its batch, so a late walk integrates it: the body's nodes that are not build nodes, under `<plan path>/L<seq>/`, where `<seq>` is the lowest seq among the tasks of turns above the mark whose build has waited (its `after_tasks` is not empty), that are `done` and that no batch has claimed. A late walk starts once the latest walk has ended with its `integrate` node run and every late walk already started has ended, when no task is building (`interpret.pass.nextLateWalk`, `interpret.pass.lateTask`, `interpret.latePrefix`). A late walk and a settle walk therefore never run at once, so their batches never race onto the branch. A late walk that ended without its task claimed starts no other, so the trigger of its failed check brings the planner first. A plan body with no `integrate` node has no late walk. Neither has a latest walk that ended at a failed check before its `integrate` node ran: the tasks it did not integrate, and a task waiting on one of them, wait for the next turn's settle walk, which the failed check's trigger asks the planner for, and a plan whose next turn plans no task stays running while that task waits. The plan ends only once its kind ends it, its latest walk and its late walks have ended and no sync of origin's main holds it (`interpret.pass.plan`; see **Sync main**), and, where its body holds an `integrate` node, as **Built or park** below gives. A failed check, or a failed batch, ends its walk and writes its trigger, and the plan does not end while a trigger is pending, so the planner plans again first (`kinds.planComplete`).

**Fan-out hold.** While an integrate run under the plan is running or parked, the fan-out starts no build child, so no task starts until its batch is green or red. Build children already running are still decided (`interpret.pass.fanOut`, `interpret.pass.batchOpen`).

**Batch integration.** An integrate run takes the accepted tasks that no run has claimed, composes them into a candidate, runs the epic's Done gates and captures at the candidate, reproduces each test item at the epic's base, and then advances the epic's branch to the candidate when it is green. Its work runs in the order below (`kinds.Integrate.Decide`, `interpret.Runner.work`).

- **Batch.** The batch is the tasks the run has claimed, or else every task that is `done` with no batch, in seq order (`kinds.batchOf`). A paused or parked epic prepares no batch (`kinds.prepare`). A batch with no task ends the run `passed` at once, with the output `no accepted task to integrate`, and with no revision (`kinds.prepareBatch`).
- **Compose.** The batch is composed on the epic's candidate when it has one, and on its stored base otherwise (`interpret.Runner.prepareBatch`, `compose.Compose`). The tasks are taken in seq order. A task's changed paths are the paths that differ between the commit it was built on and its revision. A task built on another base than that commit is composed only when its base is an ancestor of the batch base and none of its changed paths equals, contains or lies under a path changed between its base and the batch base, so laying its paths over the batch base keeps every change the batch base made, as a task built on an earlier stored base is after a later batch integrated alone. Any other task built on another base, and one whose base names no commit, is left out, with the reason `T<seq> was built on <base>, not on the batch base <base>` (`compose.taskBase`, `compose.baseCovers`, `compose.isAncestor`). A task whose changed paths equal, contain or lie under a path that an earlier composed task changed is left out, with the reason `T<seq> overlaps T<k> at <path>` (`compose.composeTasks`, `compose.overlapReason`, `compose.pathsOverlap`). Each other task becomes one commit on the previous one, the first on the base, with its revision's author, committer, dates and message, so the same inputs give the same commits. The candidate is the last commit, or the base when none composed. With any composed, the candidate is pinned create-only at `refs/orrery/<slug>/candidate/<run id>-<attempt>`: a ref already at the candidate passes, and a ref at another commit fails the compose (`compose.pinCandidate`, `interpret.candidateRef`). Nothing reads or writes the repository's working tree or index (`compose.Compose`, `compose.composeTask`).
- **Claim.** The run claims its batch in one write. Its revision becomes the candidate, its refs are snapshotted, and its result is the compose result as JSON, `{"base", "candidate", "composed": [{"task", "commit"}], "leftOut": [{"task", "reason"}]}` (`store.Store.ClaimBatch`, `interpret.encodeBatch`). Every task of the batch is claimed with the run's path, whether it composed or was left out (`store.claimBatchTasks`). A left-out task therefore stays claimed by that run, and no later batch takes it, since a batch takes only the tasks it claims and the `done` tasks with no batch (`kinds.batchOf`). A retry of the same run takes the same tasks again.
- **Nothing composed.** A batch in which nothing composed ends `failed` at once, with its left-out reasons as its output. It runs no Done gate and no capture, and takes no refs compare (`kinds.Integrate.Decide`).
- **Done gates.** Otherwise each Done gate of the epic (`done_gates`) runs in order as a check at the candidate, under the id `NodeWorkID` gives for `done <i>` (`kinds.gateCheck`, `interpret.Runner.startCheck`). A gate's check starts only once the gate before it has passed. A check that did not run, timed out or exited non-zero makes the batch red: the later gates do not start, and no capture does (`kinds.doneGates`, `kinds.checkPassed`). A check whose reason is `lost at a restart` reruns once, under `done <i> rerun`. A lost rerun parks the run and the epic with `integrate <path>: Done gate <gate> lost at a restart twice` (`kinds.gateCheck`, `interpret.pass.running`).
- **Captures.** With every Done gate passed, each Requirement that names an evidence item is captured at the candidate in the green phase, under the id `NodeWorkID` gives for `capture <R>` (`kinds.captures`, `interpret.Runner.capture`, `interpret.Runner.runItems`). The captures that have no run start together, and any still running makes the run wait. A capture lost at a restart reruns once, under `capture <R> rerun`. A lost rerun parks the run and the epic with `integrate <path>: capture <R> lost at a restart twice` (`kinds.captureFor`).
- **Reproductions.** Once every capture has ended, each Requirement that names a test item is reproduced under the id `NodeWorkID` gives for `repro <R>` (`kinds.reproductions`, `kinds.reproCheck`, `interpret.Runner.repro`). The reproduction is the epic's base with the item's test files taken from the candidate, pinned create-only at `refs/orrery/<slug>/repro/<work id>`, and the item's capture runs there as a check, so its exit is what red is compared with (`compose.Overlay`, `interpret.Runner.repro`). The reproductions that have no check start together, and any still running makes the run wait. A reproduction lost at a restart reruns once, under `repro <R> rerun`. A lost rerun parks the run and the epic with `integrate <path>: reproduction <R> lost at a restart twice` (`kinds.reproCheck`, `kinds.reproductions`). A reproduction adds no cause to the verdict (`kinds.reproductions`).
- **Verdict.** Once the Done gates, captures and reproductions have settled, the run's refs are compared as **Ref compare** gives, whatever the result (`interpret.Runner.checkRefs`, `kinds.settle`). The batch is red when a Done gate failed, when a capture did not run or has no verdict, or when a Requirement that was green at the stored base has a capture that no longer passes. A capture that ran and found its Requirement not met is not red by itself, and its line reads `<R> not yet green`. A red batch ends `failed` with one line per cause, then the left-out lines. The causes are `Done gate <gate> failed`, `<R> capture failed` and `<R> regressed from green at the stored base <base>` (`kinds.captureCauses`, `kinds.outputOf`).
- **Advance.** A batch that is not red advances the epic's branch from the stored base to the candidate, expecting the branch to be at the stored base (`compose.Advance`, `interpret.Runner.advance`). A branch already at the candidate is left as it is, which is the state a restart leaves when it cut an advance, and the run ends `passed`. A branch at another commit parks the run and the epic with `epic/<slug> moved: at <tip>, not the stored base <base>`. A branch checked out in a worktree parks them with `epic/<slug> is checked out in a worktree, so it was not moved` (`interpret.advanceBlock`, `interpret.Runner.parkBatch`). A branch at the stored base that the candidate does not build on, as when a newer batch advanced it while this one ran, parks them with `epic/<slug> is at <tip>, which the candidate <candidate> does not build on, so it was not moved`, and `compose.Advance` itself moves the branch only as a fast-forward from the commit it expects (`interpret.advanceBlock`, `compose.Advance`). Otherwise the run ends `passed`, with one line per Requirement, `<R> green` or `<R> not yet green`, then the left-out lines (`kinds.requirementLines`).
- **Batch writes.** Ending the run is one write. `EndBatch` ends it with its outcome and output, and adds its trigger (`store.Store.EndBatch`). A passed batch moves the stored base to its candidate and clears the candidate. A failed batch that composed a task, one whose result's `composed` list is not empty, sets the candidate to its revision, unless the revision is the stored base. A failed batch that composed no task sets no candidate, whatever its revision: its revision is its batch base, which is the candidate already or a stored base the epic has held, and a sync of origin's main may have moved the stored base past it before a retry ended the batch `failed` again, where a candidate set to it would leave the epic's next batch composed behind the branch (`store.settleBatchBase`, `store.composedAny`). A batch with a revision adds its trigger, `workflow:` with a derived id, pending, with the cause `workflow node <path> <passed|failed>: <output>`, so the planner plans the epic again. A batch with no revision, which is an empty one, writes no trigger (`interpret.pass.batch`, `interpret.pass.batchTrigger`). A parked batch writes no trigger, since its park adds the reason as a tell (see **Pause and park** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)).
- **Retry.** `RetryNode` reruns a parked integrate run at its next attempt. Its revision, refs, result and end are cleared, so it composes again on the candidate or the stored base, and its candidate ref is named for the new attempt (`store.Store.RetryNode`, `interpret.candidateRef`). An integrate run that ended `failed` is rerun the same way when it is the latest integrate run of its node that moved something, in the newest settle walk of the plan. The newest walk, a settle walk or a late walk, is the one whose run the store wrote last, which is the one that started last, since a run is written when it starts (`store.latestIntegrateRun`, `store.settleSeq`; `internal/store/queries.sql: ListEpicIntegrateRunPaths`). A run that ended `passed` with no revision moved nothing, so it does not count (`store.latestIntegrateRun`; see **Controls** under [Epics](#epics)). Its outcome, output, refs checked and end are cleared, and its revision, refs and result are kept, so its Done gates, captures and reproductions walk again under the new attempt on the same candidate (`kinds.Integrate.Decide`). A pass that passes advances the branch and moves the stored base as a first pass does, and a rerun that fails ends `failed` again (`store.retryFailedIntegrate`, `store.Store.EndBatch`, `interpret.Runner.advance`). A running or passed latest run, and an earlier failed run that a later run superseded, are refused and change nothing.

**Sync main.** A running plan whose body holds an `integrate` node merges origin's main into the epic's stored base at a quiet point, so the epic picks up origin's main while it runs and the planner's next turn plans from it (`interpret.pass.syncMain`, `kinds.Sync`). The sync is no node of the workflow. The interpreter adds its run under the plan run, so a pinned text and its sha256 are the same with it, and an interpreter that registers no `sync` kind starts none (`interpret.New`). Its work runs in the order below.

- **Quiet point.** Once the plan run's latest walk and its late walks have ended and no late walk is left to start, a sync is due when the epic is at a quiet point: a batch under the plan run has passed with a revision, the epic is in no phase it ends or is closed in (`kinds.Ended`), it is neither paused nor parked, whatever its park's class, its review has not frozen, it does not hold its repository's release slot, and its work is at rest. Its work is at rest when it has no candidate, no planner turn runs, no action a planner turn returned is pending, no evidence amendment is checking or capturing, no task is open, no task is `done` with no batch, and no integrate or release run is running or parked (`interpret.pass.quiet`, `kinds.Busy`). A pending action may rerun a node, fail a task, resume a release, amend a Requirement's evidence or close the epic once the planner's pass carries it out, so no sync is due before it has been (see **Actions** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)). An amendment under way decides a Requirement's item, and a capturing one its green at the stored base it captures at, which a sync would move, so no sync is due before it has settled (see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments)). An amendment whose red passes while a sync runs at its merge waits for the sync to end, as it waits for an integrate run (`store.applyAmendment`). The review compares revisions with the one it froze at, so no sync is due once it has frozen.
- **Key.** A sync is due at most once for each turn the planner ran. Its key is the highest seq among the epic's turns that did not end `free`, and a run stored at `<plan path>/M<key>` leaves no sync due, so a restart starts none twice (`interpret.pass.syncTurn`, `interpret.syncPath`). A turn ends `free` when its start was refused, as a full step cap refuses it with `step cap full` (see **Refusals at the start** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)), or when its step ended `engine_restarted` or `stopped` (see **Ends** there; `store.Store.FreeTurn`). A freed turn returns its triggers to pending, so the planner claims another turn at its next pass, and a key of every turn would make a sync due, with a run and a read of origin, on every pass while the cap stays full. A turn that ended `failed` ran, and keys a sync of its own.
- **Read.** A due sync starts from the epic's last read of origin's main, which the epic stores: the stored base it was read for, origin's commit it found, what it found, and when (`store.OriginRead`; see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). A read stands for 5 minutes, by the runner's clock, for the stored base it was read for (`interpret.syncInterval`). When there is no read, or it was read for another stored base, at least 5 minutes ago or at a time later than the runner's clock, which a clock set back leaves, the interpreter reads origin's main again before anything else is decided for the sync, and that read holds the plan as a sync does (`interpret.pass.originStale`, `interpret.Runner.readOrigin`). The read runs `git remote`, and for a repository with a remote `origin` then `git ls-remote --symref origin HEAD`, which fetches nothing, for at most 20 seconds (`interpret.Runner.lookAtOrigin`, `interpret.Runner.originHead`, `interpret.syncReadLimit`, `interpret.lsRemoteHead`). It finds `none` for a repository with no remote `origin`, `held` for an origin commit the stored base holds, `apart` for one that does not hold the epic's change base, whose sync would end skipped (see **Prepare** below), and `ahead` for any other, a commit the repository does not have among them, since the stored base holds only commits the repository has (`interpret.originState`, `interpret.hasCommit`). A read that fails, git the 20 seconds stopped among it, finds `failed` with no commit, and is logged as a warning, `origin's main was not read`. The read is stored in one write at the runner's clock (`store.Store.RecordOriginRead`). A read whose pass's context ended, as when `orreryd` stops, stores nothing. A sync run starts only from a read that stands, found `ahead`, and names an origin commit no sync run of the epic tried (see **Tried once**; `kinds.SyncTriedBy`). Any other read starts nothing and stores no run, and the plan gets its run in the same pass, so a quiet point at which origin's main has not moved leaves no row. Origin's main is read so at most once in 5 minutes for one stored base, a failed read among it, and once more each time a batch or a sync moves the stored base, so an origin that cannot be reached holds the interpreter's pass for at most 20 seconds in each 5 minutes for one stored base. A read is stored and measured by the runner's clock, so a restart keeps it, and one that stands still stands after the restart. The run's path is `<plan path>/M<key>`, its node is `sync` and its kind `sync`, and its id is `NodeRunID` of that path with the cause `sync` (`interpret.syncPath`).
- **Hold.** While a read or a sync is due, or the sync's run under the plan is running, the plan gets no run, so the interpreter does not pass the planner for the epic, and the plan does not end. The trigger the passed batch wrote stays pending, and the planner plans once the sync has ended, or once the read found no sync to start, from the stored base the sync left (`interpret.pass.plan`, `interpret.Runner.Pass`). A plan whose kind would end it syncs at that quiet point first, so the plan ends at what the sync left. A sync moves no task's worktree: it starts only while no task is open, and the planner gets no turn to plan one while it runs. The hold withholds only the epic's turn. Each planner pass carries out every epic's pending actions before it decides any, the held epic's among them, so the hold waits on no action, and an action, which the next pass carries out, never waits on the hold (`planner.Runner.pass`, `planner.Runner.carryOutActions`). Nor is a planner park held: the interpreter names a planner-parked epic for the planner's pass whatever its plan run gets (`interpret.Runner.Pass`), and a sync starts on no parked epic and parks nothing.
- **Prepare.** The prepare waits while the epic is paused or parked (`kinds.Sync.Decide`, `kinds.prepare`). A repository with no remote `origin` reads nothing more (`interpret.hasOrigin`). Otherwise origin's default branch is read with `git ls-remote` for at most 20 seconds, as the read reads it, and fetched for at most 1 minute, as a release fetches it, into `refs/orrery/<slug>/sync/origin` (`interpret.Runner.fetchMain`, `interpret.syncFetchLimit`, `interpret.fetchBranch`, `interpret.remoteOutput`). The prepare runs on the interpreter's pass, so the git that reads origin runs for at most its limit, and the fetch runs only once a read found origin's main ahead of the stored base. The local git after it, the ancestry, the merge and the pin, runs as long as it takes, as the rest of the interpreter's local git does. Git that reads origin runs in a process group of its own, which the end of its limit kills whole, with the transport git started, and it is waited for at most 2 seconds after that (`interpret.remoteKillGrace`, `interpret.stoppedAt`). An origin commit the stored base holds is noted. So is one that does not hold the epic's change base (see **After a sync** below; `interpret.changeBase`), with that change base recorded, since the change from that commit would hold commits of the change base beside the epic's own: the epic's base may be a local commit origin never had, or origin's main may have been forced past the commit the epic last synced. So is one an earlier sync run of the epic tried, which the prepare's action names with that run's path (`interpret.Runner.mergeMain`, `kinds.syncTries`). Any other origin commit is merged into the stored base with `git merge-tree --write-tree`, without a checkout. A merge that does not conflict is committed with `git commit-tree`, which honors `commit.gpgSign`, with the stored base as its first parent and origin's commit as its second, and pinned at `refs/orrery/<slug>/sync/<origin commit>` (`interpret.commitSync`, `build.Pin`). The prepare records its result, `{error, noOrigin, branch, origin, base, contained, changeBase, triedBy, conflicts, merge}` with each empty key but `base` left out, the merge as the run's revision, and its own read of origin's main as the epic's last read, in one write, once (`kinds.SyncResult`, `store.Store.PrepareSync`). Its read is stamped at the runner's clock and finds `none`, `failed`, `held` or `apart` as the result does, and `taken` for an origin commit the prepare merged, found conflicting or found tried, so the read the sync started from starts no second sync while the prepare's stands (`interpret.preparedRead`). What kept the prepare from reading or merging origin's main, such as a fetch that failed, is its `error`, and it is logged as a warning, `sync of origin's main did not run` (`interpret.Runner.prepareSync`). Git that a limit stopped makes the error `git did not finish within 20s: <git's error>` for the `ls-remote` and `git did not finish within 1m0s: <git's error>` for the fetch. A prepare whose pass's context ended records nothing, and the next pass prepares the run again.
- **Tried once.** A sync run tried an origin commit when it decided it: its merge conflicted, or a Done gate or a capture failed at its merge, each of which ends it `failed`, or its merge passed every check and advanced, which ends it `passed` (`kinds.triedOrigin`, `kinds.syncTries`). A run that ended `skipped` tried nothing, whatever stopped it, and nor did a run a close ended, which has no outcome, or a run still running. An origin commit that a sync run of the epic tried is never merged again: a read that names it starts no run, and a prepare that finds it ends `skipped` as tried. So a sync that conflicted or failed is tried again only once origin's main has moved, and an origin commit a sync skipped is merged again at a later quiet point, at the next key whose read finds it ahead, which is once the stored base has moved or the read the skipped sync's prepare stored is 5 minutes old. The runs are stored, so a restart keeps what was tried.
- **Ends with no merge.** A sync whose prepare made no merge ends at once (`kinds.syncUnmerged`). It ends `skipped` with `the repository has no remote origin`, `the sync did not run: <error>`, `origin's commit <origin> is in the stored base <base>`, `the epic's change base <change base> is not in origin's commit <origin>`, `origin's commit <origin> was tried by <path>` or `the sync result names no merge`, and with `sync result is not a JSON object: <error>` when its result does not decode (`kinds.Sync.Decide`, `kinds.ParseSyncResult`). A merge that conflicts ends it `failed` with `merge of origin's commit <origin> into the stored base <base> conflicts in <paths>`, with each conflicted path once, joined with `, `.
- **Checks.** At the merge the run walks the epic's Done gates, then the captures of its Requirements in the green phase, then the reproductions of its test items, as an integrate run walks them, under the ids `NodeWorkID` gives for `done <i>`, `capture <R>` and `repro <R>` of the sync run, each with its reruns (`kinds.syncChecks`, `kinds.doneGates`, `kinds.captures`, `kinds.reproductions`). It takes no ref compare. A Done gate that does not pass ends the run `failed`, and so does a capture that did not run or has no verdict, or whose Requirement was green at the stored base and does not pass at the merge, with the causes an integrate run gives (`kinds.captureCauses`). A failed run's output is `merge <merge> of origin's commit <origin> into the stored base <base>`, then one line per cause (`kinds.SyncResult.MergeLine`). A check lost at a restart twice, or whose last rerun exits `store.EnvironmentExit`, decides nothing of the merge, so it ends the run `skipped`, its output the merge's line and then the park reason of that walk, which opens with `sync <path>:` since a sync parks nothing (`kinds.syncWait`, `kinds.walkName`). That reason is `sync <path>: Done gate <gate> lost at a restart twice`, `sync <path>: Done gate <gate> exited 75 on its last run: an environment result, not a verdict`, `sync <path>: capture <R> lost at a restart twice`, `sync <path>: capture <R> exited 75 on its last run: an environment result, not a verdict` or `sync <path>: reproduction <R> lost at a restart twice`.
- **Work that fails.** The sync's prepare, check starts, captures, reproductions and advance are its work (`interpret.Runner.syncWork`). A start of a Done gate's check, of the captures or of the reproductions that fails ends the run `skipped` in the same pass, its output the merge's line and then `not finished: <error>`, and logs the error as a warning, `sync of origin's main did not finish` (`interpret.Runner.syncUnfinished`). So does an advance whose git or read failed, which has moved no branch (see **Advance**). Such an end tells nothing, and it is the run's one end, written once, so the run is told at most once whatever its work did before. It ends the hold, so the planner gets its turn in that pass rather than waiting on work that fails on every pass, and since it is skipped, a later quiet point merges the same origin commit again (see **Tried once**). Work whose pass's context ended leaves the run running, and the next pass carries it out again. The prepare's failures are its result (see **Prepare**), and a prepare or an end whose write failed is logged and made again on the next pass.
- **Advance.** Once every check has passed, the run's advance carries why the epic is no longer at the quiet point the sync started at, if it is not: the stored base is not the one the merge was made on, the epic is paused, parked, whatever its park's class, or in a phase it ends or is closed in, or its work is not at rest (`kinds.syncChecks`, `kinds.SyncMoved`, `kinds.Busy`). The why is `the stored base moved from <base> to <stored base>`, `the epic is paused`, `the epic is parked`, `the epic's phase is <phase>`, or what `kinds.Busy` gives: `the epic has the candidate <candidate>`, `planner turn <n> is running`, `a planner action is pending`, `<n> planner actions are pending`, `evidence amendment A<seq> is <checking|capturing>`, `task T<n> is open`, `task T<n> is accepted and no batch has claimed it`, or `<integrate|release> run <path> is <running|parked>`. The advance reads the epic's branch first (`interpret.Runner.syncEnd`). A branch already at the merge while the stored base is still the merge's first parent, as a restart that cut the advance after it moved the branch leaves it, is left as it is, and the run ends `passed` whatever the advance carries, so its end moves the stored base to the branch. Failing that, an advance that carries a why moves nothing, and the run ends `skipped`, its output the merge's line and then `not advanced: <why>`, since an advance then would leave a task or a batch made on the stored base behind the branch, or move the branch of an epic its owner holds. A branch the advance may not move, as **Advance** under **Batch integration** gives it, ends the run `skipped` too, its output the merge's line and then `not advanced: <reason>`, where the reason is `epic/<slug> moved: at <tip>, not the stored base <base>`, `epic/<slug> is at <tip>, which the candidate <merge> does not build on, so it was not moved` or `epic/<slug> is checked out in a worktree, so it was not moved`, and the epic does not park (`interpret.advanceBlock`, `interpret.notAdvanced`). Just before it moves the branch, the advance reads the epic's rows again, and a why above that holds then ends the run `skipped` with `not advanced: <why>`, so a pause, a park, a close or work that lands after the pass read the rows does not move the branch (`interpret.Runner.syncHalt`). Otherwise the epic's branch moves from the stored base to the merge, with the compare-and-swap, fast-forward-only advance a batch makes (`compose.Advance`). The run that moves the branch ends `passed`, with the output `merged origin's commit <origin> into the stored base <base> as <merge>` and then one line per Requirement, `<R> green` or `<R> not yet green`. An advance whose end's write fails is logged and left to the next pass, which finds the branch at the merge and ends the run `passed` (`interpret.Runner.advanceSync`).
- **Sync writes.** Ending the run is one write (`store.Store.EndSync`). A passed sync moves the stored base to its merge, clears the candidate and stores origin's commit as the epic's `synced`. A `failed` sync, a `skipped` one whose check could not be decided, and a `skipped` one whose branch the advance may not move add the tell `workflow node <path> <failed|skipped>: <output>`, cut to `TurnReasonMax` bytes, which is routed as a notice of its own (see **Tells** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns); `interpret.syncTell`, `kinds.Action`). Every other skipped sync tells nothing, so a repository with no origin, a fetch that fails, an advance not taken and work that failed are never told. No end writes a trigger and none parks, so a sync that fails or skips is no park of any class and no failed node the planner is woken for (see **Park classes** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)).
- **Retry and close.** A sync run is never parked and is no integrate run, so `RetryNode` of the node `sync`, the owner's `epic retry ID sync` and the planner's `retry_node` alike, reruns no sync run. It is refused with `node sync of epic <id> is not parked`, unless a workflow node of that id has a parked run or a latest integrate run that failed, which it reruns as for any node: the integrate runs a retry reads are read by kind (`store.rerunNodeIn`, `store.retryFailedIntegrate`; `internal/store/queries.sql: ListEpicIntegrateRunPaths`). A failed batch that composed a task keeps its candidate, so no sync starts between it and a retry that reruns it on that candidate. One that composed none leaves no candidate, so a sync may move the stored base past its batch base, and its rerun ends `failed` again at once at that old base and sets no candidate, so the next batch composes on the stored base the sync left and no sync is held back by a candidate behind it (`kinds.Integrate.Decide`, `store.settleBatchBase`; see **Batch writes**). A close ends a running sync run with the epic's other running runs. A prepare or an end the pass writes after the close changes nothing, since each writes a running run only, and the pass carries out nothing more on the closed epic (see **Running**; `store.Store.PrepareSync`, `store.Store.EndSync`). A read of origin's main the pass stores after the close changes the epic's stored read alone, which nothing reads once the epic is closed (`store.Store.RecordOriginRead`).
- **After a sync.** A passed sync's captures and reproductions at its merge count as a passed batch's do at the stored base (`kinds.GreenAt`, `kinds.Unbuilt`, `kinds.Disagreement`), so a later batch is red when it regresses a Requirement the sync found green, and the plan ends built at the merge. The epic's own change is read from its change base: the origin commit its stored base last merged, its `synced`, or its base when no sync has passed (`interpret.changeBase`). A sync merges only an origin commit that holds the change base before it, so the change from the change base is the epic's own and holds none of origin's commits. The review's reviewers and verifiers and an `owned` node read the change from it, so origin's commits are not taken for the epic's (see **Check kinds** and **Freeze.**). A release merges origin's main as before, and finds origin's commit already in the tip unless origin's main has moved since the last sync (see **Candidate (R2).** under [Release](#release)).

**Built or park.** Where a plan body holds an `integrate` node and the plan would end complete, the plan ends `built` when `kinds.Unbuilt` lists nothing, and otherwise the epic parks (`interpret.pass.complete`). A `built` end ends the plan run and each running run under it, and sets the epic's phase to `built` when the phase is `accepted`. Any other phase stays, so a fix plan that ends built in `correcting` leaves the phase `correcting` (`store.Store.EndPlanBuilt`, `store.endAndBuild`). Otherwise the epic parks with the reason `epic not built: <missing>`, where the lines of `kinds.Unbuilt` are joined with `; `. Each line is `R<n> names no evidence item`, `R<n> has no red at the epic's base <base>` or `R<n> is not green at the stored base <stored base>`, in contract order, or, for a test item that is green at the stored base, one of two reproduction lines. The first is `R<n> judge and exit disagree at the epic's base <base>: red was judged fails, but its reproduction with the candidate's tests exited 0`, when at least one reproduction of the item at the stored base ran and none of those that ran timed out or exited non-zero. The second is `R<n> has no reproduction at the epic's base <base>`, when none of the item's reproductions at the stored base ran. When one was started and did not run, the line ends with `: <reason>`, the reason the first one gave (`kinds.Unbuilt`, `kinds.Disagreement`, `kinds.noReproduction`). A Requirement whose item an evidence amendment applied also takes its proof from that amendment: the amendment's red at the epic's base counts as its red there, its green at the stored base counts as green when that is still the stored base, and its reproduction counts beside those of the integrate runs. Those integrate runs' reproductions count for such a Requirement only when the run's capture of it is current, since a stale capture's reproduction ran the capture of the item the amendment replaced (`kinds.amendment`, `kinds.amendedRed`, `kinds.amendedGreen`, `kinds.GreenAt`, `kinds.baseReproductions`; see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments)). The park is a planner park of class `not_built`. It writes the trigger `workflow:` with a derived id, pending, with the same text as its cause, and adds no tell (`store.Store.ParkEpicWaking`). The derived id comes from the highest turn seq, so a repeat with the same highest turn changes nothing. The planner claims that trigger on its next turn, since a planner park does not stop it (see [Planner turns](#planner-turns)). The park is the ordinary park. It sets no phase, so the epic is never `met_at_base` by it, and only the owner's `end` answer to a **Met at base** question below sets that phase (`store.Store.ParkEpicWaking`).

**Met at base.** A top-level `evidence` node at `base` that ends `failed` with a Requirement whose red run was judged `holds` parks: the run and the epic park, and the epic's phase does not change (`interpret.pass.failed`, `kinds.MetAtBase`, `store.Store.ParkNodeMetAtBase`). `interpret.pass.failed` returns an ordinary node park that names those Requirements, and the runner writes it with `store.Store.ParkNodeMetAtBase` in one write. That write also opens the park question below and keeps the parked run's path and attempt, and those Requirements, under it, so answering the question reads nothing else (`store.parkMetAtBase`; `internal/store/queries.sql: InsertEpicMetAtBase`). Its reason is `workflow node <path>: <ids> met at the epic's base <base>; you can retry (run the red check of <ids> again with a fresh judge), drop (drop <ids> as already met, recorded as your decision, and go on without their red) or end (end the epic)`, where `<ids>` are those Requirements in contract order, joined with `, `. The question's choices are `retry`, `drop` and `end`, each doing what that reason says, and it recommends `retry`, with why "the judge read the conclusion as already holding at the base, and a fresh judge checks that reading before anything is dropped" and confidence `medium` (`store.metAtBaseQuestion`). The owner's answer does one of these in the answer's write (`store.answerMetAtBase`):
- `retry` reruns the parked run at its next attempt, with its work cleared, and ends the park, as `RetryNode` does. Each Requirement then gets a new red run under a new id, judged by a new step.
- `drop` marks each Requirement the question names as dropped by the answer's decision, which is appended as every answer is. It then reruns the parked run at its next attempt and ends the park (`store.dropMetAtBase`; `internal/store/queries.sql: DropEpicRequirement`). The mark is `dropped_by` on the Requirement, the seq of that decision. `kinds.Evidence.Decide` starts no red run for a dropped Requirement and ends on the others, so the evidence node passes on the rest and the walk goes on, and `kinds.MetAtBase` skips it. `kinds.Unbuilt` still requires its green at the stored base, and names neither its missing red at the base nor a reproduction line for it. Integrate still captures its green, and it still starts a reproduction of a dropped test item, which adds no line.
- `end` sets the epic's phase to `met_at_base` and leaves the park in place, so the epic stays parked. `RetryEpic` and `RetryNode` then refuse it as **Controls** under [Epics](#epics) gives (`store.setEpicPhase`, `store.metAtBaseRefusal`).

An answer of `other` ends the park, as an answer to any park question that is not final does, and leaves the parked run where it is (see **Park questions**).

The interpreter adds no pause or park rule of its own, except that a check's `prepare` waits while the epic is paused or parked (see **Check kinds**), and a stopped review step pauses the epic once (see **End-of-epic review**). A paused or parked epic's node runs start and end as usual, and the planner and the build keep their own rules (`planner.Decide`; see [Planner turns](#planner-turns) and [Task builds](#task-builds)).

**Restart of check nodes.** Before the interpreter's first pass, `orreryd` takes up the open checks (`cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startNodeChecks`, `cmd/orreryd.startSessions`; `enginehost.NodeChecks.Resume`). A check stored with a grant re-adopts its gate under that grant, and ends `failed` with `lost at a restart` when the gate cannot be re-adopted (`enginehost.NodeChecks.readoptGranted`, `enginehost.NodeChecks.readoptLost`). A check with no grant runs from the start, after its worktree is removed. A check cut before its gate launched is left running for the next start (`enginehost.NodeChecks.readoptUnlaunched`, `enginehost.NodeChecks.notStarted`). The writes of a check's grant and of its end, and of an evidence run's grant, capture, judge and verdict, are tried again 1 second after each failure, a busy store among the causes, until they land, since nothing drives a check or run whose write is lost (`enginehost.retryStore`, `enginehost.NodeChecks.record`, `enginehost.Evidence.recordCapture`). Only shutdown stops the retries. A check whose end is still unstored then stays running, and the next start ends it `lost at a restart`, since its holder is cleared, so it reruns as a lost check does. `Resume` re-reads each check before driving it, so a check that a drive ended after `Resume` listed is left alone (`enginehost.NodeChecks.Resume`, `enginehost.NodeChecks.readoptStart`). A gate that detaches under `--holder-scope` keeps running under its holder and records nothing, so the next start readopts it. An agent node's step is re-adopted when its holder is live, as any step is. Otherwise the step ends `engine_restarted` (`enginehost.Steps.Readopt`, `enginehost.Steps.Reconcile`, `enginehost.Steps.endRestarted`), and its node fails with that end as its output, so it parks as a failed top-level check does. The writes of a node run's work and outcome each change only a run still in the state they need, so a repeat changes nothing: a repeated `Run` returns the stored check, and `PrepareNode`, `RecordNodeResult`, `CheckNodeRefs`, `ParkNode` and `EndNodesWaking` each skip a run that is past their step (`enginehost.NodeChecks.Run`, `store.Store.PrepareNode`, `store.Store.RecordNodeResult`, `store.Store.CheckNodeRefs`, `store.Store.ParkNode`, `store.Store.EndNodesWaking`). An integrate run's Done gate and capture checks restart the same way, and a lost one reruns once and then parks (see **Batch integration**). A sync run's checks restart the same way too, and a lost one reruns once and then ends the sync `skipped`, told (see **Sync main**).

**End-of-epic review.** The `review`, `closure` and `conclude` kinds are registered only when the interpreter's `Config` has both `Reviewers` and `Ending` (`interpret.New`, `interpret.Config`). `orreryd` passes `interpret.NewEpicReviewers` as `Reviewers` and the store as `Ending` (`cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startFlow`), and `store.Store.EndEpic` satisfies `interpret.Ending`. Without both, a `review` node starts and waits, so no epic ends clean (`interpret.pass.running`).

**Freeze.** A review run with no revision is prepared while its epic is neither paused nor parked (`kinds.Review.Decide`, `kinds.prepare`). Its preparation freezes the review at the epic's stored base (`interpret.storedBaseOf`, `interpret.Runner.prepareReview`). The run takes that revision, the review stores it as `frozen`, and the phase becomes `reviewing` (`store.Store.FreezeReview`). The frozen revision is stored once, so a repeat changes nothing (`store.frozenRevision`). Reviewers run at the frozen revision over the change from the epic's change base, and verifiers run over the change base to it, so a commit that lands after the freeze is not reviewed (`interpret.EpicReviewers.Start`, `interpret.Runner.reviewUnitStart`, `interpret.epicVerifierStart`). The change base is the epic's base, or, once a sync of origin's main has passed, the origin commit the stored base last merged, so origin's commits are not reviewed as the epic's (see **Sync main**; `interpret.changeBase`). A reviewer's prompt names the epic, each Requirement, each Must Not Ship entry, the epic's evidence amendments when it has any (see **Seen by** under [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments)), and the change from the change base to the frozen revision (`interpret.epicStageReviewPrompt`). The `test_parsimony` reviewer's prompt then ends with its Test cost section, read from the Done gates that ran at the frozen revision: the ended checks that the newest node run with checks at that revision ran there, the integrate or sync run that made it the stored base, in the order they were created. A check lost at a restart, and one that exited 75, which its rerun replaces, are left out (`interpret.Runner.doneGateChecks`). See **Test cost** under [Reviewers](#reviewers).

**Parts and tries.** The review has one part for each enabled epic-stage reviewer, `reviewer <n>`, where `n` is the reviewer's 1-based position among the epic's enabled epic-stage reviewers, in merged order; a disabled or task-stage reviewer takes no position (`reviewers.ForStage`). It has one part for each finding of a reviewer with verify on, `verify <n> <k>`, where `n` is that reviewer's position and `k` is the finding's 1-based place among its findings (`interpret.EpicReviewers.Epic`, `kinds.reviewPass.settleReviewer`, `kinds.reviewPass.settleFinding`). A part's tries run in order: try 1 has the part's id, and try `t` has `<part> try <t>`, each id made by `store.NodeWorkID` from the run and its attempt (`kinds.reviewTryID`, `kinds.walkTries`). The walk starts the first try with no step and waits while a try runs. The first try that gives a verdict settles the part (`kinds.walkTries`). A start that the step cap refuses starts nothing more in that pass, and it is no error (`interpret.Runner.startReviewUnits`, `enginehost.ErrCapReached`). No try starts while the epic is paused or parked (`kinds.Review.Decide`).

**Ends that cost nothing or cost one.** An `engine_restarted` end is free, and the next try starts. A `stopped` end is free once its step is in the review's holds. A `stopped` end whose step is not held yet is a hold: `store.Store.HoldReviewStep` stores it, pauses the epic as its owner's and adds the tell `<part> stopped: <step reason>`, once per step (`kinds.walkTries`, `kinds.reviewPass.pending`, `interpret.Runner.carryReview`, `store.Store.HoldReviewStep`). A hold is decided even while the epic is paused or parked. Any other end without a verdict costs one try, and the second costly end settles the part as a gap with the reason `<part> gave no verdict twice: <end>: <reason>` (`kinds.walkTries`).

**Verdicts.** A reviewer's step gives `holds` when it ends `done`. A `gate_failed` end gives `fails`, with its findings, when its return is a gate return with status `done`, no assertion `undetermined` and at least one finding (`kinds.reviewIsReviewerVerdict`, `kinds.reviewFailedFindings`). A verifier's or a closure verifier's step gives the result of the first result of its return when it ends `done`, and the evidence of that result is the reason (`kinds.reviewVerifierVerdict`, `kinds.closureVerdict`). A reviewer whose part is a gap gets the verdict `gap` with the gap's reason, and its step is the last try's (`kinds.reviewPass.settleReviewer`).

**Verifiers.** A finding of a reviewer with verify on is settled by its verifier chain: a verdict gives its result, and a gap gives `undetermined` with the gap's reason (`kinds.reviewPass.settleFinding`). A finding of a reviewer with verify off is confirmed without a verifier and marked unverified, with the reason `unverified: reviewer <name> has verify off` (`kinds.reviewPass.settleFinding`). A verifier step is read-only and runs the `verifier` role's agent with the build's verifier schema and return check (`interpret.Runner.verifierAgent`, `roles.Agent`, `build.VerifierStart`).

**Review end.** The ledger holds the findings in the order the reviewers return them, each with the id `<reviewer>-<k>` (`kinds.reviewPass.ledger`). The review run ends `passed` once every part has a verdict, with one output line per reviewer, `<name>: <verdict>` (`kinds.Review.Decide`, `kinds.reviewLines`). `store.Store.EndReview` then ends the run, stores the verdicts and the ledger, and sets the phase to `correcting` (`interpret.Runner.carryReview`, `store.storeEndedReview`). Those rows are written only there, so no finding is added after the review ends. The `review` kind is not in a loop body, so no reviewer runs again after corrections (`workflow.kinds`).

**Corrections.** While the epic is `correcting`, a plan that starts writes the ledger trigger in place of its started trigger. Its id is `ledger:` and `store.NodeRunID` of the epic, the plan's path, the round and `ledger`. Its cause is `kinds.LedgerCause` of the round (`interpret.pass.planTrigger`). The round's open findings are the confirmed findings that no closure in an earlier round closed or left undetermined (`kinds.OpenFindings`, `kinds.closureSettled`). Round 1 with none has the cause `ledger round 1: no confirmed findings; nothing to correct` (`kinds.LedgerCause`). The round's correction tasks are built and integrated as the plan's tasks are (see **Batch integration**).

**Closure.** A `closure` node in the loop body runs once in each round, after the round's fix plan (`workflow.kinds`). For each open finding it runs one closure verifier, part `close <id>`, as a read-only step at the closure run's revision, which is the epic's stored base, over the frozen revision to that revision (`interpret.Runner.prepareClosure`, `interpret.Runner.reviewUnitStart`, `interpret.closureStart`). The step has the role text `internal/interpret/closure.md`, the schema `interpret.ClosureSchema`, and the return check `interpret.closureCheck`, which accepts exactly one result, `closed`, `open` or `undetermined`. A gap is `undetermined` (`kinds.reviewPass.settleClose`). The closure run ends `passed` when no closure is `open` and `failed` otherwise. It stores its round with the revision, the seqs of the round's tasks, its outcome and its closures, and an undetermined closure leaves its finding out of later rounds (`kinds.Closure.Decide`, `kinds.closureTasks`, `kinds.closureMark`, `kinds.closureSettled`, `store.Store.EndClosure`, `store.insertClosureRound`). The round's tasks are those of the turns above the mark of its fix plan (`kinds.closureTasks`). A failed closure writes no trigger, so the loop's next round starts with its fix plan, which writes the ledger trigger (`interpret.pass.end`, `interpret.pass.loop`).

**Conclude.** The `conclude` node has no prepare and no work, so its run ends the epic as soon as the run starts, whether or not the epic is paused (`kinds.Conclude.Decide`, `interpret.Runner.conclude`). The epic ends `clean` when no gap is left and `ended_with_gaps` when one is. `store.Store.EndEpic` sets the phase. For `ended_with_gaps` it also adds the body `kinds.EndingBody` gives as a tell: `Ended with 1 gap:`, or `Ended with <n> gaps:`, then one `- <gap>` line per gap in the order the gaps are listed (`kinds.Conclude.Decide`, `kinds.EndingBody`, `store.Store.EndEpic`). For `clean` it adds no tell, since the release that follows ends the epic and tells. It also stores the stored base as the review's clean revision (`store.Store.EndEpic`; `internal/store/queries.sql: SetEpicReviewClean`). The conclude run ends `passed` in the same write, so a restart never finds the epic ended with its conclude run still running, and so never ends it a second time after a later write, such as a release, has moved its phase (`interpret.Runner.conclude`, `store.Store.EndEpic`).

**Gaps.** A conclusion's gaps are, in order: `no review ran`, when the review never froze; `reviewer <name>: <reason>`, for each reviewer with a gap verdict; and, for each ledger finding, `<id>: verifier undetermined: <reason>` when its verifier is undetermined, `<id>: closure undetermined in round <r>: <reason>` for each undetermined closure, and `<id>: open after <n> rounds` when it is confirmed and every round left it open, where `<n>` is the number of rounds run (`kinds.Gaps`, `kinds.OpenFindings`).

**Reserved names.** These names are reserved for the engine epics that fill them, so the epics can be built in parallel without colliding. Each is contract now and has no behavior until its epic lands. Migration numbers are assigned in merge order; the names stay.

| Name | What | Epic |
|---|---|---|
| `integrate` | kind, top level | O3d4 |
| `review`, `conclude` | kinds, top level; registered by the format, and by the interpreter with `Reviewers` and `Ending` | O3e2 |
| `closure` | kind, loop body; registered by the format, and by the interpreter with `Reviewers` and `Ending` | O3e2 |
| `release` | kind, top level; registered by the format and by the interpreter (see [Release](#release)) | O3e3 |
| `built`, `met_at_base` | phases | O3d4 |
| `reviewing`, `correcting`, `clean`, `ended_with_gaps` | phases | O3e2 |
| `releasing`, `released`, `done`, `stopped` | phases (see [Release](#release)) | O3e3 |
| `00033_integration` | migration | O3d4 |
| `review_ledger` | migration | O3e2 |
| `release` | migration `00038_release.sql` (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)) | O3e3 |
| `release <pos>`, `judge` | work parts (see [Release](#release)) | O3e3 |
| `ReleaseEpic` | RPC of `orrery.engine.v1.Epics` (see [Release](#release)) | O3e3 |
| `StoredEpic` 18 `releases`, 19 `release_slot` | proto fields, declared (see [Release](#release)) | O3e3 |
| `orrery.v1.EpicsGetResponse` 13 `ledger`, 14 `releases`, 15 `reviews` | proto fields, declared (see [Epics and Steps](#epics-and-steps)) | O3e4 |

The attention epic has declared its names, so they are not in the table above. Migration `00039_questions` adds the question's shape and the answer's choice (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). `EpicQuestion` fields 5 to 10 are `label`, `choices`, `recommendation`, `replaces`, `replaced_by` and `choice` (`proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicQuestion`). `AnswerEpicRequest` field 5 is `choice` (`proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: AnswerEpicRequest`). `FollowEpics` is an RPC of `orrery.engine.v1.Epics`, with its messages `FollowEpicsRequest`, `FollowEpicsResponse` and `EpicFollow` (`proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: Epics`; see [Epics](#epics)).

The style epic A1c has declared its names, so they are not in the table above. Migration `00042_endings` adds the phase time and the report to `epics` (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). `StoredEpic` fields 23 to 25 are `phase_at`, `report` and `release_proof`, `EpicSummary` field 7 is `phase_at`, and `orrery.v1.Epic` field 13 is `phase_at`. `orrery.v1.EpicsGetResponse` fields 18 and 19 are `release_steps` and `report`, and `EpicReleaseProof` and `EpicReport` are the messages of the release proof and the report (`proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicSummary`, `proto/orrery/v1/epics.proto: Epic`, `proto/orrery/v1/epics.proto: EpicsGetResponse`, `proto/orrery/v1/epics.proto: EpicReleaseProof`, `proto/orrery/v1/epics.proto: EpicReport`).

The style epic A2b has declared its names, so they are not in the table above. Migration `00043_epic_seen` adds the table `epic_seen` (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). `Epics.Seen` is an RPC of `orrery.v1.Epics`, with its messages `EpicsSeenRequest` and `EpicsSeenResponse`, and `SeeEpic` is an RPC of `orrery.engine.v1.Epics`, with `SeeEpicRequest` and `SeeEpicResponse`. `orrery.v1.Epic` field 14 is `seen`, and `EpicSummary` field 8 and `StoredEpic` field 26 are `seen`. `NoticeBlock` is a message of both notice packages, with `kind` as field 1 and `ref` as field 2, and `InAppNotice` field 5 is `block` in both (`proto/orrery/v1/epics.proto: Epics`, `proto/orrery/v1/epics.proto: Epic`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: Epics`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: EpicSummary`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: StoredEpic`, `proto/orrery/v1/notice.proto: NoticeBlock`, `proto/orrery/v1/notice.proto: InAppNotice`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/notice.proto: NoticeBlock`, `proto/orrery/engine/v1/notice.proto: InAppNotice`).

The built-in workflow takes its final shape in O3d4: a red evidence check at base, `plan` holding `build` and `integrate`, `review`, a loop `correct` (`max` 2, `until` `closure.passed`, `spent` `continue`) over a `plan` and `closure`, `conclude`, then `release`. An epic on it waits at `review` while the interpreter has no `Reviewers` and `Ending` (see **End-of-epic review**). The proto fields above are reserved by this table only, not by `reserved` statements: buf counts deleting a reserved name as a breaking change even when the same field is then declared, so each epic adds its field at the number named here when it builds. The Go name `interpret.Ending` is declared now and follows the same rule.

#### Planner turns

The planner plans an epic in a turn. A turn is a read-only step (see [Steps](#steps)) whose return is checked before it is stored. The interpreter passes every 5 seconds, its default tick (`interpret.defaultTick`, `interpret.New`), and passes the planner for each epic whose plan node run it gives a run (see **Running**; `interpret.Runner.Pass`). While a sync of origin's main is due or runs under the plan run, the run gets none, so the planner waits for the sync (see **Sync main** under [Workflows](#workflows)). The planner's pending actions are carried out all the same, since each pass carries out every epic's before it decides any (see **Actions** below).

**Triggers.** A trigger is a reason to plan. `CreateEpic` writes the first one, `created`, in its write (`store.Store.CreateEpic`). Each answer that names a question writes one more, with the id `answer:<id>` and the cause `answer <id> to Q<n>` (`store.recordQuestionAnswer`). A trigger is `pending`, `claimed` by a turn, or `consumed` by that turn's return (`internal/store/migrations/00016_planner.sql: CREATE TABLE epic_triggers`). A turn that ends without a return sets its claimed triggers back to `pending` with turn 0 (`internal/store/queries.sql: ReleaseEpicTriggers`). A note writes no trigger. A task that its build fails writes one more, with the id `T<seq>-failed` and the cause `task T<seq> failed: <reason>` (see [Task builds](#task-builds)). A later plan node's start writes one more, when the epic already has a plan run: its id is `workflow:` and a derived id, and its cause is `workflow node <path> started` (see **Running**; `interpret.pass.start`). While the epic is `correcting`, that trigger is the ledger trigger instead, as **End-of-epic review** under [Workflows](#workflows) gives (`interpret.pass.planTrigger`). A check node that fails in a plan body writes one more, with the same prefix and a derived id, and the cause `workflow node <path> failed: <output>` (see **Failed checks**; `store.Store.EndNodesWaking`). An integrate run's end writes one more when it claimed a batch, with its derived id and the cause `workflow node <path> <passed|failed>: <output>` (see **Batch integration** under [Workflows](#workflows); `store.Store.EndBatch`). A plan that ends unbuilt writes one more, with the cause `epic not built: <missing>` (see **Built or park** under [Workflows](#workflows); `store.Store.ParkEpicWaking`). A planner-class park writes one more, with the id `park:<n>` and the park reason as its cause (see **Park classes** below; `store.insertParkTrigger`). A refused planner action writes one more, with the id `action-<turn>-<position>-refused` and the cause `planner <action> refused: <refusal>` (see **Actions** below; `store.refuseAction`). The owner's kick writes one more, with the id `owner-kick:<seq>` and the cause `owner kick: <text>`, where `<seq>` is the seq of the decision it records (`store.Store.KickEpic`). An evidence amendment that is applied writes one more, with the id `amend-<seq>` and the cause `evidence of <R> amended by the <amender> (A<seq>): <outcome>` (see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments); `store.finishAmendment`). A replan of the release writes one more, with the id `release:` and a derived id, and the cause `the release goes back to planning for a change to the candidate: <report>` (see **Replan.** under [Release](#release); `store.Store.ReplanRelease`). A sync of origin's main writes none (see **Sync main** under [Workflows](#workflows)). Nothing else inserts one.

**The plan node.** The first plan node run of an epic starts with mark 0 and no trigger. A later one starts with its mark at the epic's highest turn seq, and with the trigger above (`interpret.pass.start`). While it runs, its kind decides it on each pass. It ends `complete` when the epic's highest turn seq is above its mark, that turn succeeded with outcome `complete`, no turn is running, no task is open, no trigger is pending, and no evidence amendment is under way, neither an `amend_evidence` action still pending nor an amendment checking or capturing, since its end decides what the plan has built and wakes the planner (`kinds.Plan.Decide`, `kinds.planComplete`, `kinds.amending`; `store.EpicFlow.AmendActions`; see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments)), and its latest settle walk has ended (see **Plan bodies** under [Workflows](#workflows)). With an `integrate` node in its body, what follows the end is decided as **Built or park** there gives. Otherwise it runs, and the interpreter passes the planner for the epic, unless a sync of origin's main holds the plan (`interpret.pass.running`, `interpret.pass.plan`; see **Sync main** under [Workflows](#workflows)). Its turns are the planner turns this section describes, and the tasks of each turn start build child runs (see [Task builds](#task-builds)).

**Decide.** Each pass lists the epics with a pending trigger (`store.Store.PlanStates`), then reads the role file once (`planner.Runner.pass`). Under the interpreter's pass, it decides only the epics the interpreter named (`planner.Runner.PassOnly`), and the interpreter names every planner-parked epic on each pass, whether or not a plan run was given its turn (`interpret.Runner.Pass`). `Decide` returns one of three actions for each of them (`planner.Decide`):

- None, when the epic is paused, parked by an owner class, running, or has no pending trigger (`planner.Decide`). A park of a planner class does not stop it: `PlanStates` reports such a park as no park, so the epic is claimed like any other with a pending trigger (see **Park classes**; `store.PlanStates`, `store.ownedPark`).
- Park, with the role file's error, when that read failed (`planner.Decide`; `planner.Runner.pass`). A park is a turn. `parkTurn` claims one and frees it at once, with the error as both its reason and its park, so the turn records the park. The turn starts no step, and its triggers return to `pending` (`planner.Runner.parkTurn`). The park is decided under the planner park `PlanStates` read, and a retry since that decision voids it (see **Pause and park**).
- Claim a turn (`planner.Decide`; `planner.Runner.pass`).

**Claim.** `ClaimTurn` runs in one write (`store.Store.ClaimTurn`). It refuses an unknown epic, a closed epic, a paused epic, an epic parked by an owner class, an epic with a running turn, and an epic with no pending trigger. A planner-class park is no refusal, so the turn that answers it is claimed (`store.checkClaimable`, `store.ownedPark`). A refusal changes nothing (`store.checkClaimable`). Otherwise it inserts the next turn, numbered after the epic's highest turn, with `seen` set to the epic's highest decision seq. It then claims every pending trigger for that turn (`store.startTurn`; `internal/store/queries.sql: ClaimEpicTriggers`). A partial unique index allows one `running` turn per epic (`internal/store/migrations/00016_planner.sql: CREATE UNIQUE INDEX epic_turns_one_running`).

**Actions.** A turn's `actions` are stored pending in the write that completes the turn (see **Ends**). Each pass carries out the pending actions before it claims any turn, one write for each, in the order the store lists them: by epic, then turn, then position (`planner.Runner.pass`, `planner.Runner.carryOutActions`, `store.Store.PendingActions`, `store.Store.CarryOutAction`). An action whose write fails with a store error stays pending for the next pass. Any other action is taken or refused:

- Taken. Its control runs with the action's fields as given, in one write that also marks the action `taken` and appends a decision with the id `action-<turn>-<position>`. The decision's text names the action and holds the planner's reason (`store.takeAction`, `store.actionDecisionID`, `store.appendDecision`). The kinds are these:
  - `retry_node` runs `RetryNode` for its node, which reruns the node's parked runs, or its latest failed integrate run (`store.rerunNodeIn`). A sync run of origin's main is neither, since it never parks and its kind is `sync`, so a `retry_node` that names the node `sync` reruns no sync run (see **Sync main** under [Workflows](#workflows)).
  - `retry_epic` runs `RetryEpic` (`store.retryEpicIn`), so on a release its integrator stopped it sends the release back to planning (see **Replan.** under [Release](#release)).
  - `fail_task` fails the open task it names, with the reason as its failure, as `FailTask` does. The failure adds the trigger `T<seq>-failed`, so a later turn plans a task in its place (`store.failPlannedTask`, `store.failTaskIn`).
  - `resume_release` resumes the stopped release of the epic, as `ReleaseEpic` does for a stopped release. It is refused unless the epic is in phase `stopped` and already holds its repository's release slot, so a release the owner stopped, which freed the slot, stays with the owner. It refuses any named undo-none position, since only the owner names one (`store.resumeReleaseByPlanner`).
  - `close_epic` closes the epic as `CloseEpic` does, with the planner as its closer (`store.closePlannedEpic`, `store.closeEpicIn`).
  - `amend_evidence` starts an evidence amendment of the Requirement it names to the item it gives, by the planner, as `AmendEvidence` does for the owner, keeping the action's turn and position on it. It is not taken in that write: the action stays `pending` while its amendment is checking, and carrying it out again on a later pass changes nothing. The write links the action to the park it answers, as **Park bound** gives, so the action counts on the chain of the park its turn answered however long its amendment takes (`internal/store/queries.sql: LinkPlannerActionPark`). The write that applies the amendment marks the action `taken` with its decision `action-<turn>-<position>`, and the write that refuses the amendment refuses the action with the amendment's refusal, as **Refused** below gives. An amendment the store refuses at its start, such as one that changes the conclusion, refuses the action at once and stores no amendment (`store.Store.CarryOutAction`, `store.Store.startPlannedAmendment`; see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments)).

  A taken `fail_task` returns the ids of the task's open steps. A taken `close_epic` returns the step of the turn it ends and each other open step of the epic. The runner stops each returned step (`store.openTaskSteps`, `store.openEpicSteps`, `planner.Runner.carryOutActions`).
- Refused. The control refuses with a `RefusalError`, or the action names a closed epic, a task that does not exist or is not open, or a `resume_release` that its guard refuses. Then the control writes nothing. A second write marks the action `refused` with the refusal, and appends a decision with the id `action-<turn>-<position>-refused`, which holds the refusal and the planner's reason, and for `amend_evidence` the item the Requirement holds and the item the action asked for, as ` Prior item: <json>. New item: <json>.` (`store.amendedItem`, `store.itemsText`). Unless the park the action answers is stored as `retry_bound` (see **Park bound**), the write also adds the trigger of **Triggers** above, which wakes the planner (`store.refuseAction`, `store.boundHeldPark`). A refused action returns no steps. A `retry_node` or `retry_epic` action on an epic whose park the owner holds is refused, and the park and its question stay with the owner (`store.takeAction`).

An action in the return is `kind`, `args` and `reason`. `args` is a JSON object written as a string that holds the fields the kind uses (`node`, `task`, `close`, `by`, `requirement`, `item`, `undo_none`), so the schema the model is held to keeps one small fixed shape for every kind: the API refuses a strict structured-output grammar that compiles too large, and a schema whose actions held each field as its own property was refused on 2026-11-17, failing every planner turn. `planner.Parse` reads `args` into the action's fields (`planner.readArgs`); args that do not read, or hold a key no kind uses, are refused by the check below rather than failing the turn. An `item` is an evidence item read with no key it does not have, so an `item` with such a key does not read. `planner.TestPlannerSchemaIsTheOneTheAPIAccepted` pins the schema to one measured as accepted. `planner.Check` checks each action's shape before the return is stored: its kind, its args, the fields its kind needs, the fields it does not use, and a non-empty reason. It does not check the target, which is the engine's to refuse, as above (`planner.checkAction`).

**The step.** The runner reads the epic and the turn's triggers, then starts the step (`planner.Runner.startStep`). The step has these settings:

- Id: `turnStepID`, the first 32 hex digits of the lowercase SHA-256 of `orrery-planner`, the epic and the turn's seq, joined with NUL bytes. The same epic and seq give the same id, and a start under an id that a stored step already has is refused, so a restart finds the step rather than starting another (`planner.plannerNamespace`, `planner.turnStepIDLength`, `planner.turnStepID`, `planner.Runner.startStep`; `enginehost.Steps.startID`).
- Agent: the planner role's agent (`planner.Runner.startStep`).
- Workspace: the epic's repository, read-only, with no revision, so the step starts at HEAD (`planner.Runner.startStep`; `enginehost.Steps.Start`).
- Label: the epic's id, and no task (`planner.Runner.startStep`).
- Schema: `Schema`. Its `status` must be `done`, and its `outcome` must be `work`, `wait` or `complete`. Each task requires `title`, `deliverable`, `files`, `requirements`, `contributes`, `test_changes`, `gate` and `evidence`, and each `evidence` object requires `capture`, `limit`, `artifacts`, `supplied`, `conclusion`, `judge`, `kind` and `tests`. Both refuse any other key (`planner.Runner.startStep`; `planner.Schema`).
- Prompt: the role text (`internal/planner/role.md`), then these sections, each under a `## ` heading in this order. A section with no lines says `None.` (`planner.Prompt`, `planner.WriteSection`).
  - `Contract`: the contract's fields in order, with each list as its items (`planner.contractLines`). Each Requirement shows its evidence item as compact JSON, in the form a task's evidence item takes, judge included (`planner.RequirementItems`). After the Requirements come `Gates:`, each command gate as its name, its argv as a JSON array, its limit and the pools it needs, and each agent gate as its name, its agent and what it must and must not find; then `Done gates:` and `Review gates:` with their names, or `None.` (`planner.gateItems`, `planner.nameLine`).
  - `Decisions`: each epic decision as `- id: text`, as `- id (answers Qn): text` when it answers question n, or as `- id (answers Qn with choice): text` when the answer gave a choice. The last form has no `: text` when its text is empty. Then the decisions of each earlier turn as `- turn n: text` (`planner.DecisionLines`, `planner.decisionLine`).
  - `New notes`: the notes, the epic decisions that answer no question, recorded after the last earlier turn that is not free had seen (`planner.lastTurn`, `planner.newNoteLines`).
  - `Open questions`: each open question as `- Qn (turn n, label): text`, as `- Qn (park): text` for a park question, or as `- Qn (turn n): text` for a question with no label. Each declared choice follows as `  id: does`, then `  recommended: id (confidence): why` when the question recommends one (`planner.questionLines`, `planner.questionHead`).
  - `Advice followed`: each label's counts as `- label: F of A answers took the recommendation`, where `F` is the followed count and `A` the answered count, in label order, from `store.Store.Follows` (`planner.followLines`, `planner.Runner.startStep`).
  - `Tasks`: each task as `- Tn [status] title`, then its deliverable, files, requirements, contributes, test_changes, gate, and its evidence item as compact JSON, then `  guard refused: <refusal>` when the guard has refused its build (`planner.taskLines`). A failed task is followed by `  failure: <reason>` and `  files the next task may need: <paths>`, where the paths are those its implementer named as needed beyond its owned list, or `None.` when it named none (`planner.failedLines`).
  - `Evidence amendments`: each evidence amendment of the epic as `- A<seq> <R> by <amender> [<state>]: <reason>`, then `  new item: ` and the item it asks for as compact JSON, then `  refused: <refusal>` for a refused one (`planner.amendmentLines`; see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments)).
  - `Triggers`: the turn's claimed triggers as `- id: cause` (`planner.triggerLines`).
  - `Last failure`: the reason of the last earlier turn that is not free, when that turn failed with a reason (`planner.failureLines`).
- Return check: the planner's return check, described below (`planner.Runner.startStep`, `planner.planOf`).

**The forced return and its check.** Each return that passes `schema` runs the plan check (`enginehost.stepRun.valueChecks`). A return that does not parse is one line: its parse error (`planner.returnCheck`). Any other return is checked by `Check` against the epic's Requirement ids, the files of its open tasks, as the epic stood when the step started (`planner.returnCheck`), and each task's gate and evidence judge, and each question's shape. The gate and the judge are resolved for the epic by `EnvResolver`, which `orreryd` builds from its environment and the managed settings root (`planner.Check`; `planner.returnCheck`; `planner.EnvResolver`, `planner.NewResolver`). Each task refusal is one line: `tasks[i] "<title>" <field>: <reason>`, with `files[j]`, `test_changes[j]`, `requirements[k]` or `contributes[k]` for a list item, and `evidence.<field>` for a line the evidence item's check gives. Each question refusal is one line of its own, below. The task reasons are these (`planner.reasonFileForm`, `planner.reasonFileTwice`, `planner.reasonFileOwned`, `planner.reasonGitDir`, `planner.reasonRequirement`, `planner.reasonEmpty`, `planner.reasonNoFiles`, `planner.checkTask`):

- `not a normalized repository-relative file path`, for a file that is empty, has surrounding white space, starts with `/`, contains `\`, is not clean, or has a `.` or `..` part (`planner.fileFormOK`).
- `inside a .git directory`, for a file with a `.git` part in any letter case, such as a hook under `.git/hooks`, which could run code on later git operations (`planner.fileReason`, `planner.inGitDir`).
