- `named twice in the plan`, for a file that an earlier file of the return already names (`planner.fileReason`).
- `owned by an unfinished task`, for a file that an open task owns (`planner.fileReason`). A task whose status is `done` or `failed` owns none of its files, so its files plan again (`planner.openFiles`).
- `not a Requirement of the epic`, for a Requirement id the contract does not list (`planner.checkTask`).
- `empty`, for a blank title or deliverable, and `no files`, for a task with no files (`planner.checkTask`).
- `not one of the task's files`, for a `test_changes[j]` element that is not one of the task's `files` (`planner.testChangeRefusals`, `planner.reasonNotTaskFile`).
- `gate: empty`, for a blank gate (`planner.checkTask`).
- `gate: ` and the error of resolving the gate, when the name does not resolve to a command gate for the epic. The error names the gate and the sources it searched, or says that the gate is an agent gate. The epic's gates are checked first, then the repository's project file (see [Gate files](#gate-files); `planner.checkTask`; `planner.EnvResolver.Gate`, `planner.DefineEpicGates`; `gates.ResolveDefined`).
- `evidence.` and a line of the evidence item's check, for each refusal `evidence.Check` gives: `evidence.capture: none, name a capture or supplied files`, `evidence.limit: ...`, `evidence.artifacts[0]: ...` and so on, as [Evidence](#evidence) lists them (`planner.checkTask`; `evidence.Check`).
- `evidence.judge: ` and the error of resolving the judge, when the judge is not empty and claude would not resolve it as an agent for the epic's repository, under the environment and the managed settings. The error wraps `agents.ErrUnresolved` (`planner.checkTask`; `planner.EnvResolver.Judge`).
- `questions[i] "<label>" <line>`, one line for each rule a question breaks, in the order `store.QuestionRefusals` states them. Each line is one of `label: empty`, `label: not kebab-case`, `label: park is reserved for orrery`, `text: empty`, `choices: fewer than two choices`, `choices[j].id: empty`, `choices[j].id: other is offered by orrery`, `choices[j].id: named twice`, `choices[j].does: empty`, `recommended: names no declared choice`, `why: empty`, `confidence: not low, medium or high`, `replaces: not empty or Q<n> with n >= 1`, or `catastrophe: not one of irreversible-step, contract-cannot-hold, premise-false, cannot-proceed`, which every question must name. Whether a `replaces` names a question the epic holds is not checked here; the store checks it when it writes the turn (see **Ends**, item 2) (`planner.checkQuestion`, `store.QuestionRefusals`). A question's `why` is stored as `Catastrophe <catastrophe>: <why>`, so the owner reads which catastrophe the question names, and an empty `why` is stored empty, which the store refuses (`planner.questionInputOf`).
- `actions[i] "<kind>" <field>: <reason>`, one line for each rule an action breaks, in the order `planner.checkAction` states them. An unknown kind gives `kind: not one of retry_node, retry_epic, fail_task, resume_release, close_epic, amend_evidence`, and no field of that action is checked against a kind. A `retry_node` names a `node`, a `fail_task` names a `task` above 0, and a `close_epic` names a `close` of `superseded` or `abandoned`, with a `by` when superseded. A field its kind does not use is refused as `not used by this kind; leave it empty or 0`, and an empty `reason` is refused as `empty`. After those lines, an `amend_evidence` whose args read gets one line for a `requirement` that is not a Requirement of the epic, `requirement: not a Requirement of the epic`, one `item.<field>: <reason>` line for each refusal `evidence.Check` gives its `item`, and `item.judge: ` with the error of resolving a judge that does not resolve, as a task's judge resolves (`planner.checkAmendAction`). Its `item` keeps the Requirement's conclusion and kind: an item with another conclusion gives `actions[i] "amend_evidence" item.conclusion: differs from the conclusion of <R>; only the owner amends a conclusion`, and one with another kind `actions[i] "amend_evidence" item.kind: differs from the kind of <R>; only the owner amends a kind`, after the lines of `Coverage` (`planner.checkAmendKeeps`, `planner.returnCheck`). An action that passes this check is still checked by the engine when it is carried out (see **Actions**; `planner.checkAction`, `planner.checkNeededFields`, `planner.checkUnusedFields`).
- `actions: the epic is parked (<reason>); return an action for the park or a catastrophe question`, when the epic holds a planner-class park and the return holds no action and no question (`planner.checkPark`, `planner.returnCheck`). Any action or any question satisfies it, whatever its kind; the check does not match an action to the park.

`Coverage` then checks the same return against the epic's tasks, as the step started, and its refusals follow the lines of `Check` (`planner.returnCheck`; `planner.Coverage`). A stored task counts toward coverage unless its status is `failed` (`planner.taskFailed`, `planner.storedClaims`). Each line has the task's index and title, as the lines above do, except the outcome line:

- `requirements[k]: R<n> is claimed by T<seq>`, when a live stored task already claims the Requirement, and `requirements[k]: R<n> is claimed by tasks[j]`, when an earlier task of the return claims it (`planner.coverage.claimLines`, `planner.returnClaims`).
- `contributes[k]: not a Requirement of the epic`, when the id is not a Requirement of the contract, `contributes[k]: R<n> is claimed by this task`, when the task claims it itself, and `contributes[k]: R<n> is claimed by no task`, when neither a live stored task nor a task of the return claims it (`planner.coverage.contributeLines`).
- `outcome complete: R<n> is claimed by no task`, for each Requirement that neither a live stored task nor a task of the return claims, when the outcome is `complete` (`planner.coverage.outcomeLines`).

The role text (`internal/planner/role.md`) states these rules to the planner: every task starts at once from the epic's integrated branch beside the open tasks, so a task plans only work that can start now, and work that builds on an open task's change waits for a later turn, after that task is integrated, with outcome `work` and no tasks when nothing can start; a task is small, one behavior in a few files, and its deliverable names no git command that moves a worktree or ref, since a task's worktree is kept across its attempts and a file beyond its list is granted on request; a task that deletes, renames or rewrites a base test, or lowers its assertions, lists that file in `test_changes`; the item's `kind` and `tests`, one task that has not failed claiming each Requirement, the Requirements a task contributes to, the guard's rules and what a task's `test_changes` lifts from them, and every Requirement claimed at `complete`; and `amend_evidence` only when a Requirement's evidence cannot be met as written though the behaviour it claims is delivered, never to weaken what the Requirement claims, with its conclusion and kind kept, since the engine applies the new item only once its red fails at the epic's base, and the action settles after its turn; and a trigger whose id starts with `release:` carries the release integrator's report, whose decisions the planner plans as tasks on the epic's own code, and nothing else, before it returns `complete` (see **Replan.** under [Release](#release)). The guard's rules are in **The guard** under [Task builds](#task-builds).

A failed check is sent back to the session as `return: <lines>`, its lines joined with `; `, and a failed `schema` check is sent back the same way under `schema` (`enginehost.stepLoopReturnRecord`, `enginehost.stepRun.retry`). A failed `checkout` check of a read-only planner step is not sent back: it ends the step `checks_failed` (see [Step lifecycle](#step-lifecycle)). The step is sent back three times, and a fourth failing return ends it `checks_failed` (`enginehost.stepLoopMaxContinues`, `enginehost.stepRun.retry`). A return that passes its checks ends the step `done` with no judge call (`enginehost.stepRun.endDoneReturn`).

**Ends.** The runner waits for the step in one-minute waits (`planner.waitInterval`, `planner.Runner.resume`, `planner.Runner.finish`, `planner.Runner.await`). A `Wait` that fails is logged and repeated after a tick. It never ends a running turn, since the step may still be running (`planner.Runner.await`). A timed-out wait whose row shows the step limited stops the step (`planner.Runner.await`; `planner.rowLimited`). A stop that succeeds is not repeated, and one that fails is tried again at the next timed-out wait that still shows the step limited (`planner.Runner.await`). The step then ends `stopped`, and the turn is freed and the epic paused, whether the kept rate limit or the `stopped` end settles it (see [Step lifecycle](#step-lifecycle), Usage limit). When the step ends, `settle` checks these cases in order (`planner.Runner.settle`):

1. A rejected rate limit kept for the step. The turn is freed as a limit with the reason `<kind> rate limit, resets <time>`, where the time is RFC 3339 in UTC, or `<kind> rate limit, reset time unknown` when the limit names no reset time. The turn stores the reset time, or `-1` when the limit names none, and the epic is paused by the limit. When the reset is known, the epic's due row for it is stored in the same write, unless the owner's pause stands, in which case no row is stored. This applies whatever the end (`planner.Runner.settle`; `store.pauseFreed`, `store.pauseLimitedEpic`, `store.Store.FreeTurn`).
2. `done`. In one write the turn stores its decisions, its tasks with their files, Requirements, contributes, gate and evidence item (each `open`), its questions (each `open`, with its label, choices, recommendation, why, confidence and `replaces`), its outline and its outcome. The turn becomes `succeeded`, and its triggers become `consumed` (`planner.Runner.settle`; `store.Store.CompleteTurn`). A question whose `replaces` names an open question closes it: that question becomes `replaced`, and its `replaced_by` is the seq of the new question. A `replaces` that names an answered or replaced question closes nothing, and the new question is still stored (`store.insertQuestions`, `store.replaceQuestion`). A result that does not parse fails the turn (`planner.Runner.complete`). So does a plan with a question that `store.QuestionRefusals` refuses, or whose `replaces` names a question the epic does not hold. Then `CompleteTurn` writes nothing, and the turn fails with the store's `ErrBadQuestion` text as its reason. A shape refusal reads `complete turn <seq> of epic <epic>: bad planner question: question <n> "<label>": <first refusal>`, naming the question by its position and label. A `replaces` the epic does not hold reads `complete turn <seq> of epic <epic>: questions of turn <seq> of epic <epic>: question "<label>": bad planner question: replaces Q<n>: no such question` (`planner.Runner.complete`, `store.checkQuestions`, `store.insertQuestions`; `store.ErrBadQuestion`).
3. `stopped`. The turn is freed with the reason `stopped`, and the epic is paused (`planner.Runner.settle`).
4. `engine_restarted`. The turn is freed with the reason `engine_restarted`, and the epic is not paused (`planner.Runner.settle`).
5. Any other end. The turn fails with `<end>: <reason>` (`planner.Runner.settle`).

The read of the step's kept rate limit, and each write that ends a turn (`CompleteTurn`, `FailTurn` or `FreeTurn`), is retried each tick until it lands, until the store answers `ErrTurnNotRunning` (which is logged, and the write is not retried), or until the context ends. A turn whose context ends is left running for the next start (`planner.Runner.settle`, `planner.Runner.release`, `planner.Runner.failTurn`, `planner.Runner.freeTurn`).

**Stop.** `StopEpic` flags the running turn's `stop` in the same write that pauses the epic (`store.Store.StopEpic`). The turn runs on until its step ends. When the turn already has its step, the service stops that step (`enginehost.epicService.StopEpic`). When the turn has no step yet, the runner checks the flag as it records the step, and stops the step then (`planner.Runner.startStep`, `planner.Runner.stopIfFlagged`). Either way the step ends `stopped`, and the turn is freed with the reason `stopped`, as Ends gives. A step that has already ended `done` keeps its result, and the turn completes with it.

A freed turn returns its triggers to `pending`, and it is not counted toward a park (`store.endTurn`; `internal/store/queries.sql: ReleaseEpicTriggers`). A failed turn's reason is cut to 1024 bytes at a UTF-8 boundary (`store.TurnReasonMax`, `store.capTurnText`). After a failure, when the three latest turns that are not free, and that are later than the epic's `retried` seq, have all failed, the same write parks the epic with `3 planner turns failed in a row: ` and the stored reason (`store.Store.FailTurn`, `store.failedInARow`; `internal/store/queries.sql: ReleaseEpicTriggers`).

**Refusals at the start.** A start refused before its row is written settles the turn as follows (`planner.Runner.refuse`):

- The step cap is full (`enginehost.ErrCapReached`, `enginehost.Manager.reserveStep`). The turn is freed with the reason `step cap full`, with no pause and no park (`planner.Runner.refuse`).
- The agent does not resolve (`agents.ErrUnresolved`), or its name is not valid (`enginehost.ErrBadAgent`, `enginehost.ErrBadAgent`). The turn is freed with the error's text, and the epic is parked with the same text (`planner.Runner.refuse`; `enginehost.Manager.StartStep`, `enginehost.Manager.resolveStepAgent`).
- Any other start error fails the turn with its text (`planner.Runner.refuse`).

**Pause and park.** A free that pauses sets the epic's `paused` to 1 (`store.Store.FreeTurn`; `internal/store/queries.sql: PauseEpic`). A park sets `parked` to its reason and `park_class` to its class (`store.setParked`, `store.setPark`; `internal/store/queries.sql: SetEpicParked`). The parks of a workflow node, which **Failed checks**, **Ref compare**, **Batch integration**, **Built or park** and **Met at base** under [Workflows](#workflows) give, are made the same way, each as its class says (`store.Store.ParkNode`, `store.parkCheckedRun`, `store.Store.ParkEpicWaking`, `store.Store.ParkNodeMetAtBase`; see **Park classes** below). A free that is not a usage limit records the owner as the pause's source. A usage limit records `limit`, and the due pass lifts its pause when the row resumes, 60 seconds past the reset, through `ResumeDueEpic` (`store.pauseFreed`, `store.pauseEpicAs`; see [Epics](#epics) and [Due resumes in the store](#due-resumes-in-the-store)). An owner's pause lasts until `ResumeEpic` clears it. A park lasts until `RetryEpic` ends it, or, for an owner class, until an answer to its question does (see [Epics](#epics)). A retry after a turn's claim prevails over that turn's park. A retry stores the epic's highest turn seq as its `retried` seq, and a claim numbers its turn past that seq, so a `retried` seq at or past a turn's seq is a retry that landed while the turn ran. A park that turn frees with is then not set: the turn ends free, the epic stays unparked and no park question opens (`store.Store.FreeTurn`, `store.parkVoided`). This covers each turn-end park, from a pass, a refused start or a restart (`planner.Runner.parkTurn`, `planner.Runner.refuse`, `planner.Runner.restart`). A pass's park is also decided under the planner park the epic held when the pass read it, or under no park, and is set only while the epic still holds that park, so a retry that lands between the decision and the claim prevails too. The next pass decides again on the epic as it then stands (`store.EpicPlanState`, `store.TurnFree`, `store.parkVoided`). The park after three failed turns yields the same way, since a failed turn at or below `retried` does not count toward it (`store.Store.FailTurn`). A paused epic, and an epic parked by an owner class, is not claimed (`store.checkClaimable`), and `Decide` does nothing for it (`planner.Decide`).

**Park questions.** An owner park opens a question in the write that parks the epic, and a planner-class park opens none (see **Park classes**). A park question has turn `0`, label `park`, the park reason as its text, and state `open` (`store.openPark`, `store.insertParkQuestion`, `store.parkQuestion`). A park that is not final declares one choice, `retry`, which does "end the park and start a turn on the waiting triggers, as orrery epic retry does". It recommends `retry`, with why "no model judges a park; retry runs the epic again on its waiting triggers" and confidence `low`. A **Met at base** park's question declares `retry`, `drop` and `end` instead, as **Met at base** under [Workflows](#workflows) gives. A park on an epic already in phase `met_at_base` is final: its question declares no choice and recommends none, so only `other` is offered (`store.parkQuestion`). A park while a park question is open replaces it: the open question becomes `replaced` with `replaced_by` set to the new seq, and the new question's `replaces` names it (`store.insertParkQuestion`). An answer to a park question that is not final ends the park, whatever its choice, and an answer to a final park leaves the park in place. `RetryEpic` and `RetryNode` answer each open park question `retry` (see **AnswerEpic** and **Controls** under [Epics](#epics); `store.endParkByAnswer`, `store.closeParkQuestions`). An owner park also adds a tell that carries the seq of its question (`store.insertQuestionTell`). Two owner parks add no tell, since a planner turn's end makes them: the park after three failed turns (`store.FailTurn`) and the park a turn frees with (`store.FreeTurn`). The owner reads the question of either in the `park` notice of that turn (see **Notices** below).

**Park classes.** Every park has a class, which says who answers it (`store.ParkClass`). The class is given where the park is made and is never read from the park's text. `store.parkEpicAs` stores it on the epic as `park_class`, and `store.setPark` writes it. The owner classes are answered by the owner's question, which is the park's question described above. Each opens that question and adds its tell, except the two turn-end parks named above:
- `release_undo_none`: a release that stopped at a release step with undo none that failed (`store.StopRelease`, `store.releaseStopClass`).
- `release_owner_stop`: a release the owner stopped, which leaves the epic without its release slot (`store.StopRelease`, `store.releaseStopClass`).
- `branch`: the epic's branch exists, is gone, has moved or is checked out in a worktree (`store.ParkEpic` from `build.Runner.park`, `store.ParkNode` from `interpret.Runner.parkBatch`).
- `ref_move`: a ref move the ref guard parks as unexplained (`store.CheckNodeRefs`, `store.ParkTask` from `build.Runner.checkRefs`).
- `met_at_base`: a Requirement that the evidence found met at the epic's base (`store.ParkNodeMetAtBase`).
- `orchestrator`: the orchestrator cannot run. Its planner role file does not read, or its agent does not resolve. Either frees the turn with the error as the park, through `store.FreeTurn` (`planner.Runner.parkTurn`, `planner.Runner.refuse`). Three planner turns failed in a row park it through `store.FailTurn`, and a pinned workflow that does not load parks it through `store.ParkEpic` from `interpret.Runner.parkUnloaded`.
- `retry_bound`: a planner park or refusal at the bound of its chain, as **Park bound** describes. It is stored in place of the planner park, and wakes no turn (`store.boundPark`).

The planner classes are answered by the planner's next turn. A planner-class park opens no question and adds no tell. It writes one pending trigger, `park:<n>`, whose cause is the park reason, which wakes the planner, except `not_built`, whose wake is the workflow trigger its plan writes (`store.parkEpicAs`, `store.insertParkTrigger`, `store.ParkEpicWaking`). Each planner park that is not bound is stored as a row of `epic_parks` (see **Park bound**). The planner classes are these:
- `node`: a top-level node run that failed, whose park is `workflow node <path> failed: <output>` (`store.ParkNode` from `interpret.Runner.carry`).
- `task`: a task whose build gave no verdict twice, or whose role does not resolve at its start (`store.ParkTask` from `build.Runner.execute` and `build.Runner.startUnit`). The build's hold is as **Holds** under [Task builds](#task-builds) gives.
- `loop`: a loop that spent its rounds without its outcome (`store.ParkLoop`).
- `not_built`: a plan run that ended with Requirements unbuilt (`store.ParkEpicWaking`).
- `release_step`: a release stopped at a release step that is not undo none (`store.StopRelease`, `store.releaseStopClass`).

**Park bound.** A planner park belongs to a chain, which counts the planner's actions on it (`store.parkPlanner`, `store.parkChainOf`). Each planner park that is not bound is a row of `epic_parks` with its class, its subject, its chain and its reason. A subject is the node path for `node` and `loop`, `T<seq>` for `task`, `release` for `release_step`, and `''` for `not_built`. A park joins the chain of the epic's latest park row with its class and subject, and starts a chain of its own when there is none. A `task` park with no such row joins the chain of the task it replaces, followed back through the replacements of that task. Task N replaces task F, the lowest such F, when the planner failed F by a taken `fail_task` action, F was planned before N, the turn that planned N claimed the trigger `T<F>-failed`, and N shares a claimed or contributed Requirement with F (`store.ReplacedEpicTask`). A chain never resets: an owner's retry leaves its actions counted, so the next planner park on the chain goes to the owner too (`store.parkChainOf`).

Each action is linked to a park. An action answers the planner park the epic holds when it is taken or refused, and an action taken while the epic holds no planner park answers the park that an earlier action of its turn answered, or none (`store.actionPark`). An `amend_evidence` action, which is taken or refused only when its amendment settles, is linked when it is carried out and its amendment starts, and keeps that link (`store.Store.startPlannedAmendment`, `store.linkedPark`). The link is `planner_actions.park` (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). An action counts toward its park's chain whether it was taken or refused, so a refusal counts as the park coming back. When a planner park finds two or more actions on its chain, it is stored as `retry_bound`, which opens the owner question below and adds its tell, and it wakes no turn. A refusal that leaves the chain of the epic's held planner park with two or more actions does the same to that park (`store.boundPark`, `store.boundHeldPark`). A planner park stored before migration `00051_park_bound.sql` has no row, so it starts no chain and is never bound.

The owner question of a bound park has the park reason as its text, then `Earlier parks on this chain:` with each earlier park's reason, when there are any, then `Planner actions on this chain:` with one line for each action on the chain. An action's line names it and gives the planner's reason, then `taken` or `refused: <refusal>` (`store.boundQuestionText`, `store.actionLines`).

**The kept rate limit.** Each `rate_limit_event` line becomes a `RateLimit` event, with its status, its rate-limit type and its reset time (`session.reportEvents`, `session.rateLimitEvent`; `session.RateLimit`, `session.RateLimit.isEvent`). The recorder stores a `rejected` status only, in `engine_rate_limits` under the session's id (`enginehost.recorder.ops`). A step's turn reads that row when the step ends (`planner.Runner.settle`). [Engine sessions in the store](#engine-sessions-in-the-store) gives the table.

**Restart.** At start, `orreryd` runs `Steps.Readopt`, then the manager's Reconcile, then `Steps.Reconcile` (`cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.reconcile`), and it starts the interpreter after them (`cmd/orreryd.startSessions`). `Steps.Readopt` rebuilds the run of each open step row it can and registers it with the manager, which re-adopts the step's holder when that holder is live and tracked. `Steps.Reconcile` then starts the loop of each rebuilt step the manager reports live, and ends each other open row `engine_restarted` (`enginehost.Steps.Readopt`, `enginehost.Steps.Reconcile`; see [Restart](#step-lifecycle)). Then the interpreter's `Run` has the planner's `Recover` settle each running turn before the interpreter's first pass (`interpret.Runner.Run`, `planner.Runner.Recover`, `planner.Runner.recoverTurns`, `planner.Runner.recoverTurn`, `planner.Runner.restart`):

- A turn with no recorded step reads the step stored under its `turnStepID` (`planner.Runner.recoverTurn`). A step found there is recorded on the turn, and an open one is stopped when the turn was flagged to stop (`planner.Runner.recoverTurn`, `planner.Runner.recordFound`). Here and at a claimed turn's start, a record of the step the store refuses as busy is tried again 1 second later until it lands or the runner stops, since a turn with no recorded step holds its epic's planner until a restart and a stop of the turn finds no step to stop (`planner.Runner.recordStep`). A turn with no step under that id has its step started under that id, as a claimed turn's is, and the step is waited on like any open step (`planner.Runner.restart`). The agent is read as a pass reads it: a role error frees the turn and parks the epic with its text, and a refused start settles the turn as a refused start does (see **Refusals at the start**). The started step is recorded on the turn, and stopped at once when the turn was flagged to stop (`planner.Runner.startStep`, `planner.Runner.stopIfFlagged`).
- A turn whose step row cannot be read is logged. It stays running, so its epic gets no new turn (`planner.Runner.recoverTurn`).
- A turn whose step row is still open is waited on in its own goroutine (`planner.Runner.recoverTurn`).
- A turn whose step row has ended is settled by that end (`planner.Runner.recoverTurn`).

A turn whose step `Reconcile` ended `engine_restarted` is freed with that reason, and its epic runs the turn again. A turn whose step was re-adopted waits for that step like any open step, and is settled by its end. The re-adopted step keeps its plan check: `StepsConfig.ReadoptCheck` supplies the check that `planner.ReadoptCheck` gives its epic, built with the same resolver as a new turn's check (`enginehost.Steps.readoptRun`; `enginehost.StepsConfig.GateStateDir`; `planner.ReadoptCheck`; `cmd/orreryd.startSessions`). So its return is checked against the plan and ends `done` with no judge call, as a new planner step's does (`enginehost.stepRun.endDoneReturn`). A turn whose context ends when `orreryd` stops writes nothing, and it stays running (`planner.Runner.await`, `planner.rowLimited`, `planner.Runner.release`, `planner.Runner.failTurn`, `planner.Runner.freeTurn`).

**Notices.** The planner's attention loop tells the owner when an epic needs them. `planner.NewAttention` runs beside the interpreter, under the same flow context, and starts when `orreryd` has the store open, `--sessions` and a router (`cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startEvidence`, `cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startFlow`; `planner.NewAttention`). It passes at once and then once per tick, 5 seconds by default (`planner.Attention.Run`; `planner.defaultTick`).

Each pass reads what each epic with a turn or an untold tell needs, in this order, and the first that holds is the need. An owner park on an epic with no planner turn therefore reaches the owner through its park's tell (`planner.needOf`; `store.Store.Attention`, `store.Store.attention`, `store.ListEpicsWithTurnsOrUntoldTells`):

- `park`: the epic is parked by an owner class. A planner-class park is no need, since the planner answers it, so it routes no park notice (`store.EpicAttention.Parked`, `store.ownedPark`).
- `limit`: the epic is paused, and its latest ended turn is `free` as a usage limit.
- `questions`: the epic has open questions.
- `tell`: the epic's newest tell that is not a silent move tell (see **Tells** below) is newer than its latest ended turn, or than any turn when none has ended. A silent move tell never makes the epic need a tell, so an epic whose only newer tell is silent and that needs nothing else is seen, and its earlier notice clears (`store.SilentMoveTell`, `store.attentionOf`).
- `none`: otherwise.

For an epic with the need `none`, the pass makes a seen write for the epic's key at the pass time, which clears a notice still out (`planner.Attention.attend`). For any epic whose latest ended turn is not told, the pass sends one notice when `tellsOwner` names its need: `park`, `limit`, or `questions` when that turn succeeded and asked at least one question (`planner.Attention.attend`, `planner.tellsOwner`). The notice's id is `turnNoticeID`, `<epic>:turn:<seq>:<need>`, and it is routed only when no stored epic notice carries that id (`planner.Attention.storedIDs`, `planner.turnNoticeID`). Then it marks that turn told (`planner.Attention.attend`; `store.Store.MarkTold`). The notice carries the turn's told mark, and the first write of its row applies the mark in that same write. A restart after the write finds the turn told, so the attention pass never creates the notice again, whether the row is still pending or has ended. The router restores a stored row as it restores any row (`planner.Attention.attend`, `planner.Attention.notify`; `route.Router.Notify`; `route.rowOf`; `store.Store.PutNotice`). The pass also marks the turn told after the route, as a separate write (`planner.Attention.attend`; `store.Store.MarkTold`). One window is left. A first row write that fails is logged, and a kill before the separate mark lands leaves no row and an untold turn, so the restart routes the notice again (`route.Router.putLocked`).

**Tells.** Each untold tell of an epic is routed as a notice of its own, whose body is the tell's text, except a ref move's tell that carries no park question. A ref move's tell is one whose text is `T<n> implementer moved …`, as **Ref moves** under [Task builds](#task-builds) gives it, or `workflow node <path> moved …`, as **Ref compare** under [Workflows](#workflows) gives it. Such a tell routes to no surface and gets no notice: it is marked told and stays in the epic's tells (`planner.Attention.tell`, `store.SilentMoveTell`). A ref move's tell that carries a park question routes as a park, as the next paragraphs give. A tell that belongs to a park question has that question's park body, with the tell's text as its reason (`planner.parkTell`, `planner.tellBody`, `planner.parkBody`; see **Park questions** above and **Notices** below). A tell that belongs to any other question has that question's head, `Qn label:`, and its advice lines after its text (`planner.tellBody`, `planner.adviceHead`, `planner.adviceLines`). The tells come in seq order, and each is routed for every epic with a turn or an untold tell, whatever its need (`planner.Attention.attend`). Its id is `tellNoticeID`, `<epic>:tell:<seq>`, and it is routed only when no stored epic notice carries that id. The tell's notice carries the tell's mark, which the first write of its row applies, so a restart finds the tell told once its row is written. The pass also marks the tell told after its route, as a separate write, and a tell whose notice is stored is marked without a second route (`planner.Attention.tell`, `planner.tellNoticeID`; `store.untoldTellsOf`, `store.Store.MarkTellTold`). A `tell` need sends no notice of its own. The latest turn is announced only for the needs that `tellsOwner` names (`planner.tellsOwner`). The tell that an ending adds (see **Conclude.** under [Workflows](#workflows) and **Ending.** under [Release](#release)) is routed the same way, once, and a restart does not route it again.

The notice is keyed by its epic, is in state `waiting`, and is routed by the rules of [Notice routing](#notice-routing) with no watch and no judge (`planner.Attention.notify`, `planner.attentionNotice`; `route.Router.decide`). Its desk notice has the epic's id as its title and the body as its body, except a park notice, whose desk notice has the epic's contract title as its title. Its push has the epic's title as `project` and the body as `summary` (`planner.attentionNotice`). The body is the park body below for a park, `usage limit, resets <time>` for a limit, with the time in RFC 3339 UTC or, when the limit names no reset time, `usage limit, reset time unknown`, and the open questions for questions. A park's body leads with the head `Qn park:` of its open park question and that question's advice lines, then the reason `parked: <reason>`, when the question declares a choice. A final park's question declares no choice, so its body leads with `parked: <reason>` and puts the head and advice lines after it, and those advice lines are `other` alone. A park with no open park question has the body `parked: <reason>` alone. A park's tell that carries its park question has the same body, with the tell's text as its reason (`planner.bodyOf`, `planner.parkBody`, `planner.parkTell`). Each open question gives its head, `Qn <label>: <text>`, then its advice lines. The advice lines are each declared choice as `  <id>: <does>`, then `  other: answer in your own words`, then `  recommended: <id> (<confidence>): <why>` when the question recommends one (`planner.noticeHead`, `planner.adviceHead`, `planner.adviceLines`). A question stored before migration `00039_questions.sql` has no label, so its head is `Qn: <text>`, and its advice is the `other` line alone (`planner.noticeHead`). Its stored source is the notice's id, the epic's id, title, body and park flag as JSON, and a restart rebuilds the same notice from it (`planner.RestoreNotice`). A row stored before the park flag reads as no park, so its desk notice is titled by the epic's id.

**Block.** An epic notice opens on a block, which its `block` and `block_ref` keys carry (see [Push payload](#push-payload)). A turn's notice for open questions opens on the first open question, `Q<seq>`. A park's notice opens on its open park question when it has one, and a limit's notice on none (`planner.turnBlockOf`). A tell with a question opens on that question, `Q<seq>`, except that an epic stopped with a failed release step opens on that step, `release_step` with its position (`planner.tellBlockOf`, `store.EpicAttention`). A tell with no question opens on `report` when its epic's phase is `released`, `done`, `ended_with_gaps`, `stopped` or a closed phase, and on no block otherwise (`planner.tellBlockOf`, `planner.reportedPhase`). No planner rule raises `contract`. The notice keeps its block in its stored source, so a restart restores it (`planner.attentionNotice`, `planner.RestoreNotice`). Its desk notice and its push carry the block, and the desk stream line does not (see [Desk stream](#desk-stream)). The clients land on the block: `contract` opens the contract page, `question` and `release_step` open the epic's now block, which holds the answer form or Retry and Stop…, `report` opens the epic's Report, and no block opens the top of the epic view. The web does this in `web/src/app.tsx: App` and `web/src/epicpage.tsx: EpicPage`, and the phone in `android/app/src/main/kotlin/io/github/joshsymonds/orrery/MainActivity.kt: MainActivity.epicScreenOf`.

The notice clears when the epic needs nothing: when its last open question is answered, when `ResumeEpic` clears a limit pause, or when `RetryEpic` ends a park, as the seen write of the next pass gives (see [Notice routing](#notice-routing) and [orreryd](#orreryd)).

**Caller events.** Two passes tell the session that started a step, or created an epic, what happened to it. `orreryd` starts both in `startSessions`, after the reconcile, whenever it has the store open, with or without a router, since a caller event goes to its caller and not to the owner (`cmd/orreryd.startSessions`, `cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startCallerEvents`). Each pass runs at once and then once per tick of 5 seconds, `enginehost.callerEventTick` for the step pass and `planner.defaultTick` for the epic pass (`enginehost.StepCallerEvents.Run`, `planner.EpicCallerEvents.Run`). The step pass sends the end of each ended step to its caller as the event `step:<step id>` of kind `step_end`, and a caller that read the end through `step_status` or `step_wait` is not sent it (`enginehost.StepCallerEvents.Pass`, `store.Store.CallerSteps`, `store.Store.MarkStepRead`). The body of a step end names the step, its end, status and reason, then has an `Agent:` line with the step's agent, an `Epic:` line with the epic that holds the step when one does, and a `Summary:` line whose text after `Summary: ` is the step's whole return (`enginehost.stepEndBody`). The epic pass reads each epic that `store.Store.Attention` lists, and sends the session that created the epic each open question as `epic:<epic>:Q<seq>`, of kind `park` when the question is the park and `question` otherwise. It sends the epic's phase as `epic:<epic>:<phase>`, of kind `release` for `released` and `stopped`, and of kind `end` for `met_at_base`, `ended_with_gaps` and `done`. A `clean` epic sends nothing, and an epic with no creator sends nothing (`planner.EpicCallerEvents.attend`, `store.EpicPhaseEventKind`, `store.EpicPhaseEvent`, `store.EpicQuestionEvent`, `planner.questionEventOf`). Each event's text is `enginehost.CallerEventBlock`: an `<orrery-event kind="K" id="I">` opening tag, the body on a line of its own, and `</orrery-event>`.

Both passes send through one `enginehost.CallerSender` (`enginehost.NewCallerSender`, `enginehost.CallerSender.Send`). A headless caller gets the block as a prompt from `enginehost.Manager.Deliver`, with the event id as its `PromptID`, so the prompt is stored with sender `event` and is sent once even when a pass repeats it. A pane caller gets the block as keys from `cli.WakePane`, which types it only while the pane is idle, and the event is marked once the keys are sent. `cli.WakePane` makes the text typeable first (`cli.paneWakeText`): invalid UTF-8 is replaced, CRLF and lone CR become LF, every other control character, a tab among them, becomes a space, and a text that still needs more than 200 typed invocations is cut in the middle. The cut keeps as many leading lines as fit, the last of them cut by pieces if needed, then a line saying the rest was cut, then the block's last line, so the closing `</orrery-event>` is kept. The full text stays readable through `step_status` or `epic_get`. So such an event is delivered and not held. A step caller receives nothing and its event is marked, since a step keeps `step_wait`. A headless caller is written the event at once, even while a turn runs or a background task is listed. A headless caller with a request open, a caller the owner stopped, a headless caller the owner interrupted and has said nothing to since (`enginehost.ErrSessionBusy`, `enginehost.ErrCallerStopped`, `enginehost.ErrCallerQuiet`), and a pane that is not idle hold the event unmarked for a later tick, and a stopped caller is never restarted. Any other failure is logged and leaves the event unmarked, so the next tick tries it again. Each marked event is a row of `caller_events` (`store.Store.MarkCallerEvent`, see [Caller events in the store](#caller-events-in-the-store)). A caller event that Claude cancels at Escape, or that Claude has not started when the process ends, loses its mark. The dedupe admits it once more after the owner's next `Say` or a resume, since a cancelled or discarded event prompt does not count as sent (`store.Store.EnginePromptSent`, `store.EnginePromptState`, `store.EnginePromptsDiscard`). Shutdown cancels both passes in the phase `step caller events`, which waits for the step pass, and waits for the epic pass in the phase `epic caller events`. Both phases run before `steps` and `sessions` (`cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.shutdown`).

**The owner's interrupt quiets caller events.** Once the owner interrupts a headless session with `Interrupt`, the call the web's Escape and the Control `Interrupt` make, no caller event starts a turn in it until the owner next sends it a message with `Say`. The session is quiet from the start of the `Interrupt`, with or without a turn running, and it stays quiet when the interrupt itself fails. While it is quiet, `Deliver` stores and sends nothing and returns `enginehost.ErrCallerQuiet`, so each pending event stays unmarked and both passes try it again on their next tick, as for a busy caller. A quiet session that the idle stop has stopped stays quiet: `Deliver` resumes nothing for it and returns `enginehost.ErrCallerQuiet`, so the conversation the owner stopped stays stopped. The owner's next `Say` clears the quiet as it decides how its message goes, a `Say` that resumes a stopped session among them, so that message runs as a turn, and the passes deliver the pending events as before once the session is not busy. The quiet holds back caller events only, and a turn claude starts by itself is not one. The manager keeps the quiet sessions' ids in memory, not in the store, so a restart of `orreryd` clears the quiet, and a caller event then resumes and delivers to the session as to any idle-stopped caller (`enginehost.Manager.Interrupt`, `enginehost.Manager.OwnerInterrupt`, `enginehost.Manager.claimFree`, `enginehost.Manager.admitEvent`, `enginehost.Manager.decide`, `enginehost.CallerSender.Send`).

#### Evidence

An evidence item shows that a Requirement or a planned task holds. Its evidence is either a capture that `orreryd` runs or files that the agent supplied, which `orreryd` reads from the commit and marks `supplied`, and an agent judges the kept output against the item's conclusion. `orreryd` runs each evidence run in the background, and the runs and their bytes are kept, never pruned. The verdict comes only from the judge step's end (`enginehost.Evidence.recordVerdict`).

The Evidence service is `orrery.engine.v1.Evidence` (`proto/orrery/engine/v1/epic.proto: Evidence`). It has three calls, `RunEvidence`, `ListEvidence` and `GetEvidenceArtifact`, on the session socket beside `Epics` (`enginehost.Serve`). Its consumers are the `orrery evidence` subcommands (see [orrery](#orrery)), the `evidence_*` tools of `orrery mcp` (see [Step and epic tools](engine-protocol.md#14-step-and-epic-tools)), and `serve`, which merges each Requirement's runs into `Epics.Get` and answers artifacts from `GetEvidenceArtifact` (see [Epics and Steps](#epics-and-steps)). The service answers with these Connect codes, and `unavailable` with `evidence unavailable` when orreryd has no runner (`enginehost.evidenceService.ready`):

| Connect code | When | Message |
|---|---|---|
| `invalid_argument` | `RunEvidence` with an empty `epic`, `item`, `revision` or `phase`, in that order | `<field>: empty` (`enginehost.evidenceServiceCheckRun`) |
| `invalid_argument` | `RunEvidence` refused for its item, phase or revision | `evidence run is refused: <reason>` (`enginehost.ErrEvidenceRefused`, `enginehost.Evidence.Run`, `enginehost.evidenceRunItemOf`, `enginehost.evidenceRunRequirementItem`; `enginehost.evidenceServiceRunError`) |
| `not_found` | `RunEvidence` of an epic that is not stored | `epic: no epic <id>` (`enginehost.evidenceServiceRunError`) |
| `invalid_argument` | `ListEvidence` with an empty `epic`; `GetEvidenceArtifact` with an empty `run` | `epic: empty`, `run: empty` (`enginehost.evidenceService.ListEvidence`, `enginehost.evidenceService.GetEvidenceArtifact`) |
| `not_found` | `GetEvidenceArtifact` of a run that is not stored, or of a name the run does not keep | `run: no run <id>`, or `name: no kept artifact <name>` (`enginehost.evidenceService.GetEvidenceArtifact`) |
| `internal` | a store or blob failure | the error's text (`enginehost.evidenceServiceStoreError`) |

**The item.** An item is an `evidence.Item` (`evidence.Item`). Its fields are `capture`, the argv of the capture; `limit`, the capture's limit in seconds; `artifacts`, the repository paths the capture keeps; `supplied`, the repository files the agent supplied instead, read from the commit; `conclusion`, what the evidence must show; `judge`, the agent that judges it; `kind`, how the item shows its evidence; and `tests`, the test files of a test item. `evidence.Item.Kind` and `evidence.Item.Tests` hold them, and both are left out of the stored form when empty (`evidence.Item`). `evidence.Check` returns one line per refusal, in field order (`evidence.Check`):

- Exactly one of `capture` and `supplied` is set. Neither gives `capture: none, name a capture or supplied files`, and both give `supplied: set beside a capture` (`evidence.captureLines`, `evidence.suppliedLines`).
- Each `capture` element is non-empty, and `capture[0]` contains a `/`, since it is never looked up on `PATH` (`evidence.captureLines`).
- A `limit` is set only with a capture, and a capture's limit is a whole number of seconds from 1 to the largest duration (`evidence.limitLines`).
- `artifacts` are set only with a capture, and `supplied` only without one. Each path of either list is a normalized repository-relative file path, is not inside a `.git` directory at any depth or case, and is not named twice in its list (`evidence.artifactLines`, `evidence.suppliedLines`, `evidence.pathLines`, `evidence.fileFormOK`, `evidence.inGitDir`).
- `conclusion` and `judge` are not blank after trimming white space (`evidence.Check`).
- `kind` is `test`, `capture` or `supplied` (`evidence.KindTest`, `evidence.KindCapture`, `evidence.KindSupplied`). A `test` item runs its capture beside the test files in `tests`, a `capture` item runs a capture alone, and a `supplied` item is files a person supplies. No kind is refused as `kind: none, name test, capture or supplied`, and another kind as `kind: "<kind>" is not test, capture or supplied`. A `test` or `capture` item without a capture is refused as `kind: test without a capture` or `kind: capture without a capture`, and a `supplied` item without supplied files as `kind: supplied without supplied files` (`evidence.kindLines`).
- `tests` is set only on a `test` item, and a `test` item lists at least one test file. Otherwise the refusals are `tests: none, a test item lists its test files`, `tests: set on a capture item` and `tests: set on a supplied item`. Each element is a path that is checked as `artifacts` and `supplied` are, and a bad one is `tests[j]: <reason>` (`evidence.testsLines`, `evidence.pathLines`).

Stored items are not checked. The store checks no item it reads, and `evidence.Check` runs only on an item that is about to be stored: in `enginehost.checkEpicRequirementItem` and `planner.checkTask`, and for the item an evidence amendment asks for, in `planner.checkAmendAction`, `enginehost.checkAmendEvidence` and `store.amendmentRefusal`. So an item stored before kinds keeps the fields it was stored with. Migration `00037_assertions.sql` sets the kind of a stored item that has a capture or supplied files (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). An item with neither stays kindless and reads back as stored (`store.decodeItem`; `internal/store/migrations/00037_assertions.sql: UPDATE epic_requirements`).

**Phases and passing.** A run is for one phase, `red` or `green` (`evidence.PhaseRed`, `evidence.PhaseGreen`). A run whose capture captured nothing passes neither phase. Red passes when the verdict is `fails`, and green passes when the verdict is `holds` and the capture exited 0 (`evidence.Passed`). For a test item, red's exit is also checked by the reproduction that the green stage runs at the task's base before its green run. Red's exit holds when the reproduction ran and timed out or exited non-zero. A reproduction that exited 0 without timing out, or that did not run, spends the green attempt (see **Units** under [Task builds](#task-builds); `build.decideRepro`).

**Runs.** `RunEvidence` names an item by `R<n>`, a Requirement's id, which must carry an item, and refuses any other name (`enginehost.evidenceRunItemOf`, `enginehost.evidenceRunRequirementItem`). It resolves the revision to a commit, and it stores the run in state `capturing`, with its item, phase, revision and source, before anything runs. A run's source is `captured` when its item names a capture, and `supplied` otherwise (`enginehost.Evidence.Run`, `enginehost.evidenceRunSource`). The drive then runs in the background, under a context that `Close` cancels (`enginehost.Evidence.start`).

**Task runs.** `Evidence.RunTask` runs a task's evidence for its build. Its run's item is `T<seq>`, its spec is the item the task names, and its id is the caller's, which the build derives (see [Task builds](#task-builds)). An id that is not 32 lowercase hex digits, a task the epic does not have, a task whose item is the zero value, and a phase other than `red` or `green` are refused with an error matching `ErrEvidenceRefused`, and store no run. An id that is already stored returns that run and starts nothing, whatever the other arguments are. Otherwise the revision is resolved, and the run is stored and started as `RunEvidence` starts it (`enginehost.Evidence.RunTask`). `RunTask` is a Go call. The Evidence service does not carry it.

**Amendment runs.** `Evidence.RunAmendment` runs the item an evidence amendment asks a Requirement to hold, under the caller's id, which the amendment names (see [Evidence amendments](#evidence-amendments)). Its run's item is the Requirement's id and its spec is the amendment's item, so the run is stale until the Requirement holds that item, and current from then on. An id that is not 32 lowercase hex digits, a Requirement the epic does not hold, an item that is the zero value, a phase other than `red` or `green`, and a revision that does not resolve are refused with an error matching `ErrEvidenceRefused`, and store no run. An id that is already stored returns that run and starts nothing. Otherwise the run is stored and started as `RunTask` starts one. `RunTask`, `RunItem` and `RunAmendment` share that path (`enginehost.Evidence.RunAmendment`, `enginehost.Evidence.runUnder`). `RunAmendment` is a Go call, and the Evidence service does not carry it.

**Current runs.** A run counts only while its kept spec is current with the item now stored under its Item, which holds when the two encode to the same JSON (`evidence.Current`). `store.currentRuns` applies this when `store.Store.EpicFlow` reads an epic, so `EpicFlow.Evidence` holds only current runs, and a run whose item names nothing stored is not current. A Requirement's run is named by its id and a task's run by `T<seq>` (`store.currentRuns`, `store.Store.EpicFlow`). `store.taskOf` leaves `EpicTask.Red` and `EpicTask.Green` nil when their run is not current (`store.taskOf`). `store.Store.EvidenceRuns` and `store.Store.EvidenceRun` return every run, current or not (`store.Store.EvidenceRuns`, `store.Store.EvidenceRun`). The interpreter reads its rows from `EpicFlow` (`interpret.Runner.decide`), so `kinds.Evidence.Decide`, `kinds.GreenAt`, `kinds.Unbuilt` and `kinds.MetAtBase` read a stale run as missing. A Requirement whose red run at the base is stale is therefore named by `kinds.Unbuilt` as `R<n> has no red at the epic's base <base>` (see **Built or park** under [Workflows](#workflows)). The `state` that `epic_get` reports for a Requirement reads the current runs as well (`enginehost.requirementState`; see [Epics](#epics)).

**Captured runs.** A capture runs at the run's revision in a detached worktree at `evidence-worktrees/<run id>` beside the store file (`enginehost.evidenceCapture.run`; `enginehost.NewEvidence`; `cmd/orreryd.startSessions`). It queues for one member of the `gate` pool, under the pool files of that worktree, and its gate runs in a holder named `evidence-<run id>` (`enginehost.evidenceCapture.captureRequest`, `enginehost.evidenceCapture.captureGate`; `gates.DefaultPool`). Its limit is the item's limit, and each of its stdout and stderr tails is 1 MiB (`enginehost.evidenceCaptureLogTail`, `enginehost.evidenceCapture.captureGate`). The grant and the holder are stored once the grant holds, before the gate launches (`enginehost.Evidence.capture`; `enginehost.evidenceCapture.run`).

When the gate ends, the run keeps its exit status and whether it timed out, its stdout and stderr logs, and its artifacts (`enginehost.evidenceCapture.end`):

- Each log is the newest 1 MiB of one of the capture's two streams, stored as `<sha256>.log`. The stdout log's hash and size are `log_hash` and `log_bytes`, and the stderr log's are `stderr_hash` and `stderr_bytes` (`enginehost.evidenceCapture.end`, `enginehost.evidenceCapture.keepLog`). An empty stream stores no log.
- An artifact is read through an `os.Root` of the worktree. A regular file up to 10 MiB is kept as a blob, with its hash and size. A larger file is `over_cap`, with its size recorded and nothing stored. Anything else at the path is `missing`. A symbolic link is not a regular file, so it is never followed (`enginehost.evidenceCapture.keepArtifacts`, `enginehost.evidenceCapture.keepArtifact`, `enginehost.captureRegularFile`; `enginehost.evidenceCaptureArtifactCap`).

The worktree and the gate's holder directory are removed on every path (`enginehost.evidenceCapture.end`).

**Not captured.** A capture that captured nothing records one of four reasons, and the run then ends with no judge. `could not start: <error>` is for a worktree, grant or gate that did not start (`enginehost.captureNotStarted`). `killed at its limit` is for a gate the limit stopped (`enginehost.evidenceCapture.end`). `could not finish: <error>` is for what the gate left that could not be kept or cleaned up, or a run whose context ended after its gate launched (`enginehost.evidenceCapture.run`, `enginehost.captureNotFinished`). `lost at a restart` is for a gate that a restart could not re-adopt (`enginehost.evidenceCapture.readoptLost`).

**Supplied runs.** A supplied run reads each file of `supplied` from the commit at its revision with `git cat-file`, never from the working tree (`enginehost.readSupplied`, `enginehost.keepSupplied`). A path the commit holds no blob for is `missing`. A blob over 10 MiB is `over_cap`, and any other blob is kept under its sha256 (`enginehost.evidenceMaxKeptBytes`, `enginehost.keepSupplied`). The run's exit status is 0 only when every file is kept, and 1 otherwise (`enginehost.readSupplied`). A revision that does not resolve is `could not start: <error>`, and a file that cannot be read is `could not keep <file>`, each recorded as not captured. Nothing is written to the repository (`enginehost.readSupplied`).

**The judge.** A run whose evidence was captured or read, with no not-captured reason, is judged by a judge step. The step is read-only, runs at the run's revision in the epic's repository, and is labelled with the epic and the task `evidence-<run id>`. Its step id is `evidenceJudgeID`, the first 32 hex digits of the lowercase SHA-256 of `orrery-evidence-judge`, the run id and the judge number, joined with NUL bytes, and `startJudge` starts the judge under that id (`enginehost.evidenceJudgeNamespace`, `enginehost.evidenceJudgeIDLen`, `enginehost.evidenceJudgeID`; `enginehost.Evidence.startJudge`). Its agent gate is `evidence_judge`, given as data (see [Agent gates](#agent-gates)). Its agent is the item's judge, it has one `must` assertion, which is the conclusion, and its limit is 20 minutes (`enginehost.evidenceJudgeGate`, `enginehost.evidenceJudgeLimit`, `enginehost.judgeGate`, `enginehost.judgeStart`). Its prompt names the run's capture command as a JSON array of its argv, on a `Command:` line before the exit status and whether it timed out. A supplied run has no capture, so its prompt names no command. The prompt then names the blob of each log it has, labelled `Stdout log` or `Stderr log`, and the blob of each artifact, with `missing` for one not kept and `over the 10 MiB cap` for one over it. It never names the phase, and it tells the judge to read only the listed files, with repository files as reference only (`enginehost.judgePrompt`, with the command at `enginehost.judgePrompt`, and `enginehost.judgeArtifactWhere`).

The verdict follows the step's end. A step that ends `done` holds the conclusion. A step that ends `gate_failed` whose kept return's first result is `fails` fails it. Any other end gives no verdict, and its reason is the end, followed by the step's reason when it has one (`enginehost.judgeVerdict`; see [Step lifecycle](#step-lifecycle) for the ends).

A run with no verdict is judged again on the same kept output, up to three judge steps in all, and the third end is the run's reason (`enginehost.evidenceJudgeTries`, `enginehost.Evidence.judgeRun`). The store counts the judges of a run (`internal/store/queries.sql: SetEvidenceRunJudge`). Each judge number has its own id, so a judge that a restart finds under the next number is not started again (see Restart below). The verdict, its reason and whether the run passes are recorded once, while the run is judging (`enginehost.Evidence.recordVerdict`; `store.Store.RecordEvidenceVerdict`).

**Restart.** `orreryd` resumes the open runs after its step reconcile and before its planner starts (`cmd/orreryd.startSessions`, `cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.startEvidence`; `enginehost.Evidence.Resume`). A judging run waits for the judge step it holds. A judge step stored under the id of the run's next judge number is adopted and counted as that judge, so a judge that a restart finds is waited for and not started again. Otherwise the run starts its next judge (`enginehost.Evidence.readoptJudge`, `enginehost.Evidence.readoptJudgeStep`). A capturing run with a stored grant re-adopts its gate, and a gate that cannot be re-adopted is `lost at a restart` (`enginehost.Evidence.readoptCapture`; `enginehost.evidenceCapture.readopt`, `enginehost.evidenceCapture.readoptRequest`, `enginehost.evidenceCapture.readoptLost`). A capturing run with no stored grant starts its capture again, after its worktree is removed (`enginehost.Evidence.readoptUnlaunched`). The judge step is re-adopted with its gate rebuilt from the run's stored item (`enginehost.EvidenceReadoptGate`; see [Steps](#steps)).

A shutdown under `--holder-scope` detaches each running capture gate, which then keeps running under its holder for the next start. Without that flag, the shutdown kills the gate. A detached gate records nothing, and the next start re-adopts it. A capture that ended before or during the shutdown is recorded all the same, and its run is left judging with no judge step for the next start to judge. A capture cut short before its gate launched, and a supplied read cut short, record nothing, so the next start runs them again (`enginehost.Evidence.Close`, `enginehost.Evidence.drive`, `enginehost.Evidence.capture`; `enginehost.Evidence.readoptCapture`; `enginehost.evidenceCapture.run`; `cmd/orreryd.sessionHost.shutdown`).

**Storage.** A run and its artifacts are rows of `evidence_runs` and `evidence_artifacts` (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)). Each write after a run is added needs the state the run is in, and any other state is `ErrEvidenceState`, so each record lands once (`store.Store.AddEvidenceRun`, `store.Store.SetEvidenceGrant`, `store.Store.RecordEvidenceCapture`, `store.Store.SetEvidenceJudge`, `store.Store.RecordEvidenceVerdict`). Bytes are files named by their sha256 under `evidence/blobs` beside the store file. The directory is created at mode 0700, each name takes its extension lowercased when it is up to eight characters of `[a-z0-9]`, and no file is pruned (`enginehost.evidenceDirMode`, `enginehost.evidenceHashLen`, `enginehost.evidenceMaxExt`, `enginehost.evidenceExtChars`, `enginehost.errBlobName`, `enginehost.evidenceBlobs`, `enginehost.evidenceExt`, `enginehost.evidenceBlobs.put`; `enginehost.NewEvidence`; `cmd/orreryd.startSessions`).

#### Evidence amendments

A Requirement's evidence item is fixed in the contract at `CreateEpic`. An evidence amendment replaces it while the epic runs, when the item turns out wrong though the behaviour it shows is delivered, such as a capture that runs a test from a package the test has left, or a capture that cannot show what its conclusion claims. The planner asks for one with an `amend_evidence` action (see **Actions** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)), and the owner with `AmendEvidence`, `orrery epic amend` or the `epic_amend` tool (see [Epics](#epics) and [orrery](#orrery)). Both start it with the same write, and the engine, not the prompt, enforces its rules (`store.startAmendment`, `store.Store.AmendEvidence`, `store.Store.startPlannedAmendment`).

**Start.** An amendment names a Requirement, the whole item it should hold, and a reason. Its write stores it as the epic's next amendment, `A<seq>`, in state `checking`, with its amender, `owner` or `planner`, the turn and position of the planner action that asked for it, `0` for both otherwise, the item the Requirement holds, the item it asks for, the epic's base, and the id of its red run (`store.Store.AmendEvidence`, `store.startAmendment`). The Requirement keeps its item. The write refuses with a `RefusalError`, and writes nothing, an epic in an ended phase (`epic <id> is <phase>, an ended phase, and takes no evidence amendment`), an epic with no base, and a Requirement the epic does not hold (`epic <id> has no Requirement "<R>"`). It then refuses these, in this order (`store.amendmentRefusal`):

- A Requirement dropped as already met at the base (see **Met at base** under [Workflows](#workflows)): `<R> was dropped as already met at the epic's base by decision <n>`.
- An item that `evidence.Check` refuses, the check `CreateEpic` applies to a Requirement's item: `item: ` and its lines joined with `; ` (see [Evidence](#evidence)).
- The item the Requirement already holds, compared with each empty list read as `null`, so an item that differs only in giving an empty list as `[]` is the same item: `<R> already holds this item` (`store.listsAsNull`).
- A planner amendment whose conclusion is not the Requirement's: `the planner may not change the conclusion of <R>; only the owner amends a conclusion`. The planner amends how a Requirement is shown, never what it claims, so only the owner changes a conclusion.
- A planner amendment whose kind is not the Requirement's: `the planner may not change the kind of <R>; only the owner amends a kind`. A kind decides what else proves the item, such as a test item's reproduction, so a planner's change of kind could drop that proof.
- A Requirement with an amendment that is checking or capturing: `<R> has an amendment that is not settled yet` (`internal/store/queries.sql: CountOpenRequirementAmendments`).

`AmendEvidence` is the owner's alone. A call whose session is a step of the epic, the step of its running turn or any other step labeled with the epic, is refused with `failed_precondition` and stores nothing, so the planner amends only through its action, which counts on its park's chain (`enginehost.epicService.AmendEvidence`, `enginehost.epicService.ownerCaller`, `enginehost.epicService.closerOf`).

**Driving.** Each interpreter pass drives the open amendments of the epics it read before it decides any flow, in epic, then seq, order (`interpret.Runner.Pass`, `interpret.Runner.passAmendments`, `store.Store.OpenAmendments`). For each, it starts the work the amendment's state waits on, under the ids the amendment names, and then settles it in one store write (`interpret.Runner.driveAmendment`, `store.Store.SettleAmendment`). An id is `AmendmentWorkID`: the first 32 hex digits of the lowercase SHA-256 of `orrery-amendment`, the epic, the seq, the part, `red`, `green` or `repro`, and the attempt, joined with NUL bytes (`store.AmendmentWorkID`). A run or check stored under its id is that work, and a settle whose work has not ended writes nothing, so a pass after a restart starts nothing twice, and the evidence runner and the node checks take up the work a restart left open (see **Restart** under [Evidence](#evidence)). Work the evidence runner refuses for good, such as a revision that does not resolve, settles the amendment as work that could not start (`store.Store.StopAmendment`): a checking amendment is refused with `the red run <id> of the new item at the epic's base <base> could not start: <error>`, and a capturing one is applied with `the green run <id> at the stored base <stored base> could not start: <error>` as its outcome.

**The red at the base.** A checking amendment runs the red of its new item at the epic's base, as `Evidence.RunAmendment` runs it (see **Amendment runs** under [Evidence](#evidence)). That red is the guard against weakening the Requirement: the new item replaces the old only once its red is judged not to hold at the base, as a Requirement's red must fail at the base when the epic is created. Once the red run has ended (`store.settleRed`):

- A run stored under the red's id that is not a red run of the amendment's item, for its Requirement, at its base refuses it with `the run <id> is not a red run of the new item at the epic's base <base>`, since only such a run guards the Requirement (`store.amendmentRun`).
- A red that a restart lost, or whose capture exited for its environment, runs again under the next attempt, at most `store.EnvironmentReruns` times (`store.amendmentReruns`, `store.rerunnableRun`; `internal/store/queries.sql: RetryEpicAmendmentRed`).
- A red that passed, which is a red judged `fails` that captured something (`evidence.Passed`), applies the amendment (see **Applying**).
- Any other red refuses it, and the Requirement keeps its item: `the red run <id> of the new item at the epic's base <base> was judged holds, so the new item does not fail at the base: <judge reason>` for a red that holds, and the same run named with `captured nothing: <reason>` or `gave no verdict: <reason>` otherwise (`store.redRefusal`; see **Refusing**).

**Applying.** The write that applies an amendment first counts the epic's node runs that capture its Requirements: the `evidence` and `integrate` runs that are running with a revision, the `sync` runs of origin's main that are running with a merge, whose checks capture as an integrate run's do (see **Sync main** under [Workflows](#workflows)), and the `release` runs that are running, whose release integrator captures too. While there is one, it writes nothing and returns `store.ErrAmendmentDeferred`, and a later pass settles the amendment again (`store.applyAmendment`; `internal/store/queries.sql: CountWorkingEvidenceNodeRuns`). Such a run runs the Requirement's item under the ids its node derives, and a new item would leave those stored runs stale, so the node would wait on them for good. Otherwise, in one write, the Requirement takes the new item, a decision records the amendment, and a planner action that asked for it is marked `taken`, keeping the park its start linked it to (`internal/store/queries.sql: SetEpicRequirementItem`; `store.takeAmendmentAction`, `store.linkedPark`). The decision's id is the action's `action-<turn>-<position>`, or `amend-<seq>` when no action asked for it, and its text is `<amender> amended the evidence of <R> (A<seq>): <reason>. The red run <id> of the new item at the epic's base <base> was judged fails. Prior item: <json>. New item: <json>.` (`store.amendmentDecisionID`, `store.itemsText`). Then:

- When the epic's stored base is still its base, nothing is integrated yet. The amendment is applied, and the next integrate captures the new item at its candidate (see **Batch integration** under [Workflows](#workflows)).
- Otherwise the amendment is `capturing` at the stored base, so the Requirement's green is recomputed there at once, where an epic whose work is all integrated would wait for a batch that never comes (`internal/store/queries.sql: CaptureEpicAmendment`). Its green run of the new item runs at the stored base, and for a test item its reproduction runs as an integrate run's does: the epic's base with the item's test files taken from the stored base, pinned under the repro ref of its check, where the item's capture runs as a check whose path is `amendment A<seq>` (`interpret.Runner.amendmentRepro`, `compose.Overlay`). Once the green run, and the reproduction when there is one, have ended, the amendment is applied. A run stored under the green's id that is not a green run of the amendment's item at the stored base applies it with `the run <id> is not a green run of the new item at the stored base <stored base>` as its outcome (`store.amendmentRun`, `store.applyCaptured`). A run or check that a restart lost, or that exited for its environment, first runs both again under the next attempt, at most `store.EnvironmentReruns` times (`store.settleGreen`, `store.rerunnableCheck`; `internal/store/queries.sql: RetryEpicAmendmentGreen`).

An amendment that is applied adds the pending trigger `amend-<seq>`, whose cause is `evidence of <R> amended by the <amender> (A<seq>): <outcome>`, so the planner plans on the new item. The outcome is `the next integrate captures the new item at its candidate`, or how its green at the stored base ended: `the green run <id> at the stored base <stored base>` and `passed`, `did not pass: verdict <verdict>, exit <n>`, `gave no verdict: <reason>` or `captured nothing: <reason>`, then for a test item `; its reproduction <id> at the epic's base` and `exited <n>`, `timed out` or `did not run: <reason>` (`store.finishAmendment`, `store.greenOutcome`). The owner's amendment also adds a tell of that text, capitalized, which routes to the owner as any tell does (see **Tells** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)). While an amendment is under way, from its action's pending to its end, the plan does not complete, so the plan decides what is built from the item the amendment leaves (see **The plan node** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)).

**Refusing.** A refused amendment keeps its refusal, and the Requirement keeps its item (`internal/store/queries.sql: RefuseEpicAmendment`). A planner action that asked for it is refused with that text, as any refused action is, keeping the park its start linked it to: its decision `action-<turn>-<position>-refused` holds the refusal, the planner's reason and both items, and its trigger wakes the planner, unless its park is bound (see **Actions** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns); `store.refuseLinkedAction`). The owner's refused amendment gets the decision `amend-<seq>-refused`, `owner amendment A<seq> of the evidence of <R> refused: <refusal>. Its reason: <reason>.` with both items, and the tell `Evidence amendment A<seq> of <R> refused: <refusal>` (`store.refuseAmendment`). A close of the epic settles its open amendments as **CloseEpic** under [Epics](#epics) gives (`store.settleClosedAmendments`).

**Green after an amendment.** A run counts only while its kept spec is the item its Requirement holds (see **Current runs** under [Evidence](#evidence)), so an applied amendment leaves each earlier run of the Requirement stale: the red the `evidence` node ran at the base, and each integrate capture. The amendment's own runs are current from then on. The Requirement's `state` in `epic_get` reads the current runs, so it shows them (`enginehost.requirementState`). `kinds.Unbuilt` takes the amendment's red at the base, its green at the stored base and its reproduction as the Requirement's proof, and `kinds.GreenAt` takes its green at the stored base, so a later batch whose capture no longer passes is red (see **Built or park** and **Batch integration** under [Workflows](#workflows)).

**Seen by.** `epic_get` returns every amendment in `amendments` (see **GetEpic and ListEpics** under [Epics](#epics)). The planner prompt lists them under `Evidence amendments`, and the decisions of the applied and refused ones under `Decisions` (see **The step** under [Planner turns](#planner-turns)). The prompt of each epic-stage reviewer, conformance among them, lists them after the Must Not Ship entries when the epic has any, one line each, `A<seq> <R> by <amender>, <state>: <reason>; prior item: <json>; new item: <json>`, with `; refused: <refusal>` after a refused one, so the end-of-epic review sees how each Requirement's evidence changed (`interpret.epicStageReviewPrompt`, `interpret.amendmentBullets`). Each amendment is a row of `epic_amendments` (see [Epics in the store](#epics-in-the-store)).

#### Task builds

An epic's open tasks are built by the build Runner (`internal/build`) while the epic has a base and its phase is not ended (`kinds.Build.Decide`, `kinds.Ended`), which the interpreter drives with `--sessions` and an open store (see [orreryd](#orreryd) and **Running**). A task's build runs its units in order: red at its base, one implementer step whose commit the guard checks, an orchestrator step when the implementer's request needs one (see **Requests**), green at that commit, one review step for each review gate of the epic (its enabled task-stage reviewers, see [Reviewers](#reviewers)), and one verifier step for each finding a review raises, unless the reviewer has verify off. The next action is decided from the task's stored build, its step or evidence run, and its epic (`build.StageRed`, `build.StageImplement`, `build.StageOrchestrate`, `build.StageGreen`, `build.StageReview`, `build.StageVerify`; `build.Decide`). A write to a task's build is compare-and-set: it lands only while the task is `open` and its stored build is the one the pass read (`store.Store.SetTaskBuild`; `store.moveTask`).

**Passes.** The interpreter gives the build the task seqs of its build child runs that run, through `PassTasks`, once a decision has no structural action (`interpret.Runner.deliver`; `build.Runner.PassTasks`). `PassTasks` ensures the epic's branch, then carries out the actions `Decide` gives each of those open tasks, one at a time. It reads the epic again after each action that changed something, up to eight actions for a task (`build.maxActions`, `build.Runner.passEpic`, `build.Runner.passTask`). An action that changes nothing ends the task's pass for that interpreter pass. A failed read of a task's view is logged, and so is a failed write; the next pass tries the same action again (`build.Runner.passTask`; `build.Runner.report`, `build.Runner.written`). `Run`, the build's own loop, passes at once and then once per tick, five seconds by default (`build.Runner.Run`; `build.defaultTick`), and `orreryd` does not start it.

**Children.** A task's build is a child run of the plan node run that planned it (see **Running**). `CompleteTurn` writes a running child in the turn's write for each task it stores, under the first build node of the epic's running plan run, with the path `<plan path>/<build id>/T<seq>` and the cause `turn <turn> task <position>` (`store.Store.CompleteTurn`, `store.planFanOutOf`, `store.insertTasks`). The pass also starts a child for each open task whose turn is above its plan run's mark and that has none, so a task planned before the plan node ran gets one (`interpret.pass.startTasks`). A child runs while its task is open on an epic that has a base and is accepted, waits while the epic lacks either, and ends once its task is no longer open (`kinds.Build.Decide`).

**Branch.** The epic's branch `epic/<slug>` is created once, at the epic's base, with `update-ref` and an empty old value, so an existing branch is never moved (`build.CreateBranch`; `build.BranchRef`; `build.Runner.create`). Its state, `''`, `creating` or `created`, moves from `creating` to `created` around the create (`build.Runner.create`, `build.Runner.markCreated`; `store.Store.SetEpicBranch`). `CreateBranch` creates the branch. Afterwards it moves only in an integrate batch's advance, from the stored base to the batch's candidate, and in a sync's advance, from the stored base to its merge of origin's main, which are the only writes orreryd makes to it after the create (see **Batch integration** and **Sync main** under [Workflows](#workflows); `build.Runner.create`, `interpret.Runner.advance`, `interpret.Runner.advanceSync`). A paused or parked epic with no created branch creates nothing (`build.Runner.branchTip`). When the state is `''` or `creating` and the branch exists, the branch is adopted only if the state is `creating`, the branch still names the base, and no worktree has it checked out. Otherwise the epic parks with `epic/<slug> exists` (`build.Runner.branchTip`, `build.Runner.adopt`). A create that fails parks the epic with `epic/<slug> exists: <error>` (`build.Runner.create`). A `created` epic whose branch is gone parks with `epic/<slug> is gone`, and a parked epic is not parked again, so the owner is told once (`build.Runner.createdTip`).

**Starts.** A task with no stage starts at red once the epic is neither paused nor parked and its branch has a tip. Its `base` is the epic's candidate when the epic has one, so a red batch's fixes start from it, and the branch tip otherwise (`build.decideFresh`, `build.Runner.view`; see **Batch integration** under [Workflows](#workflows)). A paused or parked epic starts nothing new. The implementer's step runs the task's command gate, each review step runs its reviewer's agent gate, and a verifier step names no gate. The red and green runs are evidence runs (`build.decideRed`, `build.decideImplement`; `build.decideGreen`, `build.decideReview`, `build.decideVerify`). Accepting, failing, pinning, checking ref moves and storing a finding are not starts, so they run while the epic is paused or parked (`build.decideReview`, `build.settledFindings`; `build.decideImplement`). A pause does not stop a running step. The step runs to its end, and the build acts on that end (`store.Store.PauseEpic`).

**Units.**

- Red (`build.decideRed`). The unit's evidence run is `RunID(epic, seq, red, 0, rerun)` at the base, started when the unit has no run (`build.decideRed`; `build.RunID`). A run that is not captured fails the task with `red not captured: <reason>`. A run with no verdict is a no-verdict. A run whose conclusion holds at the base fails the task with `red: the conclusion holds at the base`. A run that passes moves the build to implement at attempt 1 and stores the run as `red_run` (`build.decideRed`).
- Implement (`build.decideImplement`). The implementer step is `StepID(epic, seq, implement, attempt, rerun, 0, 0)`. Before it starts, the refs are snapshotted (see **Ref moves**). The step is a writing step in the task's implement worktree (see **Worktrees**), checked from the base.
